      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0273">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0273 ESTABLISHING BOUNDARY LINES RELATING TO CERTAIN STATE LANDHOLDINGS.</h1>
        <p>(a) To resolve boundary line issues affecting the ownership interests of the state and adjacent landowners, the commissioner of natural resources may, in the name of the state upon terms the commissioner deems appropriate, convey, by a boundary line agreement, quitclaim deed, or management agreement in such form as the attorney general approves, such rights, titles, and interests of the state in state lands for such rights, titles, and interests in adjacent lands as are necessary to establish boundaries. The commissioner must publish a notice of the proposed conveyance and a brief statement of the reason for the conveyance once in the State Register at least 30 days before the conveyance. This paragraph is not intended to replace or supersede laws relating to land exchange or disposal of surplus state property.</p>
        <p>(b) To resolve trespass issues affecting the ownership interests of the state and adjacent landowners, the commissioner of natural resources, in the name of the state, may sell surplus lands not needed for natural resource purposes at private sale to adjoining property owners and leaseholders. The conveyance must be by quitclaim in a form approved by the attorney general for a consideration not less than the value determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.10#stat.94.10.1">94.10, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) Paragraph (b) applies to all state-owned lands managed by the commissioner of natural resources, except school trust land as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/92.025">92.025</a>. For acquired lands, the commissioner may sell the surplus lands as provided in paragraph (b) notwithstanding the offering to public entities, public sale, and related notice and publication requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.09">94.09</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.165">94.165</a>. For consolidated conservation lands, the commissioner may sell the surplus lands as provided in paragraph (b) notwithstanding the classification and public sale provisions of chapters 84A and 282.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0273.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/458/#laws.0.9.0">1980 c 458 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/285/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 285 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.59.0">1997 c 216 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.3.1.0">2009 c 176 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 4 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0274">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0274 LANDOWNERS' BILL OF RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as "the landowners' bill of rights."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the intent of this section to clarify the responsibilities of the state in the natural resources land acquisition process and to provide additional protections to landowners in their dealings with the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Condemnation limits.</span></h2>
          <p>No lands shall be acquired by the commissioner of natural resources by means of condemnation unless the owner requests that the owner's lands be condemned or the condemnation is specifically authorized by law. Notwithstanding subdivision 5, paragraph (g), and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/117.52">117.52</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/117.521">117.521</a>, the owner shall not be paid relocation costs when the owner requests that the owner's lands be condemned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rights cumulative.</span></h2>
          <p>The protections for landowners and responsibilities of the state set forth in this section shall not limit, but shall be in addition to all rights and responsibilities contained in state or federal law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Owner's rights.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When the state proposes to purchase in fee or any lesser interest in land which will be administered by the commissioner of natural resources, the landowner shall have the following rights:</p>
          <p class="in">(a) the right to be informed of the specific intended use of the property and of any change in the intended use of the property which occurs during the acquisition process. The owner shall also be informed that the documents regarding the purchase will be public records if the land is purchased by the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(b) the right to be paid a fair price for the property. The price shall include the fair market value of the land plus:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) all necessary incidental costs such as abstracting and recording fees related to the sale. The costs of clearing title defects, paying taxes, and attorney's fees are not reimbursable; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any penalties incurred by the owner where the property is security for a loan or advance of credit that contains a provision requiring or permitting the imposition of a penalty if the loan or advance of credit is prepaid;</p>
          <p class="in">(c) the right to payment, at the owner's election, in a lump sum or in up to four annual installments;</p>
          <p class="in">(d) the right to have the property fairly appraised by the state. The state's appraiser shall physically inspect the property and the owner shall be allowed to accompany the appraiser when the appraisal is made. The state's appraiser shall certify in the appraisal report to having physically inspected the property and having given the landowner an opportunity to accompany the appraiser on inspections. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.44#stat.13.44.3">13.44, subdivision 3</a>, before an offer is made, the landowner shall be informed of the value determined pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0272">84.0272</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(e) the right to retain a qualified independent appraiser to conduct an appraisal at any time prior to certification of the state's appraisal of the property and to have that appraisal considered along with the state's in certifying the selling price and the right to be reimbursed for appraisal fees up to $1,500 if the land is sold to the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(f) the right to have the state acquire the property by means of condemnation upon the owner's request with the agreement of the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(g) when the property is being acquired by condemnation or the condemnation is specifically authorized by law, the right to receive or waive relocation assistance, services, payments and benefits as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/117.52">117.52</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/117.521">117.521</a> and to contest the state's offer for relocation and moving expenses;</p>
          <p class="in">(h) the right to accept the state's offer for the property;</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the right to continue occupancy of the property until full payment is received, provided that when the owner elects to receive payment in annual installments pursuant to clause (c), the owner may retain occupancy until the first payment is made; and</p>
          <p class="in">(j) the right to seek the advice of counsel regarding any aspect of the land transaction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">State's responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>When the state proposes to purchase land for natural resources purposes, the commissioner of natural resources and, where applicable, the commissioner of administration shall have the following responsibilities:</p>
          <p>(1) the responsibility to deal fairly and openly with the landowner in the purchase of property;</p>
          <p>(2) the responsibility to refrain from discussing price with the landowner before an appraisal has been made. In addition, the same person shall not both appraise and negotiate for purchase of a tract of land. This paragraph does not apply to the state when discussing with a landowner the trout stream easement payment determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0272#stat.84.0272.2">84.0272, subdivision 2</a>, the native prairie bank easement payment determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.96#stat.84.96.5">84.96, subdivision 5</a>, or the Camp Ripley's Army compatible use buffer easement payment determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0277#stat.84.0277.2">84.0277, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) the responsibility to use private fee appraisers to lower the state's acquisition costs to the greatest extent practicable; and</p>
          <p>(4) the responsibility to acquire land in as expeditious a manner as possible. No option shall be made for a period of greater than two months if no survey is required or for nine months if a survey is required, unless the landowner, in writing, expressly requests a longer period of time. Provided that, if county board approval of the transaction is required pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145">97A.145</a>, no time limits shall apply. If the state elects not to purchase property upon which it has an option, it shall pay the landowner $500 after the expiration of the option period. If the state elects to purchase the property, unless the landowner elects otherwise, payment to the landowner shall be made no later than 90 days following the state's election to purchase the property provided that the title is marketable and the owner acts expeditiously to complete the transaction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Disclosure.</span></h2>
          <p>When the state proposes to purchase lands for natural resources purposes, the landowner shall be given a written statement in lay terms of the rights and responsibilities provided for in subdivisions 5 and 6. Before a purchase can be made, the landowner must sign a statement acknowledging in writing that the statement has been provided and explained to the landowner. Within 60 days following the date of final approval of Laws 1980, chapter 45B, the commissioner of natural resources shall submit a proposed form for the statement to the Legislative-Citizen Commission on Minnesota Resources. The commission shall review the proposed form for compliance with the intent of this section and shall make any changes which it deems proper.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Exception for railroad right-of-way acquisitions.</span></h2>
          <p>When the commissioner of natural resources acquires abandoned railroad right-of-way from a railroad, railroad holding company, or similar entity, any or all of the provisions of this section may be waived by mutual agreement of the commissioner and the landowner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Exception for nonprofit organizations and governmental entities.</span></h2>
          <p>When the commissioner acquires land or interests in land from a nonprofit organization or governmental entity, any or all of the provisions of this section may be waived by mutual agreement of the commissioner and the nonprofit organization or governmental entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0274.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Right of first refusal agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enter into a right of first refusal agreement with a landowner prior to determining the value of the land. No right of first refusal agreement shall be made for a period of greater than two years and payment to the landowner for entry into the agreement shall not exceed $5,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0274.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/458/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 458 s 1</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.5.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/690/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 690 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.68.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 68</a>,269; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.15.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/243/#laws.0.21.0">2006 c 243 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/129/#laws.0.47.0">2007 c 129 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.2.2.0">2007 c 131 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/73/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 73 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.5.2.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 5 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0275">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0275 VIOLATIONS.</h1>
        <p>If the state acquires any land for natural resources purposes in violation of any of the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0274">84.0274</a>, subdivisions 5 to 7, the landowner may maintain an action against the commissioner of natural resources for any damages suffered. However, no title to land purchased for natural resources purposes will be invalid as a result of such violations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/458/#laws.0.8.0">1980 c 458 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0276">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0276 LAND TRANSFERS BY A FEDERAL AGENCY.</h1>
        <p>Before the commissioner of natural resources accepts agricultural land or a farm homestead transferred in fee by a federal agency, the commissioner must consult with the Board of Water and Soil Resources for a determination of marginal land, tillable farmland, and farm homestead. The commissioner must comply with the acquisition procedure under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145#stat.97A.145.2">97A.145, subdivision 2</a>, if the agricultural land or farm homestead was in an agricultural preserve as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/40A.10">40A.10</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0276.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/350/#laws.6.1.0">1989 c 350 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/353/#laws.0.7.0">1989 c 353 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0277">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0277 CAMP RIPLEY BUFFER EASEMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0277.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may acquire, from willing sellers, perpetual conservation easements on behalf of the state and federal government consistent with Camp Ripley's Army compatible use buffer project. This project is geographically defined as a three-mile zone around Camp Ripley in central Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0277.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payments; terms.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0272#stat.84.0272.1">84.0272, subdivision 1</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0274#stat.84.0274.5">84.0274, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), the commissioner may make payments to a landowner under this subdivision to acquire a perpetual conservation easement according to subdivision 1. The onetime payment may be based on the following:</p>
          <p>(1) if the easement prohibits the construction of any new buildings or permanent structures upon the land, the commissioner may pay 60 percent of the most recent assessed market value of the land as determined by the county assessor of the county in which the land is located; or</p>
          <p>(2) if the easement prohibits the construction of any new buildings or permanent structures upon the land and grants the public the right to access the land for natural resource-based outdoor recreation, the commissioner may pay 70 percent of the most recent assessed market value of the land as determined by the county assessor of the county in which the land is located.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0277.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.3.2.0">2009 c 176 art 3 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.028">
        <h1 class="shn">84.028 COMMISSIONER OF NATURAL RESOURCES; SPECIFIC ASSIGNMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.028.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner to control department responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The powers, duties and responsibilities of the Department of Natural Resources relating to boat safety, firearm safety, wild rice harvest program, ginseng harvest program, and such other programs as are now or hereafter vested by statute in the Department of Natural Resources, shall be under the control and supervision of the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.028.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Development and planning duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The overall coordination of acquisition and development programs, comprehensive planning activities, including statewide recreational planning programs required by state or federal law, and not the responsibility of the commissioner of employment and economic development, are under the control and supervision of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.028.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Game warden duties; conservation officers.</span></h2>
          <p>The operation of the Game Warden Service in the Division of Game and Fish as constituted before July 1, 1967, is under the direct control and supervision of the commissioner. The name of the personnel in such Game Warden Service is changed to conservation officers. Conservation officers shall continue to have the powers and duties of game wardens as they existed before July 1, 1967, and may be assigned to public relations, conservation instructional activities, and the enforcement of laws relating to resources management which the commissioner shall direct. The commissioner shall create a separate division entitled the Division of Enforcement, to be composed of conservation officers and shall appoint a director of the division. The commissioner may place the director's position in the unclassified service if the position meets the criteria established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.08#stat.43A.08.1a">43A.08, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.028.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.3.0">1967 c 905 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.3.5.0">1969 c 1129 art 3 s 5</a>; art 10 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/94/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 94 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/356/#laws.0.100.0">1981 c 356 s 100</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/560/#laws.0.41.0">1982 c 560 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/289/#laws.0.115.0">1983 c 289 s 115</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.3.0">2014 c 248 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0285">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0285 GAME AND FISH CITATION QUOTAS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources, or the director of the Division of Enforcement, may not order, mandate, require, or in any manner suggest, directly or indirectly, to a conservation officer that the conservation officer issue a certain number of game and fish law violations on a daily, weekly, monthly, quarterly, or yearly quota basis.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0285.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.6.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/474/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 474 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/32/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 32 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.17.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0286">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0286 CONSERVATION OFFICER PATROL VEHICLE; SECURITY BARRIER; EXEMPTION.</h1>
        <p>Marked conservation officer patrol vehicles are exempt from any law or rule requiring a security barrier in the vehicle.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0286.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/260/#laws.0.1.0">2004 c 260 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.029">
        <h1 class="shn">84.029 RECREATIONAL AREAS ON PUBLIC LAND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.029.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment, development, maintenance, and operation.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to other lawful authority, the commissioner of natural resources may establish, develop, maintain, and operate recreational areas, including but not limited to trails and state water trails, for the use and enjoyment of the public on any state-owned or leased land under the commissioner's jurisdiction. The commissioner may employ and designate individuals according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a> to enforce laws governing the use of recreational areas. The commissioner may establish the recreational areas by written order published in the State Register.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.029.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acquiring land for trails.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may acquire, by gift, purchase, or lease, easements or other interests in land for trails, and recreational uses related to trails, where necessary to complete trails established primarily in state forests, state parks, or other public land under the jurisdiction of the commissioner, when railroad rights-of-way are abandoned, when the use of township roads is compatible with vehicular travel, or when needed to complete trails established by the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.029.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Authority exercised by the commissioner according to this section is exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.029.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/190/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 190 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/713/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 713 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/353/#laws.0.13.0">1975 c 353 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.13.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.3.0">2004 c 221 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/260/#laws.0.2.0">2004 c 260 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.2.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.82.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.03">
        <h1 class="shn">84.03 ADDITIONAL DUTIES AND POWERS.</h1>
        <p>(a) So far as practicable the commissioner shall collect and arrange statistics and other information in reference to the lands and general and special resources of the state.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner is hereby authorized and empowered to take such measures as the commissioner may deem advisable to advertise, both within and without the state, sales of all state lands, and to secure, compile, and issue such valuable statistics of the resources of the state.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner may adopt and promulgate reasonable rules, not inconsistent with law, governing the use and enjoyment of state land reserved from sale, state parks, state water-access sites, state trails, state monuments, state scientific and natural areas, state wilderness areas, and recreational areas owned by other state, local and federal agencies and operated under agreement by the Department of Natural Resources, which shall have the force and effect of law. A reasonable fee may be fixed, charged, and collected by the commissioner for the privilege of the use of any or all of the foregoing privileges and facilities.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner, on or before November 15 of each even-numbered year, shall report to the legislature the commissioner's acts and doings, with recommendation for the improvement or conservation of state parks, state water-access sites, state trails, and state monuments, state scientific and natural areas, state forests, state wildlife management areas, public hunting grounds, public shooting grounds, food and cover planting areas, wildlife lands, recreational or public hunting areas, state wild and scenic rivers, state wilderness areas, and all other recreational lands under the jurisdiction of the Department of Natural Resources, and for desirable accessions thereto, such report to include an inventory of the tracts and parcels of land, and rights, interests, and easements therein, held by the state or withdrawn from sale for any of these purposes, with the value thereof, and a list of the name, location, size, and description of each state trail, state scientific and natural area, state wildlife management area, state water-access site, and state wild, scenic, or recreational river designated by the commissioner, and each public hunting grounds, public shooting grounds, food and cover planting area, wildlife lands, and recreational or public hunting area acquired by the commissioner since the last report. The commissioner shall maintain a long range plan governing the use of the public domain under the commissioner's jurisdiction.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/4">77</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/23">4342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/40">6460</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/40">6466</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1905/0/201/#laws.0.1.0">1905 c 201 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/267/#laws.0.5.0">1907 c 267 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/430/#laws.0.8.0">1923 c 430 s 8</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/222/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 222 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.4.0">1967 c 905 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/470/#laws.0.2.0">1969 c 470 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/406/#laws.0.59.0">1974 c 406 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/353/#laws.0.14.0">1975 c 353 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.031"><b>84.031</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.4.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.032"><b>84.032</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.4.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.033">
        <h1 class="shn">84.033 SCIENTIFIC AND NATURAL AREAS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.033.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition; designation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of natural resources may acquire by gift, lease, easement, exchange, or purchase, in the manner prescribed under chapter 117, in the name of the state, lands or any interest in lands suitable and desirable for establishing and maintaining scientific and natural areas. The commissioner shall designate any land so acquired as a scientific and natural area by written order published in the State Register and shall administer any land so acquired and designated as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05">86A.05</a>. Designations of scientific and natural areas are exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.033.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/161/#laws.0.26.0">2005 c 161 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.033.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">County approval.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must follow the procedures under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145#stat.97A.145.2">97A.145, subdivision 2</a>, when acquiring land for designation as a scientific and natural area under this section located outside the seven-county metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.033.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/470/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 470 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/35/#laws.0.22.0">1973 c 35 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/353/#laws.0.15.0">1975 c 353 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.5.0">2004 c 221 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/262/#laws.2.3.0">2004 c 262 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/161/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 161 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.4.0">2011 c 107 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.5.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.034">
        <h1 class="shn">84.034 MAINTAINING CEMETERY; WHITEWATER WILDLIFE MANAGEMENT AREA.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall maintain in a proper and decent manner and keep free of weeds any cemetery in the Whitewater State Wildlife Management Area.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.034.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.7.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.035">
        <h1 class="shn">84.035 PEATLAND PROTECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.035">84.035</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a> may be cited as the "Minnesota Peatland Protection Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Findings.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that certain Minnesota peatlands possess unique scientific, aesthetic, vegetative, hydrologic, geologic, wildlife, wilderness, and educational values and represent the various peatland ecological types in the state. The legislature finds that it is desirable and appropriate to protect and preserve these patterned peatlands as a peatland management system through establishment and designation of certain peatland core areas as scientific and natural areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended, the following terms, for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.035">84.035</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a>, have the meanings given to them.</p>
          <p>(a) "Winter road" means an access route which may be used by vehicles only when the substrate is frozen, except as provided in subdivision 5, paragraph (b), clause (3).</p>
          <p>(b) "Corridors of disturbance" means rights-of-way which are in existence on the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, such as ditches, ditch banks, transmission lines, pipelines, permanent roads, winter roads, and recreational trails. The existence, on the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, of a corridor of disturbance may be demonstrated by physical evidence, document recorded in the office of county recorder or other public official, aerial survey, or other evidence similar to the above.</p>
          <p>(c) "State land" means land owned by the state of Minnesota and administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Designating peatland scientific and natural areas.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the peatland areas described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a>, state lands are hereby established and designated as scientific and natural areas to be preserved and managed by the commissioner in accordance with subdivision 5 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.5">86A.05, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Activities in peatland scientific and natural areas.</span></h2>
          <p>Areas designated in subdivision 4 as peatland scientific and natural areas are subject to the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), all restrictions otherwise applicable to scientific and natural areas designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.5">86A.05, subdivision 5</a>, apply to the surface use and to any use of the mineral estate which would significantly modify or alter the peatland water levels or flows, peatland water chemistry, plant or animal species or communities, or other natural features of the peatland scientific and natural areas, including, but not limited to, the following prohibitions:</p>
          <p>(1) construction of any new public drainage systems after the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, or improvement or repair to a public drainage system in existence on the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, under authority of chapter 103E, or any other alteration of surface water or groundwater levels or flows unless specifically permitted under paragraph (b), clause (5) or (6);</p>
          <p>(2) removal of peat, sand, gravel, or other industrial minerals;</p>
          <p>(3) exploratory boring or other exploration or removal of oil, natural gas, radioactive materials or metallic minerals which would significantly modify or alter the peatland water levels or flows, peatland water chemistry, plant or animal species or communities, or natural features of the peatland scientific and natural areas, except in the event of a national emergency declared by Congress;</p>
          <p>(4) commercial timber harvesting;</p>
          <p>(5) construction of new corridors of disturbance, of the kind defined in subdivision 3, after June 5, 1991; and</p>
          <p>(6) ditching, draining, filling, or any other activities which modify or alter the peatland water levels or flows, peatland water chemistry, plant or animal species or communities, or other natural features of the peatland scientific and natural areas.</p>
          <p>(b) The following activities are allowed:</p>
          <p>(1) recreational activities, including hunting, fishing, trapping, cross-country skiing, snowshoeing, nature observation, or other recreational activities permitted in the management plan approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) scientific and educational work and research;</p>
          <p>(3) maintenance of corridors of disturbance, including survey lines and preparation of winter roads, consistent with protection of the peatland ecosystem;</p>
          <p>(4) use of corridors of disturbance unless limited by a management plan adopted by the commissioner under subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(5) improvements to a public drainage system in existence on the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, only when it is for the protection and maintenance of the ecological integrity of the peatland scientific and natural area and when included in a management plan adopted by the commissioner under subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(6) repairs to a public drainage system in existence on the effective date of Laws 1991, chapter 354, which crosses a peatland scientific and natural area and is used for the purposes of providing a drainage outlet for lands outside of the peatland scientific and natural area, provided that there are no other feasible and prudent alternative means of providing the drainage outlet. The commissioner shall cooperate with the ditch authority in the determination of any feasible and prudent alternatives. No repairs which would significantly modify or alter the peatland water levels or flows, peatland water chemistry, plant or animal species or communities, or other natural features of the peatland scientific and natural areas shall be made unless approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(7) motorized uses on a corridor of disturbance, if the corridor existed on or before January 1, 1992, provided that recreational motorized uses may occur only when the substrate is frozen, or the corridor is snow packed, subject to a management plan developed in accordance with subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(8) control of forest insects, disease, and wildfires, as described in a management plan adopted by the commissioner under subdivision 6; and</p>
          <p>(9) geological and geophysical surveys which would not significantly modify or alter the peatland water levels or flows, peatland water chemistry, plant or animal species or communities, or other natural features of the peatland scientific and natural areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Management plans.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall develop in consultation with the affected local government unit a management plan for each peatland scientific and natural area designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The management plan shall address recreational trails. In those peatland scientific and natural areas where no corridor of disturbance was used as a recreational trail on or before January 1, 1992, the plan may permit only one corridor of disturbance, in each peatland scientific and natural area, to be used as a recreational motorized trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Establishing baseline ecological data.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish baseline data on the ecology and biological diversity of peatland scientific and natural areas and provide for ongoing, long-term ecological monitoring to determine whether changes are occurring in the peatland scientific and natural areas. This research is intended to identify any changes occurring in peatland scientific and natural areas as a result of any permitted activities outside the peatland scientific and natural areas. This baseline data may include, but is not limited to, the history of the peatlands and their geologic origins, plant and animal communities, hydrology, water chemistry, and contaminants introduced from remote sources of atmospheric deposition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Ditch abandonments.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to eliminate repairs or improvements to any public drainage system that crosses a peatland scientific and natural area in those instances where the repair or improvement adversely affects an area, the commissioner may petition for the abandonment of parts of the public drainage system under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103E.811">103E.811</a>. If the public drainage system is necessary as a drainage outlet for lands outside of the peatland scientific and natural area, the commissioner will cooperate with the ditch authority in the development of feasible and prudent alternative means of providing a drainage outlet which avoids the crossing of and damage to the peatland scientific and natural area. In so doing, the commissioner shall grant flowage easements to the ditch authority for disposal of the outlet water on other state lands. The ditch authority shall approve the abandonment of parts of any public drainage system crossing a peatland scientific and natural area if the public drainage system crossing of those areas is not necessary as a drainage outlet for lands outside of the areas or if there are feasible and prudent alternative means of providing a drainage outlet without crossing such areas. In any abandonment under this subdivision the commissioner may enter into an agreement with the ditch authority regarding apportionment of costs and, contingent upon appropriations of money for that purpose, may agree to pay a reasonable share of the cost of abandonment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Compensation for trust fund lands.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall acquire by exchange or eminent domain the surface interests, including peat, on trust fund lands contained in peatland scientific and natural areas established in subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.035.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Acquisition of peatland scientific and natural areas.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may acquire by purchase the surface interests, including peat, of lands within the boundaries of the peatland areas described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.036">84.036</a>, that are owned, or that hereafter become owned, by the state and administered by the local county board.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall designate any land acquired under this subdivision as peatland scientific and natural area and preserve and administer any land so acquired and designated in accordance with subdivision 5 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05">86A.05</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.035.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.8.1.0">1991 c 354 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/464/#laws.2.1.0">1992 c 464 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 462 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/2/#laws.0.7.0">1997 c 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.5.0">2011 c 107 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.036">
        <h1 class="shn">84.036 PEATLAND SCIENTIFIC AND NATURAL AREAS; DESIGNATION.</h1>
        <p>The following scientific and natural areas are established and are composed of all of the core peatland areas identified on maps in the 1984 commissioner of natural resources report, "Recommendations for the Protection of Ecologically Significant Peatlands in Minnesota" and maps on file at the Department of Natural Resources:</p>
        <p>(1) Red Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Beltrami, Koochiching, and Lake of the Woods Counties;</p>
        <p>(2) Myrtle Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(3) Lost River Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(4) North Black River Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(5) Sand Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Lake County;</p>
        <p>(6) Mulligan Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Lake of the Woods County;</p>
        <p>(7) Lost Lake Scientific and Natural Area in St. Louis County;</p>
        <p>(8) Pine Creek Scientific and Natural Area in Roseau County;</p>
        <p>(9) Hole in the Bog Scientific and Natural Area in Cass County;</p>
        <p>(10) Wawina Scientific and Natural Area in St. Louis County;</p>
        <p>(11) Nett Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(12) East Rat Root River Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(13) South Black River Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(14) Winter Road Lake Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County;</p>
        <p>(15) Sprague Creek Scientific and Natural Area in Roseau County;</p>
        <p>(16) Luxemberg Scientific and Natural Area in Roseau County;</p>
        <p>(17) West Rat Root River Scientific and Natural Area in Koochiching County; and</p>
        <p>(18) Norris Camp Scientific and Natural Area in Lake of the Woods County.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.036.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.8.2.0">1991 c 354 art 8 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.081">
        <h1 class="shn">84.081 DEPARTMENT DIVISIONS AND BUREAUS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.081.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Directors.</span></h2>
          <p> The Department of Natural Resources shall be organized into divisions subject to the commissioner's authority to revise or abolish existing divisions and to establish new divisions as prescribed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.083#stat.84.083.1">84.083, subdivision 1</a>. Each division shall be under the immediate charge of a director, subject to the supervision and control of the commissioner. The commissioner may place a director's position in the unclassified service if the position meets the criteria established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.08#stat.43A.08.1a">43A.08, subdivision 1a</a>. They shall be chosen with regard to knowledge, training, experience, ability in administering the work of their respective divisions, and with consideration given to applicable professional registration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.081.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/326/#laws.0.27.0">1991 c 326 s 27</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.081.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Directors may employ assistants.</span></h2>
          <p>Each director, with the approval of the commissioner, may employ such assistants as may be necessary for the work of the director's division. Each director, with the approval of the commissioner, may designate one employee as deputy director, and may revoke such designation at any time, regardless of the civil service status of such employee and without affecting such status. Each deputy director may exercise all of the powers of the director, subject to the director's direction and control, including powers delegated by the commissioner unless otherwise prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.081.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.3.0">1943 c 60 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/601/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 601 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/587/#laws.0.17.0">1947 c 587 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/356/#laws.0.1.0">1949 c 356 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/739/#laws.0.7.0">1949 c 739 s 7</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/713/#laws.0.11.0">1951 c 713 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.5.0">1967 c 905 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.3.6.0">1969 c 1129 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/615/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 615 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/560/#laws.0.42.0">1982 c 560 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/306/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 306 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.4.0">2014 c 248 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.082">
        <h1 class="shn">84.082 VACANCIES.</h1>
        <p>In case of a vacancy in the office of commissioner or of any director, the respective deputy shall have all of the powers and perform all of the duties thereof until a successor, either as an acting or regular incumbent, has been appointed and has qualified; provided, no deputy commissioner serving as commissioner in the event of a vacancy shall have power to discharge a director or to revise or change the assignments of activities among the divisions of the department or to designate another deputy. While serving in such vacated office a deputy shall receive the same salary as the regular incumbent.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.082.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.4.0">1943 c 60 s 4</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.083">
        <h1 class="shn">84.083 ASSIGNMENT AND DELEGATION OF DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.083.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Division duties; delegation.</span></h2>
          <p>Each division shall have charge of administering the activities indicated by its title and such other duties and functions as may be assigned by the commissioner, subject to the right of the commissioner to revise and change assignments of any and all activities or of specific duties or functions at any time as the commissioner may see fit, including but not limited to the right to abolish or revise existing divisions or to establish new divisions. The commissioner may, by written order filed in the Office of the Secretary of State, delegate to the directors or other designated employees, any of the powers or duties vested in or imposed upon the commissioner by Laws 1943, chapter 60, or by any other law upon such conditions as the commissioner may prescribe and subject to modification or revocation at the commissioner's pleasure. Such delegated powers and duties may be exercised or performed by the respective directors or other employees in their own names or in the name of the commissioner, as the commissioner may direct.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.083.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/310/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 310 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.083.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.083.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.083.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.5.0">1943 c 60 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/356/#laws.0.2.0">1949 c 356 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.5.0">1967 c 905 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/615/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 615 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.10.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 10</a>,11</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0835">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0835 DESIGNATION OF EMPLOYEES FOR LIMITED NATURAL RESOURCES LAW ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0835.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's authority to designate employees.</span></h2>
          <p>As provided in this section, the commissioner may designate by written order certain employees to enforce laws governing the use of state parks, state monuments, state recreation areas, state waysides, forest subareas, forest lands under the authority of the commissioner when incidental to trail management or normal forestry duties, and game preserves and other lands administered as wildlife management areas. The designation by the commissioner is not subject to rulemaking under chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0835.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Designated employee authorities; generally.</span></h2>
          <p>An employee designated under subdivision 1:</p>
          <p>(1) has citizen arrest powers according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/629.37">629.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/629.39">629.39</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) may issue citations, on a form prescribed by the commissioner, in lieu of arrest for petty misdemeanor violations and misdemeanor violations, unless the violation occurs in the presence of a conservation officer or other peace officer, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.84#stat.626.84.1">626.84, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c); and</p>
          <p>(3) may issue a report of violation to be turned over to a conservation officer or other peace officer for possible charges at the peace officer's discretion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0835.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Citation authority.</span></h2>
          <p>Employees designated by the commissioner under subdivision 1 may issue citations, as specifically authorized under this subdivision, for violations of:</p>
          <p>(1) sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.052#stat.85.052.3">85.052, subdivision 3</a> (payment of camping fees in state parks), <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.45#stat.85.45.1">85.45, subdivision 1</a> (cross-country-ski pass), <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.46">85.46</a> (horse pass), and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9275">84.9275 </a>(nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail pass);</p>
          <p>(2) rules relating to hours and days of operation, restricted areas, noise, fireworks, environmental protection, fires and refuse, pets, picnicking, camping and dispersed camping, nonmotorized uses, construction of unauthorized permanent trails, mooring of boats, fish cleaning, swimming, storage and abandonment of personal property, structures and stands, animal trespass, state park individual and group motor vehicle permits, licensed motor vehicles, designated roads, and snowmobile operation off trails;</p>
          <p>(3) rules relating to off-highway vehicle registration, display of registration numbers, required equipment, operation restrictions, off-trail use for hunting and trapping, and operation in lakes, rivers, and streams;</p>
          <p>(4) rules relating to off-highway vehicle and snowmobile operation causing damage or in closed areas within the Richard J. Dorer Memorial Hardwood State Forest;</p>
          <p>(5) rules relating to parking, snow removal, and damage on state forest roads; and</p>
          <p>(6) rules relating to controlled hunting zones on major wildlife management units.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0835.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/260/#laws.0.3.0">2004 c 260 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.9.6.0">2006 c 282 art 9 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.10.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.82.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.084">
        <h1 class="shn">84.084 TRANSFER OF FUNDS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may authorize the performance of services for any division by any other division or by the department staff, and, with the approval of the commissioner of administration, may require appropriate transfers of funds to compensate for the cost of such service.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.084.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.6.0">1943 c 60 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0845">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0845 ADVANCE OF MATCHING FUNDS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may advance funds appropriated for fish and wildlife programs to government agencies, the National Fish and Wildlife Foundation, federally recognized Indian tribes and bands, and private, nonprofit organizations for the purposes of securing nonstate matching funds for projects involving acquisition and improvement of fish and wildlife habitat and related research and management. The commissioner shall execute agreements for contracts with the matching parties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.03#stat.16C.03.4">16C.03, subdivision 4</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05">16C.05</a> prior to advancing any state funds. The agreement or contract shall contain provisions for return of the state's share and the matching funds within a period of time specified by the commissioner. The state's funds and the nonstate matching funds must be deposited in a separate account and expended solely for the purposes set forth in the agreement or contract. The commissioner shall enter into agreements or contracts only with the National Fish and Wildlife Foundation and federal and nonprofit authorities deemed by the commissioner to be dedicated to the purposes of the project.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0845.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/269/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 269 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.26.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0846">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0846 NATURAL RESOURCE SEMINARS AND CONFERENCES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources may advance funds appropriated for natural resource programs to government agencies, the National Fish and Wildlife Foundation, federally recognized Indian tribes and bands, colleges and universities, and nonprofit organizations deemed by the commissioner to be dedicated to the goals and objectives of the department for the purpose of sponsoring or cosponsoring conferences and seminars related to natural resources issues and management. The commissioner shall execute grants or contracts with the responsible parties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.05">16C.05</a> prior to advancing any state funds and the agreements must provide for a full accounting of how the state's funds will be spent.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0846.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 495 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.085"><b>84.085</b> MS 1969 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/3/#laws.0.77.0">Ex1971 c 3 s 77</a> subd 3]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.085">
        <h1 class="shn">84.085 ACCEPTANCE OF GIFTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.085.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources may accept for and on behalf of the state any gift, bequest, devise, or grants of lands or interest in lands or personal property of any kind or of money tendered to the state for any purpose pertaining to the activities of the department or any of its divisions. Any money so received is hereby appropriated and dedicated for the purpose for which it is granted. Lands and interests in lands so received may be sold or exchanged as provided in chapter 94. The deed conveying land or an interest in land to the state under this paragraph must clearly indicate whether the state may resell the donated land or interest in land.</p>
          <p>(b) When the commissioner of natural resources accepts lands or interests in land, the commissioner may reimburse the donor for costs incurred to obtain an appraisal needed for tax reporting purposes. If the state pays the donor for a portion of the value of the lands or interests in lands that are donated, the reimbursement for appraisal costs shall not exceed $1,500. If the donor receives no payment from the state for the lands or interests in lands that are donated, the reimbursement for appraisal costs shall not exceed $5,000.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of natural resources, on behalf of the state, may accept and use grants of money or property from the United States or other grantors for conservation purposes not inconsistent with the laws of this state. Any money or property so received is hereby appropriated and dedicated for the purposes for which it is granted, and shall be expended or used solely for such purposes in accordance with the federal laws and regulations pertaining thereto, subject to applicable state laws and rules as to manner of expenditure or use providing that the commissioner may make subgrants of any money received to other agencies, units of local government, private individuals, private organizations, and private nonprofit corporations. Appropriate funds and accounts shall be maintained by the commissioner of management and budget to secure compliance with this section.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may accept for and on behalf of the permanent school fund a donation of lands, interest in lands, or improvements on lands. A donation so received shall become state property, be classified as school trust land as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/92.025">92.025</a>, and be managed consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.31">127A.31</a>. When the commissioner proposes to accept a donation of land or an interest in land, the commissioner must notify the landowner of the option to express in the deed whether the state may resell the land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.085.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Wetlands.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must accept a gift, bequest, devise, or grant of wetlands, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.19">103G.005, subdivision 19</a>, or public waters wetlands, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005#stat.103G.005.15a">103G.005, subdivision 15a</a>, unless:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner determines that the value of the wetland for water quality, floodwater retention, public recreation, wildlife habitat, or other public benefits is minimal;</p>
          <p>(2) the wetland has been degraded by activities conducted without a required permit by the person offering the wetland and the person has not taken actions determined by the commissioner to be necessary to restore the wetland;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner determines that the wetland has been contaminated by a hazardous substance as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02#stat.115B.02.8">115B.02, subdivision 8</a>, a pollutant or contaminant as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115B.02#stat.115B.02.13">115B.02, subdivision 13</a>, or petroleum as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115C.02#stat.115C.02.10">115C.02, subdivision 10</a>, and the contamination has not been remedied as required under chapter 115B or 115C;</p>
          <p>(4) the wetland is subject to a lien or other encumbrance; or</p>
          <p>(5) the commissioner, after reasonable effort, has been unable to obtain an access to the wetland.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.085.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/3/#laws.0.77.0">Ex1971 c 3 s 77</a> subd 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.79.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/51/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 51 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/354/#laws.10.1.0">1991 c 354 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/578/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 578 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/462/#laws.0.43.0">1996 c 462 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.4.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.14.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.3.1.0">2006 c 281 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.3.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0854">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0854 GIFT CARD AND CERTIFICATE SALES; RECEIPTS; TRANSFERS; APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0854.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sales authorized; gift cards and certificates.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may sell gift cards and certificates that can be used to purchase licenses, permits, products, or services sold by the commissioner. Gift cards and certificates are valid until they are redeemed. The commissioner may advertise the availability of this program and items offered for sale under this section. The commissioner may make the purchase and redemption of gift cards available electronically.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0854.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Receipts; disposition.</span></h2>
          <p>Proceeds of gift card and certificate sales shall be deposited in an account in the special revenue fund. When gift cards or certificates are redeemed, funds shall be transferred to the appropriate account or fund based on the license, permit, product, or service purchased. Money in the gift card and certificate account shall accrue interest, which shall be credited to the account. Interest on funds in the account is appropriated to the commissioner to help cover the cost of administering the gift card and certificate program. Money from gift cards and certificates sold but unredeemed after three years shall be transferred to the various accounts and funds receiving revenue from purchases of licenses, permits, products, or services purchased with gift card or certificate redemptions in the last two fiscal years. Unredeemed funds shall be distributed based on the dollar value of cards redeemed for the various licenses, permits, products, or services on a pro rata basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0854.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemption from rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>This section is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0854.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.11.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0855">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0855 SALES; RECEIPTS; APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0855.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Sales authorized; gift certificates.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may sell natural resources-related publications and maps; forest resource assessment products; federal migratory waterfowl, junior duck, and other federal stamps; and other nature-related merchandise, and may rent or sell items for the convenience of persons using Department of Natural Resources facilities or services. The commissioner may sell gift certificates for any items rented or sold. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285">16A.1285</a>, a fee charged by the commissioner under this section may include a reasonable amount in excess of the actual cost to support Department of Natural Resources programs. The commissioner may advertise the availability of a program or item offered under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0855.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Software sales.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.15">16E.15</a>, the commissioner may sell or license intellectual property and software products or services developed by the department or custom developed by a vendor for the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0855.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Receipts; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.421">89.421</a>, money received by the commissioner under this section or to buy supplies for the use of volunteers, may be credited to one or more special accounts in the state treasury and is appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes for which the money was received. Money received from sales at the state fair shall be available for state fair related costs. Money received from sales of intellectual property and software products or services shall be available for development, maintenance, and support of software products and systems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0855.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemption from rulemaking and legislative approval.</span></h2>
          <p>A fee charged under this section is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The commissioner may establish fees under this section notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0855.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.93.0">1987 c 404 s 93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.4.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.2.19.0">1992 c 513 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.6.0">1997 c 226 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.84.0">1999 c 231 s 84</a>,85; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.6.0">2004 c 221 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.5.23.0">2005 c 156 art 5 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.22.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 22</a>,23</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0856">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0856 FLEET MANAGEMENT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources may bill organizational units within the Department of Natural Resources and other governmental units, including tribal governments, for the costs of providing them with equipment. Costs billed may include acquisition, licensing, insurance, maintenance, repair, and other direct costs as determined by the commissioner. Receipts and interest earned on the receipts shall be credited to a special account in the state treasury and are appropriated to the commissioner to pay the costs for which the billings were made.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0856.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.94.0">1987 c 404 s 94</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.3.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0857">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0857 FACILITIES MANAGEMENT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of natural resources may bill organizational units within the Department of Natural Resources and other governmental units, including tribal governments, for the costs of providing them with building and infrastructure facilities. Costs billed may include modifications and adaptations to allow for appropriate building occupancy, building code compliance, insurance, utility services, maintenance, repair, and other direct costs as determined by the commissioner. Receipts shall be credited to a special account in the state treasury and are appropriated to the commissioner to pay the costs for which the billings were made.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Money deposited in the special account from the proceeds of a sale under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.16">94.16</a>, subdivision 3, paragraph (b), is appropriated to the commissioner to acquire facilities or renovate existing buildings for administrative use or to acquire land for, design, and construct administrative buildings for the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner of natural resources may bill organizational units within the Department of Natural Resources and other governmental units, including tribal governments, for the costs of operating facilities. Receipts shall be credited to a special account in the state treasury and are appropriated to the commissioner to pay the costs for which the billings were made.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0857.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
