        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 255 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 357 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.4.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.6.0">2014 c 289 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.086">
        <h1 class="shn">84.086 SEALS, UNIFORMS, AND BADGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.086.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seals required.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Natural Resources and the several divisions thereof shall have seals in the form and design heretofore adopted, bearing the words "State of Minnesota, Department of Natural Resources," also, in case of a division seal, the title of the division. The seals may be used to authenticate the official acts of the commissioner or the directors, respectively, but omission or absence of the seal shall not affect the validity or force of any such act.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.086.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Badges and uniforms.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may provide for the issuance at state expense of such badges and uniforms as the commissioner deems necessary and suitable for officers or employees of the department and its divisions.</p>
          <p>(b) Uniforms for conservation officers and their supervisors shall be equipped with distinctive emblems, and shall be distinctive from the uniforms of any division or section of the Department of Natural Resources, the State Patrol, or any other state department or agency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.086.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.8.0">1943 c 60 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/181/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 181 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/905/#laws.0.9.0">1967 c 905 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/37/#laws.0.2.0">1981 c 37 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.087">
        <h1 class="shn">84.087 KEEPING RECORDS AND ORDERS.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise prescribed or required by law, the originals of all official records, orders, and other documents made, executed, or issued by or under the authority of the commissioner of natural resources or the directors of the several divisions of the department shall be filed and kept in the respective offices where the same were made, executed, or issued, or in such other office in the department as the commissioner may direct.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.087.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/60/#laws.0.10.0">1943 c 60 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0871">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0871 DATA ON FOREST INDUSTRY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The following data that the Department of Natural Resources collects, receives, or maintains through voluntary responses to questionnaires or surveys by forest industry businesses are classified as private data on individuals, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02</a>, subdivision 12, if the data are data on individuals or as nonpublic data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.9">13.02, subdivision 9</a>, if the data are data not on individuals:</p>
        <p>(1) timber resource consumption;</p>
        <p>(2) origin of timber resources;</p>
        <p>(3) cost of delivered timber;</p>
        <p>(4) forest industry product output; and</p>
        <p>(5) production costs.</p>
        <p>(b) Data that the department collects, receives, or maintains through voluntary responses to questionnaires or surveys by forest industry businesses and that are not specified under paragraph (a), clauses (1) to (5), are public data.</p>
        <p>(c) Summary data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.19">13.02, subdivision 19</a>, that the department compiles from data under paragraph (a) or (b) are public data.</p>
        <p>(d) Data collected, received, or maintained by the department from bidders on state timber under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/90.145">90.145</a> are not subject to this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0871.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.6.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0872">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0872 SPECIFIC LOCATION DATA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0872.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition; general classification.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, "specific location data" means data that would enable persons to locate the protected wild animal or endangered, threatened, or special concern plant or animal identified by the data. Specific location data are public data unless otherwise classified in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0872.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Nonpublic data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Specific location data procured by the Department of Natural Resources that identify protected wild animals, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.015#stat.97A.015.39">97A.015, subdivision 39</a>, or species that are designated endangered, threatened, or of special concern under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0895#stat.84.0895.3">84.0895, subdivision 3</a>, are nonpublic data if disclosure is likely to:</p>
          <p>(1) hinder management, propagation, or research;</p>
          <p>(2) facilitate unfair chase or illegal taking, transport, or sale; or</p>
          <p>(3) decrease the likelihood of establishing a protected wild animal or bringing an endangered, threatened, or special concern species to a point at which it is no longer endangered, threatened, or of special concern.</p>
          <p>(b) If a request for access to specific location data is denied under this subdivision, the commissioner must provide the requestor with a written explanation of the reason for the denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0872.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Disclosure.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may disclose data classified as nonpublic under subdivision 2 to a person, an agency, or the public if the commissioner determines that the disclosure will promote public benefit by:</p>
          <p>(1) aiding the environmental review process;</p>
          <p>(2) aiding research, education, or conservation planning; or</p>
          <p>(3) providing information to landowners about locations occurring on the landowners' property, if provision of the information will promote protection of the resource.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0872.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/290/#laws.0.23.0">2004 c 290 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0873">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0873 DATA ON INDIVIDUALS WHO ARE MINORS.</h1>
        <p>(a) When the Department of Natural Resources collects, creates, receives, maintains, or disseminates the following data on individuals who the department knows are minors, the data are considered private data on individuals, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>, except for data classified as public data according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.43">13.43</a>:</p>
        <p>(1) name;</p>
        <p>(2) date of birth;</p>
        <p>(3) Social Security number;</p>
        <p>(4) telephone number;</p>
        <p>(5) email address;</p>
        <p>(6) physical or mailing address;</p>
        <p>(7) location data;</p>
        <p>(8) online account access information;</p>
        <p>(9) data associated with the location of electronic devices; and</p>
        <p>(10) other data that would identify participants who have registered for events, programs, or classes sponsored by the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        <p>(b) Access to data described in paragraph (a) is subject to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1205.0500">1205.0500</a>. Data about minors classified under this section maintain their classification as private data on individuals after the individual is no longer a minor.</p>
        <p>(c) When data about minors is created, collected, stored, or maintained as part of the electronic licensing system described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0874">84.0874</a>, the data is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0874">84.0874</a> and may be disclosed pursuant to the provisions therein.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0873.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.3.7.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 3 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0874">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0874 ELECTRONIC LICENSING SYSTEM DATA.</h1>
        <p>(a) The following data created, collected, stored, or maintained by the department for purposes of obtaining a noncommercial game and fish license, cross-country-ski pass, horse pass, or snowmobile trail pass; registering a recreational motor vehicle; or any other electronic licensing transaction are private data on individuals as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>: name, addresses, driver's license number, and date of birth. The data may be disclosed for law enforcement purposes. The data, other than the driver's license number, may be disclosed to a government entity and for natural resources management purposes, including recruitment, retention, and training certification and verification.</p>
        <p>(b) Private data on individuals under paragraph (a) may be disclosed as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) for use by any government agency, including a court or law enforcement agency, in carrying out its functions, or any private person or entity acting on behalf of a federal, state, or local agency in carrying out its functions;</p>
        <p>(2) for use in connection with matters of vehicle or operator safety and theft, emissions, product alterations, recalls or advisories, and performance monitoring;</p>
        <p>(3) for use in the normal course of business by a legitimate business or its agents, employees, or contractors, in order to verify the accuracy of personal information submitted by an individual. If the information as submitted is not correct or is no longer correct, correct information may be obtained only for the purpose of preventing fraud by, pursuing legal remedies against, or recovering on a debt or security interest against the individual. If the person requesting access is acting as the agent of a lienholder, the requester must submit proof of a contract with the lienholder;</p>
        <p>(4) for use in connection with any civil, criminal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings in any federal, state, or local court or agency or before any self-regulatory body, including service of process, investigation in anticipation of litigation, and the execution or enforcement of judgments and orders, or pursuant to an order of a federal, state, or local court, provided that the requester provides a copy of the court order;</p>
        <p>(5) for use by any insurer or insurance support organization, or by a self-insured entity, or its agents, employees, or contractors, in connection with claims investigation activities or antifraud activities. If the person requesting access is an agent of an insurance company, the requester must provide the insurance company's name;</p>
        <p>(6) for use in providing notice to the owners of towed or impounded recreational vehicles or watercraft. The person requesting access must provide the name, address, and telephone number of the entity that requested that the recreational vehicle or watercraft be towed;</p>
        <p>(7) for use by any licensed private investigative agency or licensed security service for any purpose permitted under this section, provided that the person provides a copy of a valid license; or</p>
        <p>(8) where the use is related to the physical safety or security of operators, vehicles, pedestrians, or property.</p>
        <p class="bl">The commissioner must not disclose data under this paragraph if the commissioner concludes that the requester is likely to use the data for an improper purpose or other purpose not authorized by this paragraph.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0874.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.4.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.82.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/290/#laws.0.69.0">2012 c 290 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.3.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to this section by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 3, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0875">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0875 ENVIRONMENTAL LEARNING CENTERS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may acquire and better, or make grants to counties, home rule charter or statutory cities, or school districts to acquire and better, residential environmental learning centers where students may learn how to use, preserve, and renew the natural resources of this state. A facility and reasonable access to it must be owned by the state or a political subdivision but may be leased to or managed by a nonprofit organization to carry out an environmental learning program established by the commissioner. The lease or management agreement must comply with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.695">16A.695</a> and must provide for the procurement of liability insurance by the nonprofit organization. A nonprofit organization that is operating an environmental learning center under this section is a municipality for purposes of the liability limitations of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.04">466.04</a> while acting within the scope of these activities.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) During the time the center is used for educational programs offered in conjunction with a college or university, the rules and standards related to space requirements are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.74">144.74</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0875.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/643/#laws.0.46.0">1994 c 643 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/360/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 360 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.4.0">2008 c 357 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.088"><b>84.088</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.376.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 376</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.0885"><b>84.0885</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.2.32.0">1992 c 513 art 2 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.0887"><b>84.0887</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.176.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 176</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.089">
        <h1 class="shn">84.089 VOLUNTEERS IN NATURAL RESOURCES PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.089.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Volunteers permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may recruit, train, and accept without regard to personnel laws, or rules, the services of individuals without compensation as volunteers for or in aid of activities in and related to the areas or programs administered by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.089.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Incidental expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may provide for the incidental expenses of a volunteer, such as transportation, uniforms, lodging, and subsistence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.089.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application of law.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in this section, a volunteer is not a state employee and is not subject to the provisions of law relating to state employment, including but not limited to those relating to hours of work, rates of compensation, leave, unemployment benefits, and state employee benefits. A volunteer accepted under this section is a state employee for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.011#stat.176.011.9">176.011, subdivision 9</a>, and the provisions of chapter 176, relating to workers' compensation apply to the volunteer.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.089.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/384/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 384 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/488/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 488 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/107/#laws.0.66.0">1999 c 107 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/343/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 343 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0894">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0894 ENFORCEMENT OF AQUATIC PLANTS AND ENDANGERED SPECIES.</h1>
        <p>An enforcement officer shall enforce a violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0895">84.0895</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.091">84.091</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.093">84.093</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.152">84.152</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.615">103G.615</a> in the same manner as a violation of the game and fish laws.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0894.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.8.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 149 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.3.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0895">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0895 PROTECTION OF THREATENED AND ENDANGERED SPECIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law, a person may not take, import, transport, release, or sell any portion of an endangered or threatened species of wild animal or plant, or sell or possess with intent to sell an article made with any part of the skin, hide, or parts of an endangered or threatened species of wild animal or plant, except as provided in subdivisions 2 and 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subdivision 1 does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) plants on land classified for property tax purposes as class 2a or 2c agricultural land under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.13">273.13</a>, on a ditch, or on an existing public road right-of-way as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92#stat.84.92.6a">84.92, subdivision 6a</a>, except for ground not previously disturbed by construction or maintenance; and</p>
          <p>(2) noxious weeds designated pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18.76">18.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/18.88">18.88</a> or to weeds otherwise designated as troublesome by the Department of Agriculture.</p>
          <p>(b) If control of noxious weeds is necessary, it takes priority over the protection of endangered plant species, as long as a reasonable effort is taken to preserve the endangered plant species first.</p>
          <p>(c) The taking or killing of an endangered plant species on land adjacent to class 3 or 3b agricultural land as a result of the application of pesticides or other agricultural chemical on the class 3 or 3b land is not a violation of subdivision 1, if reasonable care is taken in the application of the pesticide or other chemical to avoid impact on adjacent lands. For the purpose of this paragraph, class 3 or 3b agricultural land does not include timber land, waste land, or other land for which the owner receives a state paid wetlands or native prairie tax credit.</p>
          <p>(d) The accidental taking of an endangered plant, where the existence of the plant is not known at the time of the taking, is not a violation of subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Designation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall adopt rules under chapter 14, to designate species of wild animal or plant as:</p>
          <p>(1) endangered, if the species is threatened with extinction throughout all or a significant portion of its range;</p>
          <p>(2) threatened, if the species is likely to become endangered within the foreseeable future throughout all or a significant portion of its range; or</p>
          <p>(3) species of special concern, if although the species is not endangered or threatened, it is extremely uncommon in this state, or has unique or highly specific habitat requirements and deserves careful monitoring of its status. Species on the periphery of their range that are not listed as threatened may be included in this category along with those species that were once threatened or endangered but now have increasing or protected, stable populations.</p>
          <p>(b) The range of the species in this state is a factor in determining its status as endangered, threatened, or of special concern. A designation by the secretary of the interior that a species is threatened or endangered is a prima facie showing under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall reevaluate the designated species list every three years after it is first adopted and make appropriate changes. The review must consider the need for further protection of species on the species of special concern list. Species may be withdrawn from designation in the same manner that species are designated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Studies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may conduct investigations to determine the status and requirements for survival of a resident species of wild animal or plant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Management.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any other law, the commissioner may undertake management programs, issue orders, and adopt rules necessary to bring a resident species of wild animal or plant that has been designated as threatened or endangered to a point at which it is no longer threatened or endangered.</p>
          <p>(b) Subject to the provisions of subdivision 6, management programs for endangered or threatened species include research, census, law enforcement, habitat acquisition, habitat maintenance, propagation, live trapping, transplantation, and regulated taking.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>A peace officer or conservation officer, pursuant to chapter 626, may execute a warrant to search for and seize goods, merchandise, plant or animal taken, sold or offered for sale in violation of this section, or items used in connection with a violation of this section. Seized property must be held pending judicial proceedings. Upon conviction, seized property is forfeited to the state and must be offered to a scientific or educational institution or destroyed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">General exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may issue permits and prescribe conditions for an act otherwise prohibited by subdivision 1 if:</p>
          <p>(1) the act is for the purpose of zoological, educational, or scientific study;</p>
          <p>(2) the act enhances the propagation or survival of the affected species;</p>
          <p>(3) the act prevents injury to persons or property; or</p>
          <p>(4) the social and economic benefits of the act outweigh the harm caused by it.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may issue a general permit to a governmental subdivision or to the general public to conduct one or more acts described in paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) A member of an endangered species may not be destroyed under paragraph (a), clause (3) or (4), until all alternatives, including live trapping and transplantation, have been evaluated and rejected. The commissioner may prescribe conditions to propagate a species or subspecies.</p>
          <p>(d) A person may capture or destroy a member of an endangered species, without permit, to avoid an immediate and demonstrable threat to human life or property.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner must give approval under this subdivision for forest management, including permit, sale, or lease of land for timber harvesting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply retroactively.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0895.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Violations.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of this section is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0895.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.9.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.22.0">1995 c 186 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.2.0">2012 c 272 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.13.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.7.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0896">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0896 TRADE IN PROHIBITED ANIMAL PARTS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0896.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Antique" means an item that:</p>
          <p>(1) contains no more than 200 grams of prohibited animal part as a fixed component of an item that is not made wholly or partially from a prohibited animal part; and</p>
          <p>(2) is documented to be at least 100 years old.</p>
          <p>(c) "Prohibited animal part" means any of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a tooth or tusk from any species of elephant, hippopotamus, mammoth, mastodon, walrus, whale, or narwhal, or any piece thereof, whether raw or worked;</p>
          <p>(2) a product containing any of the materials described in clause (1);</p>
          <p>(3) a horn; piece of horn; or derivative of a horn, such as a powder, of any species of rhinoceros; and</p>
          <p>(4) a product containing any of the materials described in clause (3).</p>
          <p>(d) "Sell" or "sale" means an exchange for consideration and includes barter and possession with intent to sell. The term does not include a transfer of ownership by gift, donation, or bequest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0896.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>A person shall not purchase or sell any item that the person knows or should know is a prohibited animal part.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0896.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subdivision 2 does not prohibit the sale or purchase of a prohibited animal part if the sale or purchase is:</p>
          <p>(1) undertaken as part of law enforcement activities;</p>
          <p>(2) expressly authorized by federal law;</p>
          <p>(3) of an antique;</p>
          <p>(4) of a musical instrument containing a lawfully acquired fixed component made of no more than 200 grams of prohibited animal part; or</p>
          <p>(5) of a prohibited animal part by a bona fide educational or scientific institution that is a nonprofit corporation, as defined in section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
          <p>(b) Subdivision 2 does not prohibit possession of a cultural artifact containing a prohibited animal part.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0896.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disposition of seized prohibited animal parts.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other provision of law, a prohibited animal part seized under this section must, upon a conviction, be forfeited to the state and either destroyed or given to a nonprofit corporation, as defined in section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code, for an educational or scientific purpose.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0896.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.14.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.09"><b>84.09</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.57.0">1996 c 410 s 57</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.091">
        <h1 class="shn">84.091 AQUATIC VEGETATION IN PUBLIC WATERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.091.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Ownership.</span></h2>
          <p>The state is the owner of wild rice and other aquatic vegetation growing in public waters. A person may not acquire a property interest in wild rice or other aquatic vegetation or destroy wild rice or aquatic vegetation, except as authorized under this chapter or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.615">103G.615</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.091.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License required; exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in this subdivision, a person may not harvest, buy, sell, transport, or possess aquatic plants without a license required under this chapter. A license shall be issued in the same manner as provided under the game and fish laws.</p>
          <p>(b) A resident under the age of 18 years may harvest wild rice without a license, if accompanied by a person with a wild rice license.</p>
          <p>(c) Tribal band members who possess a valid tribal identification card from a federally recognized tribe located in Minnesota are deemed to have a license to harvest wild rice under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.091.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">License fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fees for the following licenses, to be issued to residents only, are:</p>
          <p>(1) for harvesting wild rice:</p>
          <p>(i) for a season, $25; and</p>
          <p>(ii) for one day, $15;</p>
          <p>(2) for buying and selling wild ginseng, $5;</p>
          <p>(3) for a wild rice dealer's license to buy and sell 50,000 pounds or less, $70; and</p>
          <p>(4) for a wild rice dealer's license to buy and sell more than 50,000 pounds, $250.</p>
          <p>(b) The fee for a nonresident one-day license to harvest wild rice is $30.</p>
          <p>(c) The weight of the wild rice shall be determined in its raw state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.091.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.10.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.96.0">1987 c 404 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/200/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 200 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.15.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.5.0">2004 c 255 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.10.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.0911">
        <h1 class="shn">84.0911 WILD RICE MANAGEMENT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0911.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>The wild rice management account is established as an account in the game and fish fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0911.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Receipts.</span></h2>
          <p>Money received from the sale of wild rice licenses issued by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.091#stat.84.091.3">84.091, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), clauses (1), (3), and (4), and subdivision 3, paragraph (b), shall be credited to the wild rice management account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.0911.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use of money in account.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, money in the wild rice management account is annually appropriated to the commissioner and shall be used for management of designated public waters to improve natural wild rice production.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.0911.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.1.1.0">1987 c 149 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a>,109; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.78.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.23.0">1995 c 186 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.17.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.17.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.2.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.092"><b>84.092</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.4.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.0921"><b>84.0921</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.4.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.093">
        <h1 class="shn">84.093 WILD GINSENG.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may establish rules including seasons for harvesting to conserve wild ginseng.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.093.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.12.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.10">
        <h1 class="shn">84.10 WILD RICE HARVESTED IN CERTAIN LAKES.</h1>
        <p>It shall be unlawful for any person to take wild rice grain from any of the waters within the original boundaries of the White Earth, Leech Lake, Nett Lake, Vermillion, Grand Portage, Fond du Lac and Mille Lacs Reservations except said persons be of Indian blood, or residents of the reservation upon which said wild rice grain is taken.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/37-38">6131-5</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/231/#laws.0.2.0">1939 c 231 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/217/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 217 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/220/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 220 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/424/#laws.0.1.0">1947 c 424 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.105">
        <h1 class="shn">84.105 WILD RICE SEASON.</h1>
        <p>Ripe wild rice may be harvested from August 15 to September 30.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.19.0">1996 c 410 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.2.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.11"><b>84.11</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/424/#laws.0.6.0">1947 c 424 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.111">
        <h1 class="shn">84.111 NATURAL WILD RICE HARVESTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.111.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Watercraft restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>It shall be unlawful to use, in harvesting wild rice in any public waters in this state, any watercraft other than a boat, skiff, or canoe propelled by hand, which boat, skiff, or canoe may have a top width of not more than 36 inches and a length of not more than 18 feet, or any machine or mechanical device for gathering or harvesting the grain other than with flails not over 30 inches in length nor over one pound in weight, which flails must be held and operated by hand.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.111.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Pole restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful to use any pole for propelling any watercraft used in such harvesting which is not forked at the end, with each branch less than 12 inches in length.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.111.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Machine restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful to use in such harvesting any machine or device for gathering the grain other than a flail not over 30 inches in length nor over one pound in weight, held and operated by hand.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.111.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Harvest hours.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful to harvest any wild rice in any public waters between 3:00 p.m. and 9:00 a.m. following except as otherwise expressly permitted in writing by an authorized committee member or other agent of the commissioner pursuant to rules of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.111.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Fee holder provisions.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivisions 1 to 3, any person holding fee title to all property surrounding a body of public waters may use mechanical harvesting devices to harvest wild rice in those waters. This subdivision does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) any body of public waters greater than 125 acres in size;</p>
          <p>(2) any body of public waters to which the public has access directly or through a channel or watercourse;</p>
          <p>(3) any body of public waters within the original boundaries of any Indian reservation; or</p>
          <p>(4) harvesting of wild rice for use or sale by any person other than the owner of the surrounding property.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.111.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/506/#laws.0.3.0">1949 c 506 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/684/#laws.0.1.0">1959 c 684 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/174/#laws.0.1.0">1963 c 174 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/543/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 543 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.12"><b>84.12</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/424/#laws.0.6.0">1947 c 424 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.13"><b>84.13</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/424/#laws.0.6.0">1947 c 424 s 6</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.14"><b>84.14</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.57.0">1996 c 410 s 57</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.15">
        <h1 class="shn">84.15 COMMISSIONER'S AUTHORITY; RICE HARVESTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.15.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to restrict harvest.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may restrict or prohibit the harvesting of wild rice grain on public waters of any designated area when, upon investigation of conditions, it shall be determined necessary or advisable to protect against undue depletion of the crop so as to retard reseeding or restocking of such area or so as to endanger its effective use as a natural food for waterfowl.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.15.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Harvest for research or planting.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may harvest not to exceed 10,000 pounds of wild rice in any calendar year for the purposes of obtaining wild rice seed for experimental and research purposes and replanting in public waters of the state, including waters within the original boundaries of the Minnesota Indian reservations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/37-38">6131-15</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/231/#laws.0.12.0">1939 c 231 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/628/#laws.0.1.0">1949 c 628 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/85/#laws.0.1.0">1957 c 85 s 1</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.151"><b>84.151</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.152">
        <h1 class="shn">84.152 WILD RICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prescribe rules for harvesting and possessing wild rice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License required.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who buys wild rice within the state for resale to anyone except consumers, or sells wild rice imported from outside the state to anyone within the state except consumers must have a wild rice dealer's license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The application for a wild rice dealer's license application must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of wild rice, whether raw or processed, bought or sold by the applicant during the preceding calendar year;</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of wild rice the applicant estimates will be bought or sold under the license; and</p>
          <p>(3) other pertinent information required by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The license fee must be paid in advance, based on the applicant's estimate. A license may not be issued for a fee based on a lesser amount of wild rice than was bought or sold by the applicant during the preceding calendar year.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Supplemental license.</span></h2>
          <p>A wild rice dealer may not buy or sell wild rice for which a license is required in excess of the amount covered by the license. If a wild rice dealer desires to buy or sell wild rice in excess of the licensed amount, the dealer must apply for a supplemental license. The supplemental license shall be issued for the additional amount of wild rice upon payment of the prescribed fee, less credit for the fees paid for the previous license or licenses issued for the same calendar year. When the supplemental license is issued, the previous licenses held by the dealer shall be surrendered to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/350/#laws.19.2.0">1989 c 350 art 19 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.152.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person is guilty of a misdemeanor who:</p>
          <p>(1) willfully makes a false statement in an application for a license or in a required report or record; or</p>
          <p>(2) violates a provision relating to wild rice dealers.</p>
          <p>(b) Each violation is a separate offense. An acquittal prohibits later prosecution based on a similar charge involving other wild rice in the same transaction.</p>
          <p>(c) If a wild rice dealer is convicted of two offenses under this subdivision within three years, the dealer's license is null and void and the dealer may not be issued a license for one year after the date of the conviction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.152.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.13.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.4.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 3 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 4, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.1525">
        <h1 class="shn">84.1525 STROMATOLITES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.1525.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permit required.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not possess, move, or disturb a stromatolite located in waters of the state except under a permit issued by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.1525.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules establishing criteria and procedures for:</p>
          <p>(1) the issuance of stromatolite permits with reasonable conditions; and</p>
          <p>(2) the denial, modification, suspension, or revocation of stromatolite permits for cause.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.1525.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/462/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 462 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/231/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 231 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.153">
        <h1 class="shn">84.153 PROPERTY; LEASING, RENTING.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner is hereby authorized at public or private vendue and at such prices and under such terms and conditions as the commissioner may prescribe, to lease any buildings or lands not now authorized to be leased, acquired in the name of the state of Minnesota by any of the several divisions of the department which are not presently needed for the uses and purposes of any of the divisions of the department. The purposes for which such leases may be executed shall be in the furtherance of the interests of conservation and such uses shall not result in any permanent injury to the land. No such lease shall be made for a term to exceed two years and shall contain a provision for cancellation at any time by the commissioner upon three months written notice. All money received from these leases shall be credited to the fund from which the property was acquired, except an amount equal to the cost of maintenance of the leased property shall be credited to the fund charged with the cost of maintenance. Money credited for leased property maintenance is appropriated to the commissioner for that purpose.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner is hereby authorized to rent or lease to employees of the various divisions of the department such cabins, buildings, or living quarters as are now or may hereafter be constructed upon state-owned lands under the control of the several divisions of the department, when this occupancy is found to be necessary or beneficial to the work of the department. These leases or rental agreements shall be upon a month to month basis and provide for surrender by the lessee upon demand at any time the lessee's services with the state may be terminated, without the necessity of any written notice. All receipts from rents shall be paid in to the commissioner of management and budget and credited to the fund charged with the cost of maintenance of such buildings and are hereby appropriated for such use.</p>
        <p>(c) All instruments and transactions so negotiated shall be approved as to form, validity, and execution by the attorney general.</p>
        <p>(d) Hunting of wild game is prohibited on any land which has been posted by the lessee to prohibit hunting. Such prohibition shall apply to all persons including the lessee.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.153.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/291/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 291 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/382/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 382 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/376/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 376 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.154">
        <h1 class="shn">84.154 LAC QUI PARLE WATER CONTROL PROJECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conservation project.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner is hereby authorized, with the approval of the Executive Council, and on such terms as may be deemed advantageous to the state, to sell and convey to the United States the fee title, free from any mineral reservation, of lands acquired by the state for the Lac qui Parle River water control project upon which dams and appurtenant structures have been or may be constructed and such rights-of-way as may be required by the United States to provide access thereto for the purposes of construction, maintenance and operation, and to grant, sell and convey either such fee title to, or flowage rights over, all lands acquired for the project on and above Lac qui Parle Lake which lie below the 935.7 foot elevation on project datum, and to grant, sell and convey flowage rights only over all lands so acquired on or above Marsh Lake which lie below the 939.5 foot elevation on project datum and over all of such lands on and above either of these lakes which lie above such elevations, and to lease to any appropriate agency of the United States for conservation purposes, subject to such flowage rights, any of such lands the ownership of which is retained by the state, or to enter into a cooperative agreement with any such agency for the development and management of any wild life or other conservation activity thereon; provided, that no such conveyance or agreement shall waive any claim of the state for reimbursement from the United States. Each such lease for conservation purposes and each such cooperative agreement for the development and management of wild life or other conservation activity on such lands shall contain specific conditions reserving to the public during all open seasons for hunting wild waterfowl at least 40 percent of the area of these lands suitable for hunting waterfowl as public shooting grounds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner may complete Lac qui Parle and Big Stone Lake projects.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner of natural resources is authorized to construct all works and improvements pertaining or incidental to said projects which the commissioner deems necessary for such purposes, and to maintain and operate the same so far as not transferred to the United States pursuant to law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Powers of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may use for any project herein authorized any land of the state under the commissioner's jurisdiction or control so far as is not inconsistent with the laws governing the same, may acquire by purchase, gift, or condemnation any additional lands or interests in lands required for such projects, including lands or interests in adjacent states if authorized by the laws thereof, may accept gifts or grants of money or property from the United States or any other source for such projects, may use and apply any money or property so received in accordance with the terms of the gift or grant so far as is not inconsistent with the provisions of this section or other laws, may act in behalf of the state as sponsor for any such project undertaken or authorized by the United States, may make any sponsor's contributions required for any such project and may cooperate with the United States or any adjacent state or any authorized agency of either in constructing, maintaining and operating any such project upon such terms and conditions as the commissioner may deem proper not inconsistent with the laws of this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">May sell or lease land.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may, in behalf of the state, with the approval of the governor, sell or lease to the United States any part of the lands or interests in lands heretofore or hereafter acquired by the state for the purposes of such projects, with any structures or improvements thereon, upon such terms and conditions as the commissioner may deem proper, providing for the continued maintenance and operation of such projects for the purposes herein specified; provided that the provisions of this section shall not be deemed to repeal or supersede the provisions of Laws 1941, chapters 142 and 518, with respect to lands or interests heretofore acquired, so far as applicable thereto; provided, that the governor shall not approve any such sale or lease without first consulting the Legislative Advisory Commission and securing their recommendation, which shall be advisory only. Failure or refusal of the commission to make a recommendation promptly shall be deemed a negative recommendation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.70.0">2014 c 290 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.154.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Transfer to commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(1) The supervision and control of the Lac qui Parle project lands, which is now vested in the Executive Council by Laws 1925, chapter 426; Laws 1933, chapter 355; Extra Session Laws 1933, chapter 25; Laws 1935, chapter 51; Extra Session Laws 1935, chapter 101; Laws 1937, chapters 209 and 459; Extra Session Laws 1937, chapter 89; Laws 1941, chapters 142 and 518; Laws 1943, chapter 476; Laws 1945, chapter 325; Laws 1947, chapter 571; and Minnesota Statutes, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.154">84.154</a>, are hereby transferred to the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
          <p>(2) These lands, which consist of 22,000 acres, more or less, located in the north and east edge of Lac qui Parle County and portions of the south and west edges of Chippewa, Swift and Big Stone Counties, shall be used and developed as a game refuge and public hunting grounds as the commissioner of natural resources may designate and shall include all state-owned lands acquired pursuant to the provisions of law above stated.</p>
          <p>(3) The power of eminent domain will not be exercised in the case of the acquisition of additional lands to this game refuge and public hunting ground.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.154.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/518/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 518 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/476/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 476 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/755/#laws.0.1.0">1957 c 755 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/214/#laws.0.20.0">2006 c 214 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 290 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.155">
        <h1 class="shn">84.155 CONSERVATION PROJECTS; BELTRAMI ISLAND, PINE ISLAND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Certain leases between state and United States of America ratified and approved.</span></h2>
          <p>Leases entered into between the United States of America and the state of Minnesota, through the commissioner of conservation, under date of August 2, 1940, demising to the state of Minnesota federal owned lands in what are known as the Beltrami and Pine Island areas, located in the counties of Koochiching, Roseau, Lake of the Woods, and Beltrami, in the state of Minnesota, for a period of 50 years, are hereby in all things ratified and approved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Beltrami Island conservation project created.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of protecting, preserving, and managing wild life, forest and water resources, there is hereby established the Beltrami Island conservation project consisting of all lands within the descriptions hereinafter contained. All public lands, except tax-forfeited lands, lying within these areas are hereby set aside and reserved from sale. These areas shall comprise the following lands and waters in Beltrami County, Minnesota:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 155 and 156 North, in Ranges 31, 32, 33, 34, and 35 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 157 and 158 North, in Ranges 36 and 37 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>and the following described lands and waters in the Lake of the Woods County, Minnesota:</p>
          <p>All of Township 157 North, Range 32 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 157, 158, and 159 North, in Range 33 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 157, 158, 159, and 160 North, in Range 34 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 157, 158, 159, and 160 North, in Range 35 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 159 and 160 North, in Range 36 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>and the following described lands and waters in Roseau County, Minnesota:</p>
          <p>The South one-half of Township 161 North, in Range 35 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>The South one-half of Township 161 North, in Range 36 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 159 and 160 and the South two-thirds of Township 161 North, in Range 37 West of the fifth principal meridian, and</p>
          <p>The East two-thirds of Township 160 North, in Range 38 West of the fifth principal meridian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Pine Island conservation project created.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of protecting, preserving and managing wild life, forest and water resources, there is hereby established the Pine Island conservation project consisting of all lands within the descriptions hereinafter contained. All public lands, except tax-forfeited lands, lying within these areas are hereby set aside and reserved from sale. These areas shall comprise the following lands and waters in Koochiching County, Minnesota:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 64 and 65 North, in Range 24 and 25 West of the fourth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 64, 65, 66, and 67 North, in Range 26 West of the fourth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of the lands in Townships 64, 65, 66, and 67 North, in Range 27 West of the fourth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Township 152; the South one-half of Township 153; all of Townships 155 and 156; the West two-thirds of Township 157; and that portion of Township 158 North situated south of the center line of Black River, all in Range 25 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Township 152 North; the South one-half of Township 153; the North one-half of Township 155; all of Townships 156 and 157; and that portion of Township 158 North situated south of the center line of Black River, all in Range 26 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 153 and 154; the North one-half of Township 155; all of Townships 156 and 157, and that portion of Township 158 North situated south of the center line of Black River and east of that branch of the Black River that flows north through Sections 33 and 28, in Range 27 West of the fifth principal meridian:</p>
          <p>All of Townships 153, 154, 155, 156, 157, and 158 and the West one-third of Township 159 North, in Range 28 West of the fifth principal meridian; and</p>
          <p>All of Townships 153, 154, 155, 156, 157, 158, and 159 North, in Range 29 West of the fifth principal meridian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lands to be under management of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>All public lands except tax-forfeited lands, owned by the state of Minnesota, as well as lands owned by the United States and leased by the state of Minnesota within the Beltrami and Pine Island projects shall be under the management and control of the commissioner, who shall have authority to negotiate for and enter into on behalf of the state of Minnesota, leases for hay stumpage and timber stumpage at such fees and prices as the commissioner may determine reasonable and just.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner to make rules.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the boundaries of the Beltrami Island and Pine Island areas, the commissioner is hereby given full power and authority to make, establish, promulgate, and enforce all necessary rules for the care, preservation, protection, breeding, propagation, and disposition of any and all species of wild life therein and the regulation, issuance, sale, and revocation of special licenses or special permits for hunting, fishing, trapping, camping and other uses within the areas not inconsistent with the terms of this section. The commissioner shall have the power and authority to declare the terms and conditions of such licenses and permits and the charges to be made therefor. The commissioner may issue rules specifying and controlling the terms under and by which any wild animals may be taken, captured, or killed therein or under and by which fur-bearing animals having commercial value may be sold and transported. The commissioner may regulate and effect the sale of merchantable timber from such lands as are owned or leased by the state; provided, authority as to the leased lands shall not exceed that provided in the leases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.155.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Two accounts created; disposition of receipts.</span></h2>
          <p>There shall be created two accounts, one to be known as the Beltrami Island conservation account and the other as the Pine Island conservation account. All income and revenue received by virtue of all hay and stumpage leases, timber sales, sales of special licenses and permits, as hereinabove provided, from each of the areas described in this section, shall be paid in to the state treasury and credited to that account bearing the name of the project from which the income was derived. These sums are hereby appropriated for the purpose of administering said areas in accordance with the terms of this section and the terms of the leases herein referred to as having been approved and ratified and for making repairs and replacements on the properties leased as provided by the leases. Any portion of income or revenue not needed for the above purposes may be used, subject to the mutual agreement between the state of Minnesota and the United States provided for in the leases, covering the acquisition by the state of Minnesota of additional lands to block in, round out and enlarge its holdings. Nothing herein contained shall alter, modify, or change the method of handling revenue or income provided for in Laws 1929, chapter 258, from lands now in the public domain under the provisions of that act and nothing herein contained shall alter, modify, or change the method of handling revenue or income provided for in Laws 1929, chapter 258, from lands now in the public domain under the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/215/#laws.0.2.0">1941 c 215 s 2</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.106.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 106</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.156">
        <h1 class="shn">84.156 INTEREST IN CERTAIN LANDS TRANSFERRED TO UNITED STATES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.156.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant of easement to United States.</span></h2>
          <p>There is hereby granted to the United States an easement and right to flow and overflow by water the right-of-way of any and all town, county and state roads or highways lying within the Lac qui Parle water control project in Chippewa, Lac qui Parle, Big Stone, and Swift Counties, below the 945.0 foot elevation on project datum, and no claims for damage shall be maintainable against the United States by the state or any of its governmental subdivisions for any damage or injury to such roadways or highways, below such 945.0 foot elevation, because of the operation of any of the dams in said project or the maintenance of any water levels thereby.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.156.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner to acquire certain titles.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources of the state of Minnesota is hereby authorized to acquire by gift, purchase or condemnation, the underlying fee title to the right-of-way of any township or county roads or highways lying within such water control project and not now in public ownership, or the right to flow and overflow the same. The commissioner is further authorized to convey such fee title or flowage easements to the United States, together with any fee titles or easements heretofore obtained by or on behalf of the state, the counties or townships involved, to the right-of-way of any such roads or highways, when such conveyances are required to carry out the purposes of Laws 1943, chapter 476, and Laws 1941, chapter 518.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.156.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant effective upon acceptance.</span></h2>
          <p>The grant contained in subdivision 1 herein shall become effective upon the acceptance of title or easements by the United States to lands adjacent to each such road or highway.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.156.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certain laws continued in effect.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing herein shall be deemed to repeal or supersede Laws 1943, chapter 476, or Laws 1941, chapter 518, but the same and the whole thereof shall be continued in effect.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.156.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/325/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 325 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.157"><b>84.157</b> MS 2012 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.3435">94.3435</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.158"><b>84.158</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.10.4.0">1990 c 391 art 10 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.161">
        <h1 class="shn">84.161 LAND ACQUISITION FOR CERTAIN PURPOSES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources is hereby authorized to acquire on behalf of the Department of Natural Resources, state of Minnesota, all dam site and flowage easements and other interests in land by gift, purchase, condemnation or otherwise which may be necessary to accomplish the purposes of this section and to construct all dams, structures and control works needed to restore and control the water levels of Goose and Mud Lakes, Cass County, Minnesota, which authority to condemn shall include the condemnation of state-owned land whether held in trust or otherwise and whether or not the same be set aside as lake shore property or other special use under other provisions of law and the commissioner may further use any land of the state under the commissioner's jurisdiction for this project; all for the purpose of improving habitat for fish, wild fowl and game, wild rice and for forestry and fire protection.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.161.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/69/#laws.0.1.0">1957 c 69 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.162">
        <h1 class="shn">84.162 POWERS OF COMMISSIONER; FLOWAGE RIGHTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources is hereby authorized to enter into contracts and agreements with the United States and any authorized agency thereof for the use by the state of any flowage rights and other interests in land held by the United States needed for the flowage of land for this project and the commissioner of natural resources may acquire such property in fee and may further contract and cooperate with the United States for the operation and control of the levels of said water and the construction and maintenance of any of the structures needed therefor upon such terms and conditions as the commissioner may deem necessary and proper not otherwise inconsistent with law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.162.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/69/#laws.0.2.0">1957 c 69 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.163"><b>84.163</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.164"><b>84.164</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/859/#laws.0.14.0">1971 c 859 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.361"><b>84.361</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.362">
        <h1 class="shn">84.362 REMOVAL OF STRUCTURES.</h1>
        <p>Until after the sale of any parcel of tax-forfeited land, whether classified as agricultural or nonagricultural hereunder, the county auditor may, with the approval of the commissioner, provide:</p>
        <p>(1) for the sale or demolition of any structure located on the land that has been determined by the county board to be especially liable to fire or so situated as to endanger life or limb or other buildings or property in the vicinity because of age, dilapidated condition, defective chimney, defective electric wiring, any gas connection, heating apparatus, or other defect; and</p>
        <p>(2) for the sale of salvage material, if any, therefrom.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.362.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/278/#laws.0.8.0">1941 c 278 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/136/#laws.9.1.0">2005 c 136 art 9 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.363">
        <h1 class="shn">84.363 SALE OF DEAD AND DOWN TIMBER.</h1>
        <p>The county auditor may with the approval of the county board sell dead, down and mature timber upon any tract of agricultural land designated by the commissioner of natural resources. Such sale of timber products shall be for cash at not less than the appraised value thereof, as determined by the commissioner, to the highest bidder after not less than one week's published notice in an official paper within the county. Any timber offered at such public sale and not sold may thereafter be sold at private sale by the county auditor at not less than the appraised value thereof. The forestry practices to be followed in the cutting of this timber shall be approved by the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.363.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
