        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/278/#laws.0.9.0">1941 c 278 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.37"><b>84.37</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/5/#laws.0.12.0">1963 c 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.38"><b>84.38</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/5/#laws.0.12.0">1963 c 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.39"><b>84.39</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/5/#laws.0.12.0">1963 c 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.40"><b>84.40</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/5/#laws.0.12.0">1963 c 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.41"><b>84.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/5/#laws.0.12.0">1963 c 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.415">
        <h1 class="shn">84.415 UTILITY LICENSES; PERMITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Utility companies; permit to cross state-owned lands.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall, on or before January 1, 1974, promulgate in the manner provided by chapter 15, rules containing standards and criteria governing the sale of licenses permitting the passage of utilities over public lands and waters. The rules shall include provisions to insure that all projects for which licenses are sold will have a minimum adverse impact on the environment. The commissioner of natural resources may, at public or private sale and for such price and upon such terms as are specified in the rules (except where prohibited by law) grant licenses permitting passage over, under, or across any part of any school, university, internal improvement, swamp, tax-forfeited or other land or public water under the control of the commissioner of natural resources, of telephone, telegraph, and electric power lines, cables or conduits, underground or otherwise, or mains or pipe lines for gas, liquids, or solids in suspension. Any such license shall be cancelable upon reasonable notice by the commissioner for substantial violation of its terms, or if at any time its continuance will conflict with a public use of the land or water over or upon which it is granted, or for any other cause. All such land or public water shall remain subject to sale or lease or other legal use, but in case of sale, lease or other use there may be excepted from the grant or other disposition of land or public water all rights included in any license over, under, or across it, and the license may contain an agreement that there will be such exception. The commissioner may charge a fee in lieu of but not less than that authorized by subdivision 5 if issuing a license containing an agreement that there will be such an exception. All rights so excepted shall be reserved to the state and be cancelable by the commissioner for the same reasons or cause as they might have been canceled before such sale, lease or other use of the land or water. Upon such cancellation, which shall be only after reasonable notice to the licensee, all rights granted by the license shall be vested in the state and may be granted again by the commissioner on the terms and conditions the commissioner may prescribe, but subject to cancellation for the same reasons or causes as they might have been originally canceled unless ownership of the fee and of the license are merged. Any license granted before April 13, 1951, may be governed by it if the licensee and commissioner so agree. Reasonable notice as used in this subdivision means a 90-day written notice addressed to the record owner of the license at the last known address, and upon cancellation the commissioner may grant extensions of time to vacate the premises affected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/536/#laws.0.3.0">1967 c 536 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application; form.</span></h2>
          <p>The application for license or permit must include a legal description of the lands or waters affected, a metes and bounds description of the required right-of-way, a map showing said features, and a detailed design of any structures necessary, or in lieu thereof shall be in such other form, and include such other descriptions, maps or designs, as the commissioner may require. The commissioner may at any time order such changes or modifications respecting construction or maintenance of structures or other conditions of the license or permit as the commissioner deems necessary to protect the public health and safety.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Joint applications for residential use.</span></h2>
          <p>An application for a utility license may cover more than one type of utility if the utility lines are being installed for residential use only. Separate applications submitted by utilities for the same crossing shall be joined together and processed as one application, provided that the applications are submitted within one year of each other and the utility lines are for residential use only. The application fees for a joint application or separate applications subsequently joined together shall be as if only one application was submitted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attorney general; duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The license or permit to be granted shall be in a form to be prescribed by the attorney general; shall describe the location of the license or permit thereby granted and shall continue until canceled by the commissioner, subject to change or modification as herein provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Fees; disposition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the event the construction of lines causes damage to timber or other property of the state on or along the same, the license or permit shall also provide for payment to the commissioner of management and budget of the amount of the damages as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The application fee specified in Minnesota Rules is credited to the general fund.</p>
          <p>(c) The utility crossing fees specified in Minnesota Rules shall be credited to the fund to which other income or proceeds of sale from the land would be credited as provided by law, otherwise to the general fund.</p>
          <p>(d) Money received from licenses and permits issued under this section for use of the beds of navigable waters shall be credited to the permanent school fund.</p>
          <p>(e) Money received under subdivision 6 must be credited to the land management account in the natural resources fund and is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources to cover the costs incurred for issuing and monitoring utility licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Supplemental application fee and monitoring fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the application fee and utility crossing fees specified in Minnesota Rules, the commissioner of natural resources shall assess the applicant for a utility license the following fees:</p>
          <p>(1) to cover reasonable costs for reviewing an application and preparing a license, supplemental application fees as follows:</p>
          <p>(i) $1,750 for a public water crossing license and $3,000 for a public lands crossing license for electric power lines, cables, or conduits of 100 kilovolts or more and for main pipelines for gas, liquids, or solids in suspension;</p>
          <p>(ii) $1,000 for a public water crossing license and $1,000 for a public lands crossing license for applications to which item (i) does not apply; and</p>
          <p>(iii) for all applications, an additional $500 for each water crossing or land crossing in excess of two crossings; and</p>
          <p>(2) a monitoring fee to cover the projected reasonable costs for monitoring the construction of the utility line and preparing special terms and conditions of the license to ensure proper construction. The commissioner must give the applicant an estimate of the monitoring fee before the applicant submits the fee.</p>
          <p>(b) The applicant shall pay fees under this subdivision to the commissioner of natural resources. The commissioner shall not issue the license until the applicant has paid all fees in full.</p>
          <p>(c) Upon completion of construction of the improvement for which the license or permit was issued, the commissioner shall refund the unobligated balance from the monitoring fee revenue. The commissioner shall not return the application fees, even if the application is withdrawn or denied.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) "water crossing" means each location where the proposed utility will cross a public water between banks or shores; and</p>
          <p>(2) "land crossing" means each quarter-quarter section or government lot where the proposed utility will cross public land.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Application fee exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A utility license for crossing public lands or public waters is exempt from all application fees specified in rules adopted under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision does not apply to electric power lines, cables, or conduits 100 kilovolts or greater or to main pipelines for gas, liquids, or solids in suspension.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reimbursing costs.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to fees specified in this section or in rules adopted by the commissioner, the applicant must reimburse the state for costs incurred for cultural resources review, monitoring, or other services provided by the Minnesota Historical Society under contract with the commissioner of natural resources or the State Historic Preservation Office of the Department of Administration in connection with the license application, preparing the license terms, or constructing the utility line.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.415.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Fees for renewing license.</span></h2>
          <p>At the end of the license period, if both parties wish to renew a license, the commissioner must assess the applicant for all fees in this section as if the renewal is an application for a new license.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/145/#laws.0.0.0">1941 c 145</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/540/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 540 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/568/#laws.0.1.0">1947 c 568 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/356/#laws.0.1.0">1951 c 356 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/536/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 536 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/516/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 516 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/479/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 479 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.12.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.5.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.3.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.4.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.2.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 2</a>-5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.42">
        <h1 class="shn">84.42 VIOLATIONS; PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person violating any of the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.091">84.091</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.15">84.15</a>, or any of the orders of the commissioner promulgated in pursuance of the provisions thereof, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor; and, upon a second conviction within a period of three years, the person's license shall become null and void and no license of the same kind shall be issued to the person for one year after the date of such conviction; and any person violating, or threatening to violate, any provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.091">84.091</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.15">84.15</a> and Laws 1939, chapter 231, may be restrained by injunction proceedings brought in the name of the state by the attorney general or by any county attorney.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/45/#laws.0.73.0">1965 c 45 s 73</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/14">3109-6</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/37-38">6131-19</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/207/#laws.0.6.0">1939 c 207 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/231/#laws.0.16.0">1939 c 231 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/129/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 129 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.58.0">1996 c 410 s 58</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.43"><b>84.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.45"><b>84.45</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.47"><b>84.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.48"><b>84.48</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.49"><b>84.49</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.50"><b>84.50</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.51"><b>84.51</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.52"><b>84.52</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.521"><b>84.521</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.70.0">2014 c 289 s 70</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.523">
        <h1 class="shn">84.523 BWCA; MANAGING MINERALS AND RELATED RESOURCES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the term "Boundary Waters Canoe Area" means that area of lands and waters included within the boundaries designated in federal regulation REG U-3, Code of Federal Regulations, title 36, section 293.16, as that regulation provided on January 1, 1975.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Intent.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that a combination of state legislative and administrative actions and court decisions have established a public policy of primarily wilderness management for state lands and waters within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area. This state policy, together with a similar federal policy and international actions consistent with these state and federal policies, has created an area of hundreds of thousands of acres of land and water containing myriad lakes and streams, wooded shores, virgin forests, and other natural attractions of surpassing scenic beauty and solitude, free from substantially all commercial activities and artificial development such as hydroelectric dams and power lines, resorts, roads, sawmills, and timber harvesting in no-cut zones.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mining; prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>Except with the prior approval of the legislature in those cases of national emergency which have been declared by the Congress and which direct the need for exploration and mining of federal lands within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area, and after an investigation and determination by the commissioner of natural resources pursuant to subdivision 5 no state-owned or administered land may be leased for exploration or mining of minerals, and no state permits, licenses or leases shall be issued to use any other state natural resources for any mineral exploration or mining operations in the Boundary Waters Canoe Area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Peat harvesting; prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>Except with prior approval of the legislature in those cases of national emergency which have been declared by the Congress and which direct the need for exploitation of peat deposits on federal land within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area, and after an investigation and determination by the commissioner of natural resources pursuant to subdivision 5 no state-owned or administered land may be leased for the purpose of harvesting peat, and no state permits, licenses or leases shall be issued to use any other state natural resources for the purpose of harvesting peat in the Boundary Waters Canoe Area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Investigation and determination.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event of a national emergency declared by Congress which requires, for the protection of national interests, exploitation of natural resources of the type found in the Boundary Waters Canoe Area, the commissioner of natural resources shall investigate and determine if there are reasonable alternative methods for providing the needed resources. If the investigation shows there are reasonable alternatives to exploitation of natural resources in the area, no permit for development shall be issued. If the commissioner of natural resources determines there is a need to provide resources from within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area, and that there is no reasonable alternative available to meet the need, a permit may be issued upon approval by the state legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.523.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">State-owned lands.</span></h2>
          <p>Acquired lands owned by the state within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area defined in subdivision 1 are designated as state wilderness areas under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05#stat.86A.05.6">86A.05, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.523.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/322/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 322 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/13/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 13 s 1</a></p>
      </div>

        </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.84.525">
        <h1 class="shn">84.525 MAINTAINING CAMPSITES IN BWCA.</h1>
        <p>All reservation fees paid to the state attributable to state-owned lands within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area must be credited to an account in the special revenue fund and are appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for maintenance of state-owned campsites within the Boundary Waters Canoe Area. The commissioner may enter into cooperative agreements with the federal government for maintenance of the campsites.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.525.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.2.21.0">1992 c 513 art 2 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.53"><b>84.53</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.54"><b>84.54</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/163/#laws.1.35.0">1993 c 163 art 1 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.55"><b>84.55</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.56"><b>84.56</b> [Temporary appropriation]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.57"><b>84.57</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.58"><b>84.58</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.59"><b>84.59</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.60"><b>84.60</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.61"><b>84.61</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.611"><b>84.611</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.62"><b>84.62</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.621"><b>84.621</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/326/#laws.3.48.0">1989 c 326 art 3 s 48</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.625">
        <h1 class="shn">84.625 CONVEYING CONSERVATION EASEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, the commissioner of natural resources may, on state-owned lands administered by the commissioner and on behalf of the state, convey conservation easements as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84C.01">84C.01</a>, upon such terms and conditions, including reversion in the event of nonuse, as the commissioner may determine. Any terms and conditions obligating the state to incur costs related to monitoring or maintaining a conservation easement must acknowledge the state is liable for the costs only to the extent of an available appropriation according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.138">16A.138</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.625.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 21</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.63">
        <h1 class="shn">84.63 CONVEYING INTERESTS IN LANDS TO STATE, FEDERAL, AND TRIBAL GOVERNMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding any existing law to the contrary, the commissioner of natural resources is hereby authorized on behalf of the state to convey to the United States, to a federally recognized Indian Tribe, or to the state of Minnesota or any of its subdivisions, upon state-owned lands under the administration of the commissioner of natural resources, permanent or temporary easements for specified periods or otherwise for trails, highways, roads including limitation of right of access from the lands to adjacent highways and roads, flowage for development of fish and game resources, stream protection, flood control, and necessary appurtenances thereto, such conveyances to be made upon such terms and conditions including provision for reversion in the event of non-user as the commissioner of natural resources may determine.</p>
        <p>(b) In addition to the fee for the market value of the easement, the commissioner of natural resources shall assess the applicant the following fees:</p>
        <p>(1) an application fee of $2,000 to cover reasonable costs for reviewing the application and preparing the easement; and</p>
        <p>(2) a monitoring fee to cover the projected reasonable costs for monitoring the construction of the improvement for which the easement was conveyed and preparing special terms and conditions for the easement. The commissioner must give the applicant an estimate of the monitoring fee before the applicant submits the fee.</p>
        <p>(c) The applicant shall pay these fees to the commissioner of natural resources. The commissioner shall not issue the easement until the applicant has paid in full the application fee, the monitoring fee, and the market value payment for the easement.</p>
        <p>(d) Upon completion of construction of the improvement for which the easement was conveyed, the commissioner shall refund the unobligated balance from the monitoring fee revenue. The commissioner shall not return the application fee, even if the application is withdrawn or denied.</p>
        <p>(e) Money received under paragraph (b) must be deposited in the land management account in the natural resources fund and is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources to cover the reasonable costs incurred for issuing and monitoring easements.</p>
        <p>(f) A county or joint county regional railroad authority is exempt from all fees specified under this section for trail easements on state-owned land.</p>
        <p>(g) In addition to fees specified in this section, the applicant must reimburse the state for costs incurred for cultural resources review, monitoring, or other services provided by the Minnesota Historical Society under contract with the commissioner of natural resources or the State Historic Preservation Office of the Department of Administration in connection with the easement application, preparing the easement terms, or constructing the trail, highway, road, or other improvements.</p>
        <p>(h) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) to (g), the commissioner of natural resources may elect to assume the application fee under paragraph (b), clause (1), and waive or assume some or all of the remaining fees and costs imposed under this section if the commissioner determines that issuing the easement will benefit the state's land management interests.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/21/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1967 c 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/403/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 403 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.14.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.4.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/9/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 9 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.631">
        <h1 class="shn">84.631 ROAD EASEMENTS ACROSS STATE LANDS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.015#stat.85.015.1b">85.015, subdivision 1b</a>, the commissioner of natural resources, on behalf of the state, may convey a road easement across state land under the commissioner's jurisdiction to a private person requesting an easement for access to property owned by the person only if the following requirements are met:</p>
        <p>(1) there are no reasonable alternatives to obtain access to the property; and</p>
        <p>(2) the exercise of the easement will not cause significant adverse environmental or natural resource management impacts.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner shall:</p>
        <p>(1) require the applicant to pay the market value of the easement;</p>
        <p>(2) limit the easement term to 50 years if the road easement is across school trust land;</p>
        <p>(3) provide that the easement reverts to the state in the event of nonuse; and</p>
        <p>(4) impose other terms and conditions of use as necessary and appropriate under the circumstances.</p>
        <p>(c) An applicant shall submit an application fee of $2,000 with each application for a road easement across state land. The application fee is nonrefundable, even if the application is withdrawn or denied.</p>
        <p>(d) In addition to the payment for the market value of the easement and the application fee, the commissioner of natural resources shall assess the applicant a monitoring fee to cover the projected reasonable costs for monitoring the construction of the road and preparing special terms and conditions for the easement. The commissioner must give the applicant an estimate of the monitoring fee before the applicant submits the fee. The applicant shall pay the application and monitoring fees to the commissioner of natural resources. The commissioner shall not issue the easement until the applicant has paid in full the application fee, the monitoring fee, and the market value payment for the easement.</p>
        <p>(e) Upon completion of construction of the road, the commissioner shall refund the unobligated balance from the monitoring fee revenue.</p>
        <p>(f) Fees collected under paragraphs (c) and (d) must be credited to the land management account in the natural resources fund and are appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources to cover the reasonable costs incurred under this section.</p>
        <p>(g) In addition to fees specified in this section, the applicant must reimburse the state for costs incurred for cultural resources review, monitoring, or other services provided by the Minnesota Historical Society under contract with the commissioner of natural resources or the State Historic Preservation Office of the Department of Administration in connection with the easement application, preparing the easement terms, or constructing the road.</p>
        <p>(h) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) to (g), the commissioner of natural resources may elect to assume the application fee under paragraph (c) and waive or assume some or all of the remaining fees and costs imposed under this section if the commissioner determines that issuing the easement will benefit the state's land management interests.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.631.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/297/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 297 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/628/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 628 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.58.0">1995 c 220 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.18.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.15.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/236/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 236 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.23.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/9/#laws.0.2.0">2023 c 9 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.632">
        <h1 class="shn">84.632 CONVEYANCE OF UNNEEDED STATE EASEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/92.45">92.45</a>, the commissioner of natural resources may, in the name of the state, release all or part of an easement acquired by the state upon application of a landowner whose property is burdened with the easement if the easement is not needed for state purposes.</p>
        <p>(b) All or part of an easement may be released by payment of the market value of the easement. The release must be in a form approved by the attorney general.</p>
        <p>(c) Money received under paragraph (b) must be credited to the account from which money was expended for purchase of the easement. If there is no specific account, the money must be credited to the land acquisition account established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/94.165">94.165</a>.</p>
        <p>(d) In addition to payment under paragraph (b), the commissioner of natural resources shall assess a landowner who applies for a release under this section an application fee of $2,000 for reviewing the application and preparing the release of easement. The applicant shall pay the application fee to the commissioner of natural resources. The commissioner shall not issue the release of easement until the applicant has paid the application fee in full. The commissioner shall not return the application fee, even if the application is withdrawn or denied.</p>
        <p>(e) Money received under paragraph (d) must be credited to the land management account in the natural resources fund and is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources to cover the reasonable costs incurred under this section.</p>
        <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) to (e), the commissioner of natural resources may elect to assume the application fee under paragraph (d) and waive or assume some or all of the remaining fees and costs imposed under this section if the commissioner determines that issuing the easement release will benefit the state's land management interests.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.632.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/628/#laws.0.3.0">1988 c 628 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/285/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 285 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.16.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/9/#laws.0.3.0">2023 c 9 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.633">
        <h1 class="shn">84.633 EXCHANGING ROAD EASEMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources, on behalf of the state, may convey a road easement according to this section for access across state land under the commissioner's jurisdiction in exchange for a road easement for access to property owned by the United States, the state of Minnesota or any of its subdivisions, or a private party. The exercise of the easement across state land must not cause significant adverse environmental or natural resources management impacts. Exchanges under this section are limited to existing access corridors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Substantially equal acres.</span></h2>
          <p>The acres covered by the state easement conveyed by the commissioner must be substantially equal to the acres covered by the easement being received by the commissioner. For purposes of this section, "substantially equal" means that the acres do not differ by more than 20 percent. The commissioner's finding of substantially equal acres is in lieu of an appraisal or other determination of value of the lands. A state easement may be exchanged for an easement that has more than substantially equal acres if the other party to the exchange waives payment for the difference.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School trust lands.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner conveys a road easement over school trust land to a nongovernmental entity, the term of the road easement is limited to 50 years. The easement exchanged with the state may be limited to 50 years or may be perpetual.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Terms and conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may impose terms and conditions of use as necessary and appropriate under the circumstances. The state may accept an easement with similar terms and conditions as the state easement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Survey.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that a survey is required, the governmental unit or private landowner shall pay to the commissioner a survey fee of not less than one half of the cost of the survey as determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Application fee.</span></h2>
          <p>When a private landowner or governmental unit, except the state, presents to the commissioner an offer to exchange road easements, the private landowner or governmental unit shall pay an application fee as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.63">84.63</a> to cover reasonable costs for reviewing the application and preparing the easements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Title.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner determines it is necessary to obtain an opinion as to the title of the land being encumbered by the easement that will be received by the commissioner, the governmental unit or private landowner shall submit an abstract of title or other title information sufficient to determine possession of the land, improvements, liens, encumbrances, and other matters affecting title.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.633.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Disposition of fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any fee paid under subdivision 5 must be credited to the account from which expenses are or will be paid and the fee is appropriated for the expenditures in the same manner as other money in the account.</p>
          <p>(b) Any fee paid under subdivision 6 must be deposited in the land management account in the natural resources fund and is appropriated to the commissioner to cover the reasonable costs incurred for preparing and issuing the state road easement and accepting the road easement from the private landowner or governmental entity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.633.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.5.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/54/#laws.0.1.0">2017 c 54 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.64">
        <h1 class="shn">84.64 CONSERVATION RESTRICTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition.</span></h2>
          <p>A conservation restriction for a definite period or in perpetuity may be acquired by:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner of natural resources, in the name of the state, by gift, purchase or exchange, with funds specifically made available for that purpose;</p>
          <p>(2) a nonprofit charitable corporation whose purposes include conservation of land or water areas; or</p>
          <p>(3) a home rule charter or statutory city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of this section, a "conservation restriction" means a right, whether or not stated in the form of a restriction, easement, covenant or condition, in any deed, will or other instrument executed by or on behalf of the owner of land or in any order of taking, appropriate to retaining land or water areas predominately in their natural, scenic, open or wooded condition, or as suitable habitat for fish and wild life, to forbid or limit any or all:</p>
          <p>(1) construction or placing of buildings, roads, signs, billboards or other advertising, utilities or other structures on or above the ground;</p>
          <p>(2) dumping or placing of soil or other substance or material as landfill, or dumping or placing of trash, waste or unsightly or offensive materials;</p>
          <p>(3) removal or destruction of trees, shrubs or other vegetation;</p>
          <p>(4) excavation, dredging or removal of loam, peat, gravel, soil, rock or other material substance in such manner as to affect the surface;</p>
          <p>(5) surface use except for purposes permitting the land or water area to remain predominately in its natural condition;</p>
          <p>(6) activities detrimental to drainage, flood control, water conservation, erosion control or soil conservation, or fish and wild life habitat preservation; or</p>
          <p>(7) other acts or uses detrimental to such retention of land or water areas.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/531/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 531 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/163/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 163 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/159/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 159 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.65">
        <h1 class="shn">84.65 CONSERVATION RESTRICTIONS; EFFECT OF RECORDING; RELEASE OF RESTRICTIONS; ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.65.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Enforceability; notice; recording.</span></h2>
          <p>No conservation restriction defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.64">84.64</a> and acquired pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.64#stat.84.64.1">84.64, subdivision 1</a>, shall be unenforceable on account of lack of privity of estate or contract or lack of benefit to particular land. Nonprofit charitable corporations or home rule charter or statutory cities acquiring such conservation restrictions shall file a notice of each such acquisition with the Department of Natural Resources within 90 days. All restrictions shall be duly recorded and indexed in the office of the county recorder or the registrar of titles for the county where the land lies so as to affect its title, in the manner of other conveyances of interests in land, and shall describe the land subject to the restrictions by adequate legal description or by reference to a recorded plat showing its boundaries.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.65.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Remedy; right to enter.</span></h2>
          <p>The restriction may be enforced by injunction and shall entitle representatives of the holder of it to enter the land in a reasonable manner and at reasonable times to assure compliance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.65.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Conveyance; release; reversion.</span></h2>
          <p>The restriction may be conveyed in the same manner as any other interest in land. Notwithstanding any other provision to the contrary, a restriction may be released by the holder of the restriction or the dominant interest to the holder of the fee title or the servient interest. In the event a corporation, defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.64#stat.84.64.1">84.64, subdivision 1</a>, clause (2), no longer holds a license to do business in Minnesota, and said corporation has made no provision for the disposition of a conservation restriction held by it by transfer to another nonprofit corporation, the conservation restriction shall revert to and vest in the state of Minnesota and be administered by the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.65.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Construction.</span></h2>
          <p>This section shall not be construed to imply that any restriction, easement, covenant or condition which does not have the benefit of this section shall, on account of any provision hereof, be unenforceable. Nothing in this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.64">84.64</a> shall diminish the powers granted by law to the commissioner of natural resources or to any other state agency or political subdivision to acquire by purchase, gift, eminent domain or otherwise and to use land for public purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/531/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 531 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/163/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 163 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/181/#laws.0.2.0">1976 c 181 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/159/#laws.0.2.0">1979 c 159 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FORESTS FOR THE FUTURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.66">
        <h1 class="shn">84.66 MINNESOTA FORESTS FOR THE FUTURE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The Minnesota forests for the future program identifies and protects private, working forest lands for their timber, scenic, recreational, fish and wildlife habitat, threatened and endangered species, and other cultural and environmental values.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For the purpose of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "forest land" has the meaning given under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.001#stat.89.001.4">89.001, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "forest resources" has the meaning given under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.001#stat.89.001.8">89.001, subdivision 8</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) "guidelines" has the meaning given under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89A.01#stat.89A.01.8">89A.01, subdivision 8</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) "riparian land" has the meaning given under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.511#stat.103F.511.8c">103F.511, subdivision 8c</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) "working forest land" means land that provides a broad range of goods and services, including forest products, recreation, fish and wildlife habitat, clean air and water, and carbon sequestration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of natural resources shall establish and administer a Minnesota forests for the future program. Land selected for inclusion in the program shall be evaluated on the land's potential for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) producing timber and other forest products;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) maintaining forest landscapes;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) providing public recreation; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) providing ecological, fish and wildlife habitat, and other cultural and environmental values and values consistent with working forest lands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Land eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Land may be placed in the Minnesota forests for the future program if it:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) forest land;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) desirable land adjacent to forest land, as determined by the commissioner; or</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) beneficial to forest resource protection;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) is at least five acres in size, except for a riparian area or an area providing access to state forest land; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) is not set aside, enrolled, or diverted under another federal or state program, unless enrollment in the Minnesota forests for the future program would provide additional conservation benefits or a longer enrollment term than under the current federal or state program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Land interests.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may acquire permanent interests in lands by fee title, easement acquisition, gift, or donation. An acquired easement shall require a forestry management plan unless the requirement is waived or modified by the commissioner. The plan will guide forest management activities consistent with the purposes and terms of the easement and shall incorporate guidelines and other forest management practices as determined by the commissioner to provide perpetuation of the forest. The plan shall be developed in accordance with the guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall accept applications from owners of eligible lands at the time, in the form, and containing the information as the commissioner may prescribe. If the number of applications exceeds the ability to fund them all, priority shall be given to those applications covering lands providing the greatest public benefits for timber productivity, public access, and ecological and wildlife values.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Landowner responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may enroll eligible land in the program by signing an easement in recordable form with a landowner in which the landowner agrees to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) convey to the state a permanent easement that is not subject to any prior title, lien, or encumbrance, except for preexisting easements that are acceptable to the commissioner; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) manage the land in a manner consistent with the purposes for which the land was selected for the program and not convert the land to other uses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Correcting easement boundary lines.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">To correct errors in legal descriptions for easements that affect the ownership interests in the state and adjacent landowners, the commissioner may, in the name of the state, convey without consideration, interests of the state necessary to correct legal descriptions of boundaries. The conveyance must be by quitclaim deed or release in a form approved by the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Terminating or changing an easement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may terminate an easement, with the consent of the property owner, if the commissioner determines termination to be in the public interest. The commissioner may modify the terms of an easement if the commissioner determines that modification will help implement the Minnesota forests for the future program or facilitate the program's administration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Payments.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Payments to landowners under the Minnesota forests for the future program shall be made in accordance with law and Department of Natural Resources acquisition policies, procedures, and other funding requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Monitoring, enforcement, and damages.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall establish a long-term program for monitoring and enforcing Minnesota forests for the future easements. The program must require that a financial contribution be made for each easement to cover the costs of managing, monitoring, and enforcing the easement.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A landowner who violates the terms of an easement under this section or induces, assists, or allows another to do so is liable to the state for damages due to the loss of timber, scenic, recreational, fish and wildlife habitat, threatened and endangered species, and other cultural and environmental values.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Upon request of the commissioner, the attorney general may commence an action for specific performance, injunctive relief, damages, including attorney fees, and any other appropriate relief to enforce this section in district court in the county where all or part of the violation is alleged to have been committed or where the landowner resides or has a principal place of business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.66.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking exemption.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Easements agreed to under this section are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.5.0">2008 c 357 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.1.6.0">2008 c 368 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/172/#laws.2.11.0">2009 c 172 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.3.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.6.1.0">2023 c 60 art 6 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.67">
        <h1 class="shn">84.67 FORESTS FOR THE FUTURE REVOLVING ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p class="in">A forests for the future revolving account is created in the natural resources fund. Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for the acquisition of forest lands that meet the eligibility criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.66#stat.84.66.4">84.66, subdivision 4</a>. The commissioner shall sell the lands acquired under this section, subject to an easement as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.66">84.66</a>. Money received from the sale of forest lands acquired under this section and interest earned on the account shall be deposited into the account.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.67.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.6.0">2008 c 357 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.1.7.0">2008 c 368 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.3.0">2012 c 272 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.68"><b>84.68</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.150.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 150</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.69">
        <h1 class="shn">84.69 NATURAL RESOURCES CONSERVATION EASEMENT STEWARDSHIP ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.69.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established; sources.</span></h2>
          <p>The natural resources conservation easement stewardship account is created in the special revenue fund. The account consists of money credited to the account and interest and other earnings on money in the account. The State Board of Investment must manage the account to maximize long-term gain. The following revenue must be deposited in the natural resources conservation easement stewardship account:</p>
          <p>(1) contributions to the account or specified for any purpose of the account;</p>
          <p>(2) contributions under subdivision 3; section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.66#stat.84.66.11">84.66, subdivision 11</a>; or other applicable law;</p>
          <p>(3) money appropriated for any of the purposes described in subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(4) money appropriated for monitoring and enforcement of easements and earnings on the money appropriated that revert to the state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.056">97A.056, subdivision </a>17, or other applicable law; and</p>
          <p>(5) gifts under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.085">84.085</a> for conservation easement stewardship.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.69.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appropriation; purposes of account.</span></h2>
          <p>Five percent of the balance on July 1 of each year in the natural resources conservation easement stewardship account is annually appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources and may be spent only to cover the costs of managing conservation easements held by the Department of Natural Resources, including costs associated with monitoring, landowner contacts, records storage and management, processing landowner notices, requests for approval or amendments, enforcement, and legal services associated with conservation easement management activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.69.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Financial contributions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall seek a financial contribution to the natural resources conservation easement stewardship account for each conservation easement acquired by or assigned to the Department of Natural Resources. Unless otherwise provided by law, the commissioner shall determine the amount of the contribution, which must be an amount calculated to earn sufficient money to meet the costs of managing the conservation easement at a level that neither significantly overrecovers nor underrecovers the costs. In determining the amount of the financial contribution, the commissioner shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the estimated annual staff hours needed to manage the conservation easement, taking into consideration factors such as easement type, size, location, and complexity;</p>
          <p>(2) the average hourly wages for the class or classes of employees expected to manage the conservation easement;</p>
          <p>(3) the estimated annual travel expenses to manage the conservation easement;</p>
          <p>(4) the estimated annual miscellaneous costs to manage the conservation easement, including supplies and equipment, information technology support, and aerial flyovers;</p>
          <p>(5) the estimated annualized cost of legal services, including the cost to enforce the easement in the event of a violation; and</p>
          <p>(6) the expected rate of return on investments in the account.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.5.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.705">
        <h1 class="shn">84.705 COMMUNITY TREE-PLANTING GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.705.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Shade tree" means a woody perennial grown primarily for aesthetic or environmental purposes with minimal to residual timber value.</p>
          <p>(c) "Supplemental demographic index" means an index in the Environmental Justice Screening and Mapping Tool developed by the United States Environmental Protection Agency that is based on socioeconomic indicators, including low income, unemployment, less than high school education, limited English speaking, and low life expectancy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.705.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must establish a grant program to provide grants to cities, counties, townships, Tribal governments, and park and recreation boards in cities of the first class for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) removing and planting shade trees on public or Tribal land to provide environmental benefits;</p>
          <p>(2) replacing trees lost to forest pests, disease, or storms; and</p>
          <p>(3) establishing a more diverse community forest better able to withstand disease and forest pests.</p>
          <p>(b) Any tree planted with money granted under this section must be a climate-adapted species to Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.705.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Priority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Priority for grants awarded under this section must be given to:</p>
          <p>(1) projects removing and replacing ash trees that pose significant public safety concerns; and</p>
          <p>(2) projects located in a census block group with a supplemental demographic index score in the 70th percentile or higher within the state of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may not prioritize projects based on criteria other than the criteria established under paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.705.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Eligible projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The proceeds of state general obligation bonds may only be expended for grants to cities, counties, townships, and park and recreation boards in cities of the first class.</p>
          <p>(b) Appropriations from the general fund may be expended for grants to Tribal governments, cities, counties, townships, and park and recreation boards in cities of the first class.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.705.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.9.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RECREATIONAL VEHICLES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.76">
        <h1 class="shn">84.76 APPRENTICE RIDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.76.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of this section, "accompanied by" means within a distance of another person that permits uninterrupted visual contact and verbal communication.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.76.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Apprentice riders; requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.793">84.793</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.862">84.862</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.925">84.925</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9256">84.9256</a>, a person who is age 12 or over and who does not possess a required safety certificate may participate in up to two trail-riding events sponsored by the commissioner in state parks, state trails, state recreation areas, and state forests that are designed to involve apprentice riders. The person must be accompanied by an adult with a valid safety certificate. All vehicles must be properly registered for use in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.76.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.4.0">2012 c 272 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.765">
        <h1 class="shn">84.765 OPERATING OFF-ROAD RECREATIONAL VEHICLES WHILE IMPAIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.765.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, "controlled substance," "intoxicating substance," and "off-road recreational vehicle" have the meanings given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.03">169A.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.765.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Acts prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An owner or other person having charge or control of an off-road recreational vehicle must not authorize or allow an individual the person knows or has reason to believe is under the influence of alcohol, a controlled substance, or an intoxicating substance to operate the off-road recreational vehicle anywhere in the state or on the ice of a boundary water of the state.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who operates or is in physical control of an off-road recreational vehicle anywhere in the state or on the ice of a boundary water of the state is subject to chapter 169A.</p>
          <p>(c) The provisions of chapters 169A, 171, and 609 relating to revoking, suspending, or canceling a driver's license, an instruction permit, or a nonresident operating privilege for alcohol, controlled substance, or intoxicating substance violations apply to operators of off-road recreational vehicles and operating privileges for off-road recreational vehicles.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner of public safety must notify a person of the period during which the person is prohibited from operating an off-road recreational vehicle under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54">169A.54</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The court must promptly forward to the commissioner of public safety copies of all convictions and criminal and civil sanctions imposed under chapter 169A and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) If the person operating or in physical control of an off-road recreational vehicle is a program participant in the ignition interlock device program described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.306">171.306</a>, the off-road recreational vehicle may be operated only if it is equipped with an approved ignition interlock device and all requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.306">171.306</a> are satisfied. For purposes of this paragraph, "program participant" and "ignition interlock device" have the meanings given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.306#stat.171.306.1">171.306, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.765.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who violates subdivision 2, paragraph (a), or an ordinance conforming to subdivision 2, paragraph (a), is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who operates an off-road recreational vehicle during the period the person is prohibited from operating an off-road recreational vehicle under subdivision 2, paragraph (d), is subject to the penalty provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.24">171.24</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.771">
        <h1 class="shn">84.771 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE DEFINITION.</h1>
        <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.771">84.771</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.930">84.930</a>, "off-highway vehicle" means an off-highway motorcycle, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787#stat.84.787.7">84.787, subdivision 7</a>; an off-road vehicle, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797#stat.84.797.7">84.797, subdivision 7</a>; or an all-terrain vehicle, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92#stat.84.92.8">84.92, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.771.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.18.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.773">
        <h1 class="shn">84.773 RESTRICTIONS ON OPERATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.773.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not intentionally operate an off-highway vehicle:</p>
          <p>(1) on a trail on public land that is designated or signed for nonmotorized use only;</p>
          <p>(2) on restricted areas within public lands that are posted or where gates or other clearly visible structures are placed to prevent unauthorized motorized vehicle access;</p>
          <p>(3) except as specifically authorized by law or rule adopted by the commissioner, in unfrozen public waters, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.005">103G.005</a>; in a state park; in a scientific and natural area; or in a wildlife management area; or</p>
          <p>(4) in a calcareous fen, as identified by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.773.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Wetland disturbance.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not operate an off-highway vehicle in a manner to:</p>
          <p>(1) indicate a willful, wanton, or reckless disregard for the safety of persons or property;</p>
          <p>(2) carelessly upset the natural and ecological balance of a wetland or public waters wetland; or</p>
          <p>(3) impact a wetland or public waters wetland in excess of the amounts authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241#stat.103G.2241.9">103G.2241, subdivision 9</a>, unless:</p>
          <p>(i) sequencing of the impact is followed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.222#stat.103G.222.1">103G.222, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), and the impact is repaired under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2242">103G.2242</a>, and rules adopted pursuant to that section; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the activity is exempt under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103G.2241">103G.2241</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.773.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Private land access.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may grant up to a ten-year permit to exempt a private landowner or leaseholder from this section when the only reasonable access to a permit applicant's land is across state land.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.773.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.19.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.6.0">2004 c 255 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.774">
        <h1 class="shn">84.774 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE CRIMINAL PENALTIES.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b) and section 169A.20, a person who violates a provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.788">84.788</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.798">84.798</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.90">84.90</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922">84.922</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a> or rules of the commissioner relating to off-highway vehicle use is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A person is guilty of a gross misdemeanor if the person violates section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a>, subdivision 2, clause (2), and the person recklessly upsets the natural and ecological balance of a wetland or public waters wetland.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) A person is prohibited from operating an off-highway vehicle for a period of one year if the person is:</p>
        <p>(1) convicted of a gross misdemeanor under paragraph (b);</p>
        <p class="in">(2) convicted of or subject to a final order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.775">84.775</a> for a violation of the prohibition on the intentional operation on unfrozen public water, in a state park, in a scientific and natural area, or in a wildlife management area under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a>, subdivision 1, clause (3);</p>
        <p class="in">(3) convicted of or is subject to a final order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.775">84.775</a> for a violation of the prohibition on the willful, wanton, or reckless disregard for the safety of persons or property under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a>, subdivision 2, clause (1); or</p>
        <p>(4) convicted of or subject to a final order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.775">84.775</a> for a violation of the prohibition on carelessly upsetting the natural and ecological balance of a wetland or public waters wetland under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.2">84.773, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2).</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall notify the person of the time period during which the person is prohibited from operating an off-highway vehicle.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.774.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.4.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.12.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.7741">
        <h1 class="shn">84.7741 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE FORFEITURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) As used in this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Appropriate agency" means a law enforcement agency that has the authority to make an arrest for a violation of a designated offense.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Claimant" means an owner of an off-highway vehicle or a person claiming a leasehold or security interest in an off-highway vehicle.</p>
