          <p class="in">(d) "Designated offense" means a second gross misdemeanor violation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.774">84.774</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(e) "Family or household member" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a parent, stepparent, or guardian;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any of the following persons related by blood, marriage, or adoption: brother, sister, stepbrother, stepsister, first cousin, aunt, uncle, nephew, niece, grandparent, great-grandparent, great-uncle, or great-aunt; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) persons residing together or persons who regularly associate and communicate with one another outside of a workplace setting.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) "Off-highway vehicle" and "vehicle" do not include an off-highway vehicle that is stolen or taken in violation of the law.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) "Owner" means a person legally entitled to possession, use, and control of an off-highway vehicle, including a lessee of an off-highway vehicle if the lease agreement has a term of 180 days or more. There is a rebuttable presumption that a person registered as the owner of an off-highway vehicle according to the records of the Department of Public Safety or the Department of Natural Resources is the legal owner. For purposes of this section, if an off-highway vehicle is owned jointly by two or more people, each owner's interest extends to the whole of the vehicle and is not subject to apportionment.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) "Prosecuting authority" means the attorney in the jurisdiction in which the designated offense occurred, or a designee, who is responsible for prosecuting violations of a designated offense. If a state agency initiated the forfeiture and the attorney responsible for prosecuting the designated offense declines to pursue forfeiture, the attorney general's office, or its designee, may initiate forfeiture under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) "Security interest" means a bona fide security interest perfected according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168A.17#stat.168A.17.2">168A.17, subdivision 2</a>, based on a loan or other financing that, if an off-highway vehicle is required to be registered under chapter 168, is listed on the vehicle's title.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Seizure.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An off-highway vehicle subject to forfeiture under this section may be seized by the appropriate agency upon process issued by any court having jurisdiction over the vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Property may be seized without process if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the seizure is incident to a lawful arrest or a lawful search;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the vehicle subject to seizure has been the subject of a prior judgment in favor of the state in a criminal injunction or forfeiture proceeding under this section; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the appropriate agency has probable cause to believe that the delay occasioned by the necessity to obtain process would result in the removal or destruction of the vehicle. If property is seized without process under this clause, the prosecuting authority must institute a forfeiture action under this section as soon as is reasonably possible by serving a notice of seizure and intent to forfeit at the address of the owner as listed in the records of the Department of Public Safety or Department of Natural Resources.</p>
          <p>(c) When an off-highway vehicle is seized, the officer must provide a receipt to the person found in possession of the vehicle; or in the absence of any person, the officer must leave a receipt in the place where the vehicle was found, if reasonably possible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Right to possession vests immediately; custody.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All right, title, and interest in an off-highway vehicle subject to forfeiture under this section vests in the appropriate agency upon commission of the conduct resulting in the designated offense giving rise to the forfeiture. Any vehicle seized under this section is not subject to replevin, but is deemed to be in the custody of the appropriate agency subject to the orders and decrees of the court having jurisdiction over the forfeiture proceedings. When an off-highway vehicle is seized under this section, the appropriate agency shall use reasonable diligence to secure the property and prevent waste and may do any of the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) place the vehicle under seal;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) remove the vehicle to a place designated by the agency; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) place a disabling device on the vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Bond by owner for possession.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If the owner of an off-highway vehicle that has been seized under this section seeks possession of the vehicle before the forfeiture action is determined, the owner may give security or post bond payable to the appropriate agency in an amount equal to the retail value of the seized vehicle. On posting the security or bond, the seized vehicle may be returned to the owner. The forfeiture action must proceed against the security as if it were the seized vehicle. This subdivision does not apply to a vehicle being held for investigatory purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Evidence.</span></h2>
          <p>Certified copies of court records and off-highway vehicle and driver's records concerning prior incidents are admissible as substantive evidence where necessary to prove the commission of a designated offense.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Petition for remission or mitigation.</span></h2>
          <p>Prior to the entry of a court order disposing with the forfeiture action, any person who has an interest in forfeited property may file with the prosecuting authority a petition for remission or mitigation of the forfeiture. The prosecuting authority may remit or mitigate the forfeiture upon terms and conditions the prosecuting authority deems reasonable if the prosecuting authority finds that:</p>
          <p>(1) the forfeiture was incurred without willful negligence or without any intention on the part of the petitioner to violate the law; or</p>
          <p>(2) extenuating circumstances justify the remission or mitigation of the forfeiture.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Vehicle subject to forfeiture.</span></h2>
          <p>An off-highway vehicle is subject to forfeiture under this section if it was used in the commission of a designated offense.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Presumptions; limitations on vehicle forfeiture.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An off-highway vehicle is presumed subject to forfeiture under this section if the driver:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is convicted of the designated offense upon which the forfeiture is based; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) fails to appear for a scheduled court appearance with respect to the designated offense charged and fails to voluntarily surrender within 48 hours after the time required for appearance.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An off-highway vehicle encumbered by a security interest perfected according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168A.17#stat.168A.17.2">168A.17, subdivision 2</a>, or subject to a lease that has a term of 180 days or more, is subject to the interest of the secured party or lessor unless the party or lessor had knowledge of or consented to the act upon which the forfeiture is based. However, when the proceeds of the sale of a seized vehicle do not equal or exceed the outstanding loan balance, the appropriate agency shall remit all proceeds of the sale to the secured party after deducting the agency's costs for the seizure, tow, storage, forfeiture, and sale of the vehicle. If the sale of the vehicle is conducted in a commercially reasonable manner consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.9-610">336.9-610</a>, the agency is not liable to the secured party for any amount owed on the loan in excess of the sale proceeds. The validity and amount of a nonperfected security interest must be established by its holder by clear and convincing evidence.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Notwithstanding paragraph (b), the secured party's or lessor's interest in an off-highway vehicle is not subject to forfeiture based solely on the secured party's or lessor's knowledge of the act or omission upon which the forfeiture is based if the secured party or lessor demonstrates by clear and convincing evidence that the party or lessor took reasonable steps to terminate use of the vehicle by the offender.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) An off-highway vehicle is not subject to forfeiture under this section if its owner can demonstrate by clear and convincing evidence that the owner did not have actual or constructive knowledge that the vehicle would be used or operated in any manner contrary to law or that the owner took reasonable steps to prevent use of the vehicle by the offender. If the offender is a family or household member of the owner and has three or more prior off-highway vehicle convictions, the owner is presumed to know of any vehicle use by the offender that is contrary to law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Administrative forfeiture procedure.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An off-highway vehicle used to commit a designated offense is subject to administrative forfeiture under this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Within 60 days from when an off-highway vehicle is seized under subdivision 2, or within a reasonable time after seizure, the appropriate agency shall serve the driver or operator of the vehicle with a notice of the seizure and intent to forfeit the vehicle. Additionally, when an off-highway vehicle is seized under subdivision 2, or within a reasonable time after that, all persons known to have an ownership, possessory, or security interest in the vehicle must be notified of the seizure and the intent to forfeit the vehicle. For those vehicles required to be registered under chapter 168, the notification to a person known to have a security interest in the vehicle is required only if the vehicle is registered under chapter 168 and the interest is listed on the vehicle's title. Upon motion by the appropriate agency or prosecuting authority, a court may extend the time period for sending notice for a period not to exceed 90 days for good cause shown. Notice mailed by certified mail to the address shown in Department of Public Safety records is sufficient notice to the registered owner of the vehicle. For off-highway vehicles not required to be registered under chapter 168, notice mailed by certified mail to the address shown in the applicable filing or registration for the vehicle is sufficient notice to a person known to have an ownership, possessory, or security interest in the vehicle. Otherwise, notice may be given in the manner provided by law for service of a summons in a civil action.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The notice must be in writing and contain:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a description of the vehicle seized;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the date of the seizure; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) notice of the right to obtain judicial review of the forfeiture and of the procedure for obtaining that judicial review, printed in English. This requirement does not preclude the appropriate agency from printing the notice in other languages in addition to English.</p>
          <p>Substantially the following language must appear conspicuously in the notice:</p>
          <p>"WARNING: You will automatically lose the above-described property and the right to be heard in court if you do not file a lawsuit and serve the prosecuting authority within 60 days. You may file your lawsuit in conciliation court if the property is worth $15,000 or less; otherwise, you must file in district court. You may not have to pay a filing fee for your lawsuit if you are unable to afford the fee. You do not have to pay a conciliation court fee if your property is worth less than $500."</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If notice is not sent in accordance with paragraph (b), and no time extension is granted or the extension period has expired, the appropriate agency shall return the property to the person from whom the property was seized, if known. An agency's return of property due to lack of proper notice does not restrict the agency's authority to commence a forfeiture proceeding at a later time. The agency shall not be required to return contraband or other property that the person from whom the property was seized may not legally possess.</p>
          <p>(e) Within 60 days following service of a notice of seizure and forfeiture under this subdivision, a claimant may file a demand for a judicial determination of the forfeiture. The demand must be in the form of a civil complaint and must be filed with the court administrator in the county in which the seizure occurred, together with proof of service of a copy of the complaint on the prosecuting authority having jurisdiction over the forfeiture and the standard filing fee for civil actions unless the petitioner has the right to sue in forma pauperis under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/563.01">563.01</a>. The claimant may serve the complaint on the prosecuting authority by any means permitted by court rules. If the value of the seized property is $15,000 or less, the claimant may file an action in conciliation court for recovery of the seized vehicle. A copy of the conciliation court statement of claim must be served personally or by mail on the prosecuting authority having jurisdiction over the forfeiture within 60 days following service of the notice of seizure and forfeiture under this subdivision. If the value of the seized property is less than $500, the claimant does not have to pay the conciliation court filing fee. No responsive pleading is required of the prosecuting authority and no court fees may be charged for the prosecuting authority's appearance in the matter. Pleadings, filings, and methods of service are governed by the Rules of Civil Procedure.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The complaint must be captioned in the name of the claimant as plaintiff and the seized vehicle as defendant and must state with specificity the grounds on which the claimant alleges the vehicle was improperly seized, the claimant's interest in the vehicle seized, and any affirmative defenses the claimant may have. Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an action for the return of an off-highway vehicle seized under this section may not be maintained by or on behalf of any person who has been served with a notice of seizure and forfeiture unless the person has complied with this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) If the claimant makes a timely demand for a judicial determination under this subdivision, the forfeiture proceedings must be conducted according to subdivision 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Judicial forfeiture procedure.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) This subdivision governs judicial determinations of the forfeiture of an off-highway vehicle used to commit a designated offense. An action for forfeiture is a civil in rem action and is independent of any criminal prosecution. All proceedings are governed by the Rules of Civil Procedure.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If no demand for judicial determination of the forfeiture is pending, the prosecuting authority may, in the name of the jurisdiction pursuing the forfeiture, file a separate complaint against the vehicle, describing it, specifying that it was used in the commission of a designated offense, and specifying the time and place of its unlawful use.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The prosecuting authority may file an answer to a properly served demand for judicial determination, including an affirmative counterclaim for forfeiture. The prosecuting authority is not required to file an answer.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A judicial determination under this subdivision must be held at the earliest practicable date, and in any event no later than 180 days following the filing of the demand by the claimant. If a related criminal proceeding is pending, the hearing shall not be held until the conclusion of the criminal proceedings. The district court administrator shall schedule the hearing as soon as practicable after the conclusion of the criminal prosecution. The district court administrator shall establish procedures to ensure efficient compliance with this subdivision. The hearing is to the court without a jury.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) There is a presumption that an off-highway vehicle seized under this section is subject to forfeiture if the prosecuting authority establishes that the vehicle was used in the commission of a designated offense. A claimant bears the burden of proving any affirmative defense raised.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) If the forfeiture is based on the commission of a designated offense and the person charged with the designated offense appears in court as required and is not convicted of the offense, the court shall order the property returned to the person legally entitled to it upon that person's compliance with the redemption requirements of subdivision 12.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) If the lawful ownership of the vehicle used in the commission of a designated offense can be determined and the owner makes the demonstration required under subdivision 7, paragraph (d), the vehicle must be returned immediately upon the owner's compliance with the redemption requirements of subdivision 12.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) If the court orders the return of a seized vehicle under this subdivision, it must order that filing fees be reimbursed to the person who filed the demand for judicial determination. In addition, the court may order sanctions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/549.211">549.211</a>. Any reimbursement fees or sanctions must be paid from other forfeiture proceeds of the law enforcement agency and prosecuting authority involved and in the same proportion as distributed under subdivision 10, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Disposition of forfeited vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If the vehicle is administratively forfeited under subdivision 8, or if the court finds under subdivision 9 that the vehicle is subject to forfeiture under subdivisions 6 and 7, the appropriate agency shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) sell the vehicle and distribute the proceeds under paragraph (b); or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) keep the vehicle for official use. If the agency keeps a forfeited off-highway vehicle for official use, the agency shall make reasonable efforts to ensure that the off-highway vehicle is available for use by the agency's officers who participate in off-highway vehicle enforcement or education programs.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The proceeds from the sale of forfeited vehicles, after payment of seizure, towing, storage, forfeiture, and sale expenses and satisfaction of valid liens against the property, must be distributed as follows:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) 70 percent of the proceeds must be forwarded to the appropriate agency for deposit as a supplement to the state or local agency's operating fund or similar fund for use in purchasing equipment for off-highway vehicle enforcement, training, and education; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) 30 percent of the money or proceeds must be forwarded to the prosecuting authority that handled the forfeiture for deposit as a supplement to its operating fund or similar fund for prosecutorial purposes.</p>
          <p>(c) If a vehicle is sold under paragraph (a), the appropriate agency shall not sell the vehicle to:</p>
          <p>(1) an officer or employee of the agency that seized the property or to a person related to the officer or employee by blood or marriage; or</p>
          <p>(2) the prosecuting authority or any individual working in the same office or a person related to the authority or individual by blood or marriage.</p>
          <p>(d) Sales of forfeited vehicles under this section must be conducted in a commercially reasonable manner.</p>
          <p>(e) If a vehicle is forfeited administratively under this section and no demand for judicial determination is made, the appropriate agency shall provide the prosecuting authority with a copy of the forfeiture or evidence receipt, the notice of seizure and intent to forfeit, a statement of probable cause for forfeiture of the property, and a description of the property and its estimated value. Upon review and certification by the prosecuting authority that (1) the appropriate agency provided a receipt in accordance with subdivision 2, paragraph (c), (2) the appropriate agency served notice in accordance with subdivision 8, and (3) probable cause for forfeiture exists based on the officer's statement, the appropriate agency may dispose of the property in any of the ways listed in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Sale of forfeited vehicle by secured party.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A financial institution with a valid security interest in or a valid lease covering a forfeited off-highway vehicle may choose to dispose of the vehicle under this subdivision, in lieu of the appropriate agency disposing of the vehicle under subdivision 10. A financial institution wishing to dispose of an off-highway vehicle under this subdivision shall notify the appropriate agency of its intent, in writing, within 30 days after receiving notice of the seizure and forfeiture. The appropriate agency shall release the vehicle to the financial institution or its agent after the financial institution presents proof of its valid security agreement or of its lease agreement and the financial institution agrees not to sell the vehicle to a family or household member of the violator, unless the violator is not convicted of the offense on which the forfeiture is based. The financial institution shall dispose of the vehicle in a commercially reasonable manner as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.9-610">336.9-610</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) After disposing of the forfeited vehicle, the financial institution shall reimburse the appropriate agency for its seizure, storage, and forfeiture costs. The financial institution may then apply the proceeds of the sale to its storage costs, to its sale expenses, and to satisfy the lien or the lease on the vehicle. If any proceeds remain, the financial institution shall forward the proceeds to the state treasury, which shall credit the appropriate fund as specified in subdivision 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Redemption requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If an off-highway vehicle is seized by a peace officer for a designated offense, the seized vehicle must be released only:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) to the registered owner, a person authorized by the registered owner, a lienholder of record, or a person who has purchased the vehicle from the registered owner who provides proof of ownership of the vehicle;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) if the vehicle is subject to a rental or lease agreement, to a renter or lessee who provides a copy of the rental or lease agreement; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) to an agent of a towing company authorized by a registered owner if the owner provides proof of ownership of the vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The proof of ownership or, if applicable, the copy of the rental or lease agreement required under paragraph (a) must be provided to the law enforcement agency seizing the vehicle or to a person or entity designated by the law enforcement agency to receive the information.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) No law enforcement agency, local unit of government, or state agency is responsible or financially liable for any storage fees incurred due to a seizure under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7741.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>The appropriate agency and prosecuting authority shall report on forfeitures occurring under this section as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.5315">609.5315</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.7741.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.5.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/391/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 391 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/128/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 128 s 1</a>-7</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.775">
        <h1 class="shn">84.775 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE CIVIL CITATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.775.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Civil citation; authority to issue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A conservation officer or other licensed peace officer may issue a civil citation to a person who operates:</p>
          <p>(1) an off-highway motorcycle in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.1">84.773, subdivision 1</a> or 2, clause (1); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.788">84.788</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) an off-road vehicle in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.1">84.773, subdivision 1</a> or 2, clause (1); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.798">84.798</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) an all-terrain vehicle in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.1">84.773, subdivision 1</a> or 2, clause (1); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922">84.922</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) a snowmobile in violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82">84.82</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.872">84.872</a>; or</p>
          <p>(5) an off-highway motorcycle, an off-road vehicle, an all-terrain vehicle, or a snowmobile in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.90">84.90</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.001">97B.001</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A civil citation under paragraph (a) shall require restitution for public and private property damage and impose a penalty of:</p>
          <p>(1) $250 for the first offense;</p>
          <p>(2) $500 for the second offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) $1,000 for third and subsequent offenses.</p>
          <p>(c) A conservation officer or other licensed peace officer may issue a civil citation to a person who operates an off-highway motorcycle, off-road vehicle, or all-terrain vehicle in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.2">84.773, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2) or (3). A civil citation under this paragraph shall require restitution for damage to wetlands and impose a penalty of:</p>
          <p>(1) $100 for the first offense;</p>
          <p>(2) $500 for the second offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) $1,000 for third and subsequent offenses.</p>
          <p>(d) If the peace officer determines that there is damage to property requiring restitution, the commissioner must send a written explanation of the extent of the damage and the cost of the repair by first class mail to the address provided by the person receiving the citation within 15 days of the date of the citation.</p>
          <p>(e) An off-road vehicle that is equipped with a snorkel device and receives a civil citation under this section is subject to twice the penalty amounts in paragraphs (b) and (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.775.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appeals.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil citations issued under subdivision 1 may be appealed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a>, if the recipient of the citation requests a hearing by notifying the commissioner in writing within 30 days after receipt of the citation or, if applicable, within 15 days after the date of mailing the explanation of restitution. For the purposes of this section, the terms "commissioner" and "agency" as used in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a> mean the commissioner of natural resources. If a hearing is not requested within the 30-day period, the citation becomes a final order not subject to further review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.775.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil citations issued under subdivision 1 may be enforced under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072#stat.116.072.9">116.072, subdivision 9</a>. Penalty amounts must be remitted within 30 days of issuance of the citation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.775.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Allocating penalty amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Penalty amounts collected from civil citations issued under this section must be paid to the treasury of the unit of government employing the officer that issued the civil citation. Penalties retained by the commissioner shall be credited as follows: to the off-highway motorcycle account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.794">84.794</a> for citations involving off-highway motorcycles; to the off-road vehicle account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.803">84.803 </a>for citations involving off-road vehicles; to the all-terrain vehicle account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.927">84.927</a> for citations involving all-terrain vehicles; or to the snowmobile trails and enforcement account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.83">84.83</a> for citations involving snowmobiles. Penalty amounts credited under this subdivision are dedicated for enforcing off-highway vehicle laws or for enforcing snowmobile laws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.775.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Selecting remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>A peace officer may not seek both civil and misdemeanor penalties for offenses listed in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.775.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.7.0">2004 c 221 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.19.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.15.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/46/#laws.0.1.0">2022 c 46 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.777">
        <h1 class="shn">84.777 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLES AND SNOWMOBILES; USE OF STATE LANDS RESTRICTED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.777.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Designated trails.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as otherwise allowed by law or rules adopted by the commissioner, effective June 1, 2003, notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>, the use of off-highway vehicles is prohibited on state land administered by the commissioner of natural resources, and on county-administered forest land within the boundaries of a state forest, except on roads and trails specifically designated and posted by the commissioner for use by off-highway vehicles.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to county-administered land within a state forest if the county board adopts a resolution that modifies restrictions on the use of off-highway vehicles on county-administered land within the forest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.777.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Seasonal restrictions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except for designated forest roads, a person must not operate an off-highway vehicle or snowmobile on state forest lands during the firearms deer-hunting season in areas of the state where deer may be taken by rifle. This paragraph does not apply to a person in possession of a valid deer-hunting license operating an off-highway vehicle or snowmobile before or after legal shooting hours or from 11:00 a.m. to 2:00 p.m.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner may designate and post winter trails on state forest lands for use by off-highway vehicles.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For the purposes of this subdivision, "state forest lands" means forest lands under the authority of the commissioner as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.001">89.001</a>, subdivision 13, and lands managed by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/282.011">282.011</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.777.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mapped trails.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.926">84.926</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>, after completion of official department off-highway vehicle maps for the area, a person must not operate an off-highway vehicle on state land that is not mapped for the type of off-highway vehicle. This paragraph does not apply to state forest land north of U.S. Highway 2 until after June 30, 2009.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This subdivision does not apply to a forest access route in a managed forest north of U.S. Highway 2 that the commissioner has not designated as a road or trail. Forest access routes will not be signed or maintained and will not be included on published user maps of the forest. Off-highway vehicle operation on forest access routes is subject to the prohibitions on causing erosion, rutting, damage to trees or crops, and construction of unauthorized trails contained in Minnesota Rules. Damaged routes are subject to closure to off-highway vehicle use.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.777.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking exemption.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Determinations of the commissioner under this section may be by written order published in the State Register and are exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.777.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.21.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.24.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.3.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.7.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.6.0">2011 c 107 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.4.0">2014 c 290 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.780">
        <h1 class="shn">84.780 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE DAMAGE ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The off-highway vehicle damage account is created in the natural resources fund. Money in the off-highway vehicle damage account is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources for the repair or restoration of property damaged by the illegal operation of off-highway vehicles or the operation of off-highway vehicles in an unpermitted area after August 1, 2003, and for the costs of administration for this section. Before the commissioner may make a payment from this account, the commissioner must determine whether the damage to the property was caused by the unpermitted or illegal use of off-highway vehicles, that the applicant has made reasonable efforts to identify the responsible individual and obtain payment from the individual, and that the applicant has made reasonable efforts to prevent reoccurrence.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Determinations of the commissioner under this section may be made by written order and are exempt from the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Money in the account is available until expended.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.780.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.22.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.8.0">2004 c 221 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.25.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.781">
        <h1 class="shn">84.781 USE OF DEPARTMENT RESOURCES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources may permit Department of Natural Resources personnel and equipment to be used to assist local units of government in developing and maintaining off-highway vehicle grant-in-aid trails located on property owned by or under the control of the local unit of government.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.781.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 248 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OFF-HIGHWAY MOTORCYCLES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.787">
        <h1 class="shn">84.787 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accompanied.</span></h2>
          <p>"Accompanied" means subject to continuous direction or control.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">City.</span></h2>
          <p>"City" means a statutory or home rule charter city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Dealer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Dealer" means a person engaged in the business of selling off-highway motorcycles at wholesale or retail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Manufacturer" means a person engaged in the business of manufacturing off-highway motorcycles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Off-highway motorcycle.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Off-highway motorcycle" means a motorized, off-highway vehicle traveling on two wheels and having a seat or saddle designed to be straddled by the operator and handlebars for steering control, including a vehicle that is registered under chapter 168 for highway use if it is also used for off-highway operation on trails or unimproved terrain.</p>
          <p>(b) Off-highway motorcycle does not include an electric-assisted bicycle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011#stat.169.011.27">169.011, subdivision 27</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person, other than a person with a security interest, that has a property interest in or title to an off-highway motorcycle and is entitled to the use and possession of the motorcycle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.1-201">336.1-201</a>(b)(27).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Public road right-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public road right-of-way" means the entire right-of-way of a town road or a county, county state-aid, or trunk highway, including the traveled portions, banks, ditches, shoulders, and medians.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.787.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Register.</span></h2>
          <p>"Register" means the act of assigning a registration number to an off-highway motorcycle.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.787.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.1.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/162/#laws.3.2.0">2004 c 162 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/5/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2021 c 5 art 4 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.788">
        <h1 class="shn">84.788 REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Unless exempted in subdivision 2, a person may not operate and an owner may not give permission for another to operate an off-highway motorcycle unless the vehicle has been registered under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Registration is not required for off-highway motorcycles:</p>
          <p>(1) owned and used by the United States, an Indian tribal government, the state, another state, or a political subdivision;</p>
          <p>(2) registered in another state or country that have not been within this state for more than 30 consecutive days;</p>
          <p>(3) registered under chapter 168, when operated on forest roads to gain access to a state forest campground;</p>
          <p>(4) operated on state or grant-in-aid trails by a nonresident possessing a nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail pass;</p>
          <p>(5) operated by a person participating in an event for which the commissioner has issued a special use permit; or</p>
          <p>(6) operated on boundary trails and registered in another state or country providing equal reciprocal registration or licensing exemptions for registrants of this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application; issuance; reports.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Application for registration or continued registration must be made to the commissioner or an authorized deputy registrar of motor vehicles in a form prescribed by the commissioner. The form must state the name and address of every owner of the off-highway motorcycle.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person who purchases from a retail dealer an off-highway motorcycle shall make application for registration to the dealer at the point of sale. The dealer shall issue a dealer temporary 21-day registration permit to each purchaser who applies to the dealer for registration. The dealer shall submit the completed registration applications and fees to the deputy registrar at least once each week. No fee may be charged by a dealer to a purchaser for providing the temporary permit.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Upon receipt of the application and the appropriate fee, the commissioner or deputy registrar shall issue to the applicant, or provide to the dealer, an assigned registration number or a commissioner or deputy registrar temporary 21-day permit. Once issued, the registration number must be affixed to the motorcycle according to paragraph (f). A dealer subject to paragraph (b) shall provide the registration materials or temporary permit to the purchaser within the 21-day temporary permit period.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall develop a registration system to register vehicles under this section. A deputy registrar of motor vehicles acting under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.33">168.33</a>, is also a deputy registrar of off-highway motorcycles. The commissioner of natural resources in agreement with the commissioner of public safety may prescribe the accounting and procedural requirements necessary to ensure efficient handling of registrations and registration fees. Deputy registrars shall strictly comply with the accounting and procedural requirements.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) In addition to other fees prescribed by law, a filing fee of $4.50 is charged for each off-highway motorcycle registration renewal, duplicate or replacement registration card, and replacement decal and a filing fee of $7 is charged for each off-highway motorcycle registration and registration transfer issued by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a deputy registrar and must be deposited in the treasury of the jurisdiction where the deputy is appointed, or kept if the deputy is not a public official; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the commissioner and must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-highway motorcycle account.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Unless exempted in paragraph (g), the owner of an off-highway motorcycle must display a registration decal issued by the commissioner. If the motorcycle is licensed as a motor vehicle, a registration decal must be affixed on the upper left corner of the rear license plate. If the motorcycle is not licensed as a motor vehicle, the decal must be attached on the side of the motorcycle and may be attached to the fork tube. The decal must be attached in a manner so that it is visible while a rider is on the motorcycle. The issued decals must be of a size to work within the constraints of the electronic licensing system, not to exceed three inches high and three inches wide.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) Display of a registration decal is not required for an off-highway motorcycle:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) while being operated on private property; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) while competing in a closed-course competition event.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Registration card; signature; replacement fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide to the registrant a registration card that includes the registration number, the date of registration, the make and serial number of the off-highway motorcycle, the owner's name and address, and additional information the commissioner may require. The registration is not valid unless signed by at least one owner. Information concerning registrations must be kept by the commissioner. Upon a satisfactory showing that the registration card has been lost or destroyed, the commissioner shall issue a replacement registration card upon payment of a fee of $4. The fees collected from replacement registration cards must be credited to the off-highway motorcycle account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report of ownership transfers; fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Application for transfer of ownership of an off-highway motorcycle registered under this section must be made to the commissioner within 15 days of the date of transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) An application for transfer must be executed by the current owner and the purchaser using a bill of sale that includes the vehicle serial number.</p>
          <p>(c) The purchaser is subject to the penalties imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.774">84.774</a> if the purchaser fails to apply for transfer of ownership as provided under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Report of registration transfers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Application for transfer of registration under this section must be made to the commissioner within 15 days of the date of transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) An application for transfer must be executed by the current owner and the purchaser using a bill of sale that includes the vehicle serial number.</p>
          <p>(c) The purchaser is subject to the penalties imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.774">84.774</a> if the purchaser fails to apply for transfer of registration as provided under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Registration fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fee for registration of an off-highway motorcycle under this section, other than those registered by a dealer or manufacturer under paragraph (b) or (c), is $45 for three years and $4 for a duplicate or transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) The total registration fee for off-highway motorcycles owned by a dealer and operated for demonstration or testing purposes is $50 per year. Dealer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p>(c) The total registration fee for off-highway motorcycles owned by a manufacturer and operated for research, testing, experimentation, or demonstration purposes is $150 per year. Manufacturer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p>(d) The fees collected under this subdivision must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-highway motorcycle account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner of an off-highway motorcycle must renew registration in a manner prescribed by the commissioner upon payment of the appropriate registration fee in subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Vehicles owned by state or political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>A registration number must be issued without the payment of a fee for off-highway motorcycles owned by the state or political subdivision upon application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Licensing by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision of this state may not require licensing or registration of off-highway motorcycles covered by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Registration by minors prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person under the age of 18 may not register an off-highway motorcycle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a refund on a registration, not including any issuing fees paid under subdivision 3, paragraph (e), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), if the refund request is received within 12 months of the original registration and:</p>
          <p>(1) the off-highway motorcycle was registered incorrectly by the commissioner or the deputy registrar; or</p>
          <p>(2) the off-highway motorcycle was registered twice, once by the dealer and once by the customer.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.788.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Dual registration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An off-highway motorcycle registered under this section may also be registered as a motorcycle under chapter 168 for use on public roads and highways.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the off-highway motorcycle was not originally constructed primarily for use on public roads and highways, the off-highway motorcycle must be equipped with mirrors and a headlight, taillight, and horn and be otherwise modified as necessary to meet the requirements of chapter 169, the safety standards of the National Traffic and Motor Safety Act, Code of Federal Regulations, title 49, part 571, and the regulations adopted under that federal act, for motorcycles regarding safety and acceptability to operate on public roads and highways.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) An applicant for registration under chapter 168 must submit a form, prescribed by the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(d) For the purposes of this subdivision, off-highway motorcycle according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787#stat.84.787.7">84.787, subdivision 7</a>, does not include a golf cart; mini truck; dune buggy; go-cart; moped; pocket bike; gray market vehicle; or vehicle designed and used specifically for lawn maintenance, agriculture, logging, or mining purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.788.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.2.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.59.0">1995 c 220 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.20.0">1996 c 410 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.2.0">2001 c 185 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.23.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.4.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.7.0">2008 c 357 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.8.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.7.0">2011 c 107 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.12.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.6.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.4.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.16.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.6.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.5.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.10.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 10</a>,11</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b> The amendment to subdivision 11 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 5, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.789">
        <h1 class="shn">84.789 REQUIREMENTS OF MAKERS OF OFF-HIGHWAY MOTORCYCLES; SOUND EMISSIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.789.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Identification number.</span></h2>
          <p>An off-highway motorcycle made after January 1, 1994, and sold in the state, must have a manufacturer's permanent identification number stamped in letters and numbers on the vehicle in the form and at a location prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.789.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Registration number.</span></h2>
          <p>An off-highway motorcycle made after January 1, 1995, and sold in the state, must be designed and made to provide an area to affix the registration number. This area must be at a location and of dimensions prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.789.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sound emissions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On and after July 1, 2006, off-highway motorcycles, when operating on public lands, shall at all times be equipped with a silencer or other device that limits sound emissions according to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Sound emissions of competition off-highway motorcycles manufactured on or after January 1, 1998, are limited to not more than 96 dbA and, if manufactured prior to January 1, 1998, to not more than 99 dbA, when measured from a distance of 20 inches using test procedures established by the Society of Automotive Engineers under Standard J-1287, as applicable.</p>
          <p>(c) Sound emissions of all other off-highway motorcycles are limited to not more than 96 dbA if manufactured on or after January 1, 1986, and not more than 99 dbA if manufactured prior to January 1, 1986, when measured from a distance of 20 inches using test procedures established by the Society of Automotive Engineers under Standard J-1287, as applicable.</p>
          <p>(d) Off-highway motorcycles operating in closed course competition events are excluded from the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.789.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.3.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.24.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.79">
        <h1 class="shn">84.79 RULEMAKING; ACCIDENT REPORT.</h1>
        <p>(a) With a view of achieving proper use of off-highway motorcycles consistent with protection of the environment, the commissioner, in consultation with the commissioners of public safety and transportation, shall adopt rules under chapter 14 relating to:</p>
        <p>(1) registration of off-highway motorcycles and display of registration numbers;</p>
        <p>(2) use of off-highway motorcycles insofar as game and fish resources are affected;</p>
        <p>(3) use of off-highway motorcycles on public lands and waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner;</p>
        <p>(4) uniform signs to be used by the state, counties, and cities necessary or desirable to control, direct, or regulate the operation and use of off-highway motorcycles; and</p>
        <p>(5) off-highway motorcycle sound levels.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner of public safety, in consultation with the commissioners of natural resources and transportation, may adopt rules under chapter 14 regulating the use of off-highway motorcycles on public roads.</p>
        <p>(c) The operator and an officer investigating an accident of an off-highway motorcycle resulting in injury requiring medical attention or hospitalization to or death of a person or total damage to an extent of $500 or more shall forward within ten days a written report of the accident to the commissioner on a form prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.4.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.791">
        <h1 class="shn">84.791 EDUCATION AND TRAINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.791.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established; when required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish a comprehensive off-highway motorcycle environment and safety education and training program, including the preparation and dissemination of vehicle information and safety advice to the public, the training of off-highway motorcycle operators, and the issuance of off-highway motorcycle safety certificates to operators under the age of 16 years who successfully complete the off-highway motorcycle environment and safety education and training courses.</p>
          <p>(b) An individual who is convicted of violating a law related to the operation of an off-highway motorcycle must successfully complete the environment and safety education and training program established under paragraph (a) before continuing operation of an off-highway motorcycle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.791.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of administering the program and to defray a portion of the expenses of training and certifying vehicle operators, the commissioner shall collect a fee not to exceed $5 from each person who receives the training. The commissioner shall collect a fee for issuing a duplicate off-highway motorcycle safety certificate. The commissioner shall establish the fee for a duplicate off-highway motorcycle safety certificate, to include a $1 issuing fee for licensing agents, that neither significantly overrecovers nor underrecovers costs, including overhead costs, involved in providing the service. The fees, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents under this subdivision, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-highway motorcycle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.791.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cooperation and consultation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall cooperate with private organizations and associations, private and public corporations, and local governmental units in furtherance of the program established under this section. The commissioner shall consult with the commissioner of public safety in regard to training program subject matter and performance testing that leads to the certification of off-road motorcycle operators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.791.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Off-highway motorcycle safety courses; reciprocity with other states; accepted equivalencies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may enter into reciprocity agreements or otherwise certify off-highway motorcycle environment and safety education and training courses from other states that are substantially similar to in-state courses. Proof of completion of a course subject to a reciprocity agreement or certified as substantially similar is adequate to meet the safety certificate requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Proof of completion of the Motorcycle Safety Foundation Dirtbike School is adequate to meet the safety certificate requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.791.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exemption from rulemaking and legislative approval.</span></h2>
          <p>The fees provided for under subdivision 2 are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The commissioner may establish the fees under subdivision 2 notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.791.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.5.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.5.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.9.0">2004 c 221 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.25.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 25</a>,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.7.0">2014 c 289 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.792"><b>84.792</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.34.0">2001 c 185 s 34</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.793">
        <h1 class="shn">84.793 YOUTHFUL OPERATORS; PROHIBITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.793.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions on youthful operators.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person six years or older but less than 16 years of age operating an off-highway motorcycle on public lands or waters must possess a valid off-highway motorcycle safety certificate issued by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Except for operation on public road rights-of-way that is permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>, subdivision 1, a driver's license issued by the state or another state is required to operate an off-highway motorcycle along or on a public road right-of-way.</p>
          <p>(c) A person under 12 years of age may not:</p>
          <p>(1) make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way;</p>
          <p>(2) operate an off-highway motorcycle on a public road right-of-way in the state; or</p>
          <p>(3) operate an off-highway motorcycle on public lands or waters unless accompanied by a person 18 years of age or older or participating in an event for which the commissioner has issued a special use permit.</p>
          <p>(d) Except for public road rights-of-way of interstate highways, a person less than 16 years of age may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway only if that person is accompanied by a person 18 years of age or older who holds a valid driver's license.</p>
          <p>(e) A person less than 16 years of age may operate an off-highway motorcycle on public road rights-of-way in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (a), only if that person is accompanied by a person 18 years of age or older who holds a valid driver's license.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), a nonresident less than 16 years of age may operate an off-highway motorcycle on public lands or waters if the nonresident youth has in possession evidence of completing an off-road safety course offered by the Motorcycle Safety Foundation or another state as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.791">84.791</a>, subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.793.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Helmet required.</span></h2>
          <p>A person less than 18 years of age may not operate an off-highway motorcycle on public land, public waters, or on a public road right-of-way unless wearing a safety helmet approved by the commissioner of public safety.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.793.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions on owner.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner of an off-highway motorcycle may not knowingly allow it to be operated contrary to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.793.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Eye protection required.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not operate an off-highway motorcycle without an eye-protective device.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.793.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.7.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.6.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.9.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.5.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.794">
        <h1 class="shn">84.794 OFF-HIGHWAY MOTORCYCLE ACCOUNT; RECEIPTS AND ALLOCATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.794.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registration revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees from the registration of off-highway motorcycles and the unrefunded gasoline tax attributable to off-highway motorcycle use under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.18">296A.18</a> must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-highway motorcycle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.794.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subject to appropriation by the legislature, money in the off-highway motorcycle account may only be spent for:</p>
          <p>(1) administration, enforcement, and implementation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) acquisition, maintenance, and development of off-highway motorcycle trails and use areas;</p>
          <p>(3) grants-in-aid to counties and municipalities to construct and maintain off-highway motorcycle trails and use areas; and</p>
          <p>(4) grants for enforcement and public education to local law enforcement agencies.</p>
          <p>(b) The distribution of funds made available for grants-in-aid must be guided by the statewide comprehensive outdoor recreation plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.794.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.8.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.12.1.0">1994 c 587 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/299/#laws.0.30.0">1998 c 299 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.17.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.7945">
        <h1 class="shn">84.7945 NONRESIDENTS; STATE TRAIL PASS FOR OFF-HIGHWAY MOTORCYCLES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7945.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass required; fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A tribal member exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.788#stat.84.788.2">84.788, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2), or a nonresident, may not operate an off-highway motorcycle on a state or grant-in-aid off-highway motorcycle trail unless the operator carries a valid nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail pass in immediate possession. The pass must be available for inspection by a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of natural resources shall issue a pass upon application and payment of a $20 fee. The pass is valid from January 1 through December 31. Fees collected under this section, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-highway motorcycle account in the natural resources fund and, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, must be used for grants-in-aid to counties and municipalities for off-highway motorcycle organizations to construct and maintain off-highway motorcycle trails and use areas.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail pass is not required for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an off-highway motorcycle that is owned and used by the United States, another state, or a political subdivision thereof that is exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.788">84.788</a>, subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a person operating an off-highway motorcycle only on the portion of a trail that is owned by the person or the person's spouse, child, or parent; or</p>
          <p>(3) a nonresident operating an off-highway motorcycle that is registered according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.788">84.788</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7945.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may appoint agents to issue and sell nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time. The commissioner may adopt additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall observe all rules adopted by the commissioner for accounting and handling of passes pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall promptly deposit and remit all money received from the sale of the passes, exclusive of the issuing fee, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7945.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Issuing passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue and sell nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail passes. The commissioner shall also make the passes available through the electronic licensing system established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7945.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agent's fee.</span></h2>
