          <p>In addition to the fee for a pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee may be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes issued by the commissioner shall be deposited in the off-highway motorcycle account in the natural resources fund and retained for the operation of the electronic licensing system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.7945.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duplicate passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue a duplicate pass to persons whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405#stat.97A.405.3">97A.405, subdivision 3</a>, and rules adopted thereunder. The fee for a duplicate nonresident off-highway motorcycle state trail pass is $2, with an issuing fee of 50 cents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.7945.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.13.13.0">2014 c 312 art 13 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.795">
        <h1 class="shn">84.795 OPERATION REQUIREMENTS; LOCAL REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Operation on public road rights-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not operate an off-highway motorcycle within the right-of-way of a town road or a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway in this state unless the right-of-way encompasses:</p>
          <p>(1) a trail administered by the commissioner and designated for off-highway motorcycle use or multiple use; or</p>
          <p>(2) a corridor access trail designated under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) A road authority, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>, may designate, with the approval of the commissioner, corridor access trails on public road rights-of-way for gaining access to established off-highway motorcycle trails.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not operate an off-highway motorcycle upon a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway in this state unless the vehicle is equipped with at least one headlight and one taillight, each of minimum candlepower as prescribed by rule of the commissioner, and with brakes conforming to standards prescribed by rule of the commissioner, all of which are subject to the approval of the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(d) A person may not operate an off-highway motorcycle at any time within the right-of-way of an interstate highway or freeway within this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Crossing public road right-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person operating an off-highway motorcycle may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way provided:</p>
          <p>(1) the crossing is made at an angle of approximately 90 degrees to the direction of the road and at a place where no obstruction prevents a quick and safe crossing;</p>
          <p>(2) the off-highway motorcycle is brought to a complete stop before crossing the shoulder or main-traveled way of the road;</p>
          <p>(3) the driver yields the right-of-way to all oncoming traffic that constitutes an immediate hazard;</p>
          <p>(4) in crossing a divided road, the crossing is made only at an intersection of the road with another public road; and</p>
          <p>(5) if the crossing is made between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise or in conditions of reduced visibility, only if both front and rear lights are on.</p>
          <p>(b) Chapters 169 and 169A apply to the operation of off-highway motorcycles upon streets and highways, except for those provisions relating to required equipment and those provisions that by their nature have no application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivisions 1 and 2 do not apply to vehicles registered for public road use under chapter 168 when being operated on a traveled portion of a public road.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Operation generally.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not drive or operate an off-highway motorcycle:</p>
          <p>(1) at a rate of speed greater than reasonable or proper under the surrounding circumstances;</p>
          <p>(2) in a careless, reckless, or negligent manner so as to endanger or to cause injury or damage to the person or property of another;</p>
          <p>(3) in a tree nursery or planting in a manner that damages or destroys growing stock;</p>
          <p>(4) without a brake operational by either hand or foot;</p>
          <p>(5) at a speed exceeding ten miles per hour on the frozen surface of public waters within 100 feet of a person fishing or a fishing shelter; or</p>
          <p>(6) in a manner that violates operation rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Operating under influence of alcohol or controlled substance.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not operate or be in control of an off-highway motorcycle anywhere in this state or on the ice of any boundary water of this state while under the influence of alcohol or a controlled substance, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, and is subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.50">169A.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.53">169A.53 </a>or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Operation prohibited on airports.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not drive or operate an off-highway motorcycle on an airport defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/360.013#stat.360.013.39">360.013, subdivision 39</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Organized contests.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Nothing in this section or chapter 169 prohibits the use of off-highway motorcycles within the right-of-way of a state trunk or county state-aid highway or upon public lands or waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner of natural resources, in an organized contest or event, subject to the consent of the official or board having jurisdiction over the highway or public lands or waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In permitting the contest or event, the official or board having jurisdiction may prescribe restrictions, conditions, or permit revocation procedures, as the official or board considers advisable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.795.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Regulation by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>A county, city, or town, acting through its governing body, may regulate the operation of off-highway motorcycles on public lands, waters, and property under its jurisdiction other than public road rights-of-way within its boundaries, by resolution or ordinance of the governing body and by giving appropriate notice, provided that:</p>
          <p>(1) the regulations must be consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.795">84.795</a> and rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.79">84.79</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) an ordinance may not impose a fee for the use of public land or water under the jurisdiction of either the Department of Natural Resources or another agency of the state, or for the use of an access to it owned by the state, a county, or a city; and</p>
          <p>(3) an ordinance may not require an off-highway motorcycle operator to possess a motor vehicle driver's license while operating an off-highway motorcycle.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.795.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.1.9.0">1993 c 311 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/83/#laws.3.18.0">2017 c 83 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.796"><b>84.796</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.52.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 52</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OFF-ROAD VEHICLES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.797">
        <h1 class="shn">84.797 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">City.</span></h2>
          <p>"City" means a statutory or home rule charter city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Dealer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Dealer" means a person engaged in the business of selling off-road vehicles at wholesale or retail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Manufacturer" means a person engaged in the business of manufacturing off-road vehicles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Off-road.</span></h2>
          <p>"Off-road" means on trails or nonpublic roads or for cross-country travel on natural terrain. For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.8045">84.8045</a>, nonpublic roads include state forest roads, county forest roads, and other roads and trails that are not operated by a public road authority as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Off-road vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Off-road vehicle" or "vehicle" means a motor-driven recreational vehicle capable of cross-country travel on natural terrain without benefit of a road or trail.</p>
          <p>(b) Off-road vehicle does not include a snowmobile; an all-terrain vehicle; a motorcycle; an electric-assisted bicycle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011#stat.169.011.27">169.011, subdivision 27</a>; a watercraft; a farm vehicle being used for farming; a vehicle used for military, fire, emergency, or law enforcement purposes; a construction or logging vehicle used in the performance of its common function; a motor vehicle owned by or operated under contract with a utility, whether publicly or privately owned, when used for work on utilities; a commercial vehicle being used for its intended purpose; snow-grooming equipment when used for its intended purpose; or an aircraft.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Off-road vehicle use area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Off-road vehicle use area" means an area that is posted or designated for off-road vehicle use in accordance with rules adopted by the managing authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person, other than a person with a security interest, that has a property interest in or title to an off-road vehicle and is entitled to the use and possession of the vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.1-201">336.1-201</a>(b)(27).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Public road right-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public road right-of-way" means the entire right-of-way of a roadway that is not privately owned, including the traveled portions, banks, ditches, shoulders, and medians.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.797.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Off-road vehicle staging area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Off-road vehicle staging area" means a parking lot, trail head, campground, or other location to or from which an off-road vehicle is transported by truck, trailer, or other motor vehicle so that it may be placed into operation or removed from operation on public lands. Off-road vehicle staging area does not include a location to which an off-road vehicle is transported primarily for servicing, maintenance, repair, storage, or sale.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.797.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.1.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/162/#laws.3.3.0">2004 c 162 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.15.0">2010 c 382 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/5/#laws.4.4.0">1Sp2021 c 5 art 4 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.798">
        <h1 class="shn">84.798 REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless exempted under paragraph (b) or subdivision 2, after January 1, 1995, a person may not operate and an owner may not give permission for another to operate an off-road vehicle on off-road vehicle-designated trails or areas on land administered by the commissioner, or on off-road vehicle grant-in-aid trails and areas funded under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.803">84.803</a>, unless the vehicle has been registered under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Annually on the third Saturday of May, nonregistered off-road vehicles may be operated at the Iron Range Off-Highway Vehicle Recreation Area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Registration is not required for an off-road vehicle that is:</p>
          <p>(1) owned and used by the United States, an Indian tribal government, the state, another state, or a political subdivision;</p>
          <p>(2) registered in another state or country and has not been in this state for more than 30 consecutive days; or</p>
          <p>(3) operated with a valid state trail pass according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.8035">84.8035</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application; issuance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Application for registration or continued registration must be made to the commissioner, or an authorized deputy registrar of motor vehicles in a form prescribed by the commissioner. The form must state the name and address of every owner of the off-road vehicle. Upon receipt of the application and the appropriate fee, the commissioner shall register the off-road vehicle and assign a registration number that must be affixed to the vehicle in accordance with subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(b) A deputy registrar of motor vehicles acting under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.33">168.33</a> is also a deputy registrar of off-road vehicles. The commissioner of natural resources in cooperation with the commissioner of public safety may prescribe the accounting and procedural requirements necessary to ensure efficient handling of registrations and registration fees. Deputy registrars shall strictly comply with the accounting and procedural requirements. In addition to other fees prescribed by law, a filing fee of $4.50 is charged for each off-road vehicle registration renewal, duplicate or replacement registration card, and replacement decal and a filing fee of $7 is charged for each off-road vehicle registration and registration transfer issued by:</p>
          <p>(1) a deputy registrar and must be deposited in the treasury of the jurisdiction where the deputy is appointed, or retained if the deputy is not a public official; or</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner and must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-road vehicle account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Registration sticker.</span></h2>
          <p>An off-road vehicle must display a registration sticker issued by the commissioner. If the vehicle is licensed as a motor vehicle, the registration sticker must be affixed on the upper left corner of the rear license plate. If the vehicle is not licensed as a motor vehicle, the owner shall provide a plate not less than four inches high and 7-1/2 inches wide. The plate must be attached to the rear of the vehicle at least 12 inches from the ground. The registration sticker must be affixed on the upper left corner of the plate. Plates and registration stickers must be maintained in a clean and legible condition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Registration card; signature; replacement fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide to the registrant a registration card that includes the registration number, date of expiration, make and serial number of the off-road vehicle, owner's name and address, and additional information the commissioner may require. The registration is not valid unless signed by at least one owner. Information concerning each registration must be kept by the commissioner. If a registration card is lost or destroyed, the commissioner shall issue a replacement registration card on payment of a fee of $4. The fees collected from replacement registration cards must be credited to the off-road vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Registration fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fee for registration of an off-road vehicle under this section, other than those registered by a dealer or manufacturer under paragraph (b) or (c), is $30 for three years and $4 for a duplicate or transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) The total registration fee for off-road vehicles owned by a dealer and operated off-road for demonstration or testing purposes is $50 per year. Dealer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p>(c) The total registration fee for off-road vehicles owned by a manufacturer and operated off-road for research, testing, experimentation, or demonstration purposes is $150 per year. Manufacturer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p>(d) The fees collected under this subdivision must be credited to the off-road vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>An owner of an off-road vehicle must renew registration in a manner prescribed by the commissioner upon payment of the appropriate registration fee under subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Licensing by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>A political subdivision may not require licensing or registration of off-road vehicles regulated under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Registration by minors prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A person under the age of 18 may not register an off-road vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.798.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a refund on a registration, not including any issuing fees paid under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), if the refund request is received within 60 days of the original registration, the registration is not used or transferred, and:</p>
          <p>(1) the off-road vehicle was registered incorrectly; or</p>
          <p>(2) the off-road vehicle was registered twice, once by the dealer and once by the customer.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.798.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.2.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.60.0">1995 c 220 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.5.0">2001 c 185 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.25.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.27.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 27</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; art 2 s 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.10.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.11.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.6.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 10 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 6, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.799">
        <h1 class="shn">84.799 VEHICLE IDENTIFICATION NUMBER.</h1>
        <p>An off-road vehicle manufactured after January 1, 1995, and sold in the state must have a manufacturer's permanent identification number stamped in letters and numbers on the vehicle.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.799.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.3.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.80">
        <h1 class="shn">84.80 RULEMAKING; ACCIDENT REPORT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.80.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules under chapter 14 relating to:</p>
          <p>(1) the use of off-road vehicles, in a manner consistent with protection of the environment, on public lands and waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner of natural resources, including measures to minimize adverse impacts on soils, waters, vegetation, and wildlife;</p>
          <p>(2) off-road vehicle equipment and safety standards, in consultation with the commissioner of public safety;</p>
          <p>(3) uniform signs to be used by the state, counties, and cities to control, direct, or regulate the operation and use of off-road vehicles; and</p>
          <p>(4) maximum off-road vehicle sound levels.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.80.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accident report; requirement and form.</span></h2>
          <p>The operator and an officer investigating an accident involving an off-road vehicle and resulting in injury requiring medical attention or hospitalization, death, or total damage of $300 or more shall forward within ten days a written report of the accident to the commissioner of natural resources on a form prescribed by either the commissioner or the commissioner of public safety.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.4.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.801"><b>84.801</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.34.0">2001 c 185 s 34</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8015">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8015 EDUCATION AND TRAINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8015.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established; when required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish a comprehensive off-road vehicle environment and safety education and training program, including the preparation and dissemination of vehicle information and safety advice to the public, the training of off-road vehicle operators, and the issuance of off-road vehicle safety certificates to operators 16 to 18 years of age who successfully complete the off-road vehicle environment and safety education and training courses.</p>
          <p>(b) Beginning July 1, 2006, an individual who is convicted of violating a law related to the operation of an off-road vehicle must successfully complete the environment and safety education and training program established under paragraph (a) before continuing operation of an off-road vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8015.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of administering the program and to defray a portion of the expenses of training and certifying vehicle operators, the commissioner shall collect a fee not to exceed $15 from each person who receives the training. The commissioner shall collect a fee for issuing a duplicate off-road vehicle safety certificate. The commissioner shall establish the fee for a duplicate off-road vehicle safety certificate that neither significantly overrecovers nor underrecovers costs, including overhead costs, involved in providing the service. The fees must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-road vehicle account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8015.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cooperation and consultation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall cooperate with private organizations and associations, private and public corporations, and local governmental units in furtherance of the program established under this section. The commissioner shall consult with the commissioner of public safety in regard to training program subject matter and performance testing that leads to the certification of off-road vehicle operators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8015.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reciprocity with other states.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may enter into reciprocity agreements or otherwise certify off-road vehicle environment and safety education and training courses from other states that are substantially similar to in-state courses. Proof of completion of a course subject to a reciprocity agreement or certified as substantially similar is adequate to meet the safety certificate requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8015.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.28.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 28</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.802">
        <h1 class="shn">84.802 YOUTHFUL OPERATORS; PROHIBITIONS.</h1>
        <p>(a) A person under 16 years of age may not operate an off-road vehicle.</p>
        <p>(b) Except for operation on public road rights-of-way that is permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a>, a driver's license issued by the state or another state is required to operate an off-road vehicle along or on a public road right-of-way.</p>
        <p>(c) An owner of an off-road vehicle may not knowingly allow it to be operated in violation of this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.802.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.6.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.803">
        <h1 class="shn">84.803 OFF-ROAD VEHICLE ACCOUNT; RECEIPTS AND ALLOCATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.803.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registration revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees from the registration of off-road vehicles and unrefunded gasoline tax attributable to off-road vehicle use under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.18">296A.18</a> must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-road vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.803.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to appropriation by the legislature, money in the off-road vehicle account may only be spent for:</p>
          <p>(1) administration, enforcement, and implementation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.8045">84.8045</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) acquisition, maintenance, and development of off-road vehicle trails and use areas;</p>
          <p>(3) grant-in-aid programs to counties and municipalities to construct and maintain off-road vehicle trails and use areas;</p>
          <p>(4) grants-in-aid to local safety programs; and</p>
          <p>(5) enforcement and public education grants to local law enforcement agencies.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.803.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.7.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.12.2.0">1994 c 587 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/299/#laws.0.30.0">1998 c 299 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.26.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.16.0">2010 c 382 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8031">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8031 GRANT-IN-AID APPLICATIONS; REVIEW PERIOD.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner must review an off-road vehicle grant-in-aid application and, if approved, begin public review of the application within 60 days after the completed application has been locally approved and submitted to an area parks and trails office. If the commissioner fails to approve or deny the application within 60 days after submission, the application is deemed approved and the commissioner must provide for a 30-day public review period. If the commissioner denies an application, the commissioner must provide the applicant with a written explanation for denying the application at the time the applicant is notified of the denial.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8031.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.8.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.6.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8035">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8035 OFF-ROAD VEHICLE STATE TRAIL PASS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8035.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass required; fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except as provided under paragraph (c), a person may not operate an off-road vehicle on a state or grant-in-aid off-road vehicle trail or use area unless the vehicle displays an off-road vehicle state trail pass issued according to this section. The pass must be available to be viewed by a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner of natural resources shall issue a pass upon application and payment of the fee. Fees collected under this section, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the off-road vehicle account in the natural resources fund and, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, must be used for grants-in-aid to counties and municipalities for off-road vehicle organizations to construct and maintain off-road vehicle trails and use areas.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) An off-road vehicle state trail pass is not required for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an off-road vehicle that is owned and used by the United States, another state, or a political subdivision thereof that is exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.798">84.798</a>, subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a person operating an off-road vehicle only on the portion of a trail that is owned by the person or the person's spouse, child, or parent; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a person operating an off-road vehicle that is registered according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.798">84.798</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The fee for an annual nonresident off-road vehicle state trail pass is $20. The nonresident pass is valid from January 1 through December 31. The fee for a nonresident three-year pass is $30.</p>
          <p>(e) The fee for a resident off-road vehicle state trail pass is $20. The resident pass is valid for 30 consecutive days after the date of issuance.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8035.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may appoint agents to issue and sell off-road vehicle state trail passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time. The commissioner may adopt additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall observe all rules adopted by the commissioner for accounting and handling of passes pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall promptly deposit and remit all money received from the sale of the passes, exclusive of the issuing fee, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8035.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Issuing passes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner and agents shall issue and sell off-road vehicle state trail passes. The commissioner shall also make the passes available through the electronic licensing system established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8035.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agent's fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In addition to the fee for a pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee may be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes issued by the commissioner shall be deposited in the off-road vehicle account in the natural resources fund and retained for the operation of the electronic licensing system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8035.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duplicate passes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner and agents shall issue a duplicate pass to persons whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405#stat.97A.405.3">97A.405, subdivision 3</a>, and rules adopted thereunder. The fee for a duplicate off-road vehicle state trail pass is $4, with an issuing fee of 50 cents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8035.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.8.0">2011 c 107 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.3.12.0">2016 c 189 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.7.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 1 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 7, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.804">
        <h1 class="shn">84.804 OPERATION REQUIREMENTS; LOCAL REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Operation on public road rights-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not operate a vehicle off-road within a public road right-of-way in this state except on a trail designated by the commissioner and approved by the unit of government having jurisdiction over the right-of-way.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not operate a vehicle off-road within a public road right-of-way between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise, except on the right-hand side of the right-of-way and in the same direction as traffic on the nearest lane of the road.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not operate an off-road vehicle within the right-of-way of an interstate highway.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Crossing public road rights-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An off-road vehicle not registered under chapter 168 may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way for the purpose of continuing on a designated off-road trail if:</p>
          <p>(1) the crossing is made at an angle of approximately 90 degrees to the direction of the road and at a place where no obstruction prevents a quick and safe crossing;</p>
          <p>(2) the vehicle is brought to a complete stop before crossing the shoulder or main-traveled way of the road;</p>
          <p>(3) the driver yields the right-of-way to all traffic;</p>
          <p>(4) in crossing a divided road, the crossing is made only at an intersection of the road with another public road; and</p>
          <p>(5) if the crossing is made between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise or in conditions of reduced visibility, only if both front and rear lights are on.</p>
          <p>(b) An off-road vehicle not registered under chapter 168 may be operated on a bridge, other than a bridge that is part of the main-traveled lanes of an interstate highway, or a roadway shoulder or inside bank of a public road right-of-way when required to avoid obstructions to travel and no other method of avoidance is possible, provided that the vehicle is operated in the farthest right-hand lane, the entrance to the roadway is made within 100 feet of the bridge or obstacle, and the crossing is made without undue delay.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not operate an off-road vehicle on a public street or highway unless the off-road vehicle is equipped with at least one headlight and one taillight, each of minimum candlepower as prescribed by rules of the commissioner, and with brakes conforming to standards prescribed by rule of the commissioner, and all of which are subject to the approval of the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(d) Chapter 169 applies to the operation of off-road vehicles on streets and highways, except that those provisions that by their nature have no application and those provisions relating to required equipment do not apply to vehicles not registered under chapter 168. Chapter 169A applies to the operation of off-road vehicles anywhere in the state and on the ice of boundary waters.</p>
          <p>(e) A road authority, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>, may, with the approval of the commissioner, designate access trails on public road rights-of-way for gaining access to established off-road vehicle trails.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Operation generally.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not drive or operate a vehicle off-road:</p>
          <p>(1) at a rate of speed greater than is reasonable under the surrounding circumstances;</p>
          <p>(2) in a careless, reckless, or negligent manner which may endanger or cause injury or damage to the person or property of another;</p>
          <p>(3) without a functioning stoplight if so equipped;</p>
          <p>(4) in a tree nursery or planting in a manner that damages or destroys growing stock;</p>
          <p>(5) without a brake operational by either hand or foot; or</p>
          <p>(6) in a manner that violates rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Operation prohibited on airports.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for a person to drive or operate an off-road vehicle on an airport, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/360.013#stat.360.013.39">360.013, subdivision 39</a>, except in connection with the operation of the airport.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Organized contests.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Nothing in this section or chapter 169 prohibits the use of vehicles off-road within the right-of-way of a state trunk or county state-aid highway or on public lands or waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner in an organized contest or event, subject to the consent of the official or board having jurisdiction over the highway or public lands or waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In permitting the contest or event, the official or board having jurisdiction must obtain the commissioner's approval and may prescribe restrictions or conditions it considers advisable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.804.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Regulation by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subject to paragraphs (b) and (c), a county, city, or town acting through its governing body may regulate the operation of off-road vehicles on public lands, waters, and property under its jurisdiction, other than public road rights-of-way within its boundaries, by ordinance of the governing body and by giving appropriate notice.</p>
          <p>(b) The ordinance must be consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a> and rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.80">84.80</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) An ordinance may not impose a fee for the use of public land or water under the jurisdiction of the Department of Natural Resources or another agency of the state, or for the use of an access to the public land or water owned by the state, a county, or a city.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.804.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/311/#laws.2.8.0">1993 c 311 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8045">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8045 RESTRICTIONS ON OFF-ROAD VEHICLE TRAILS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Notwithstanding any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.804">84.804</a> or other law to the contrary, the commissioner shall not permit land administered by the commissioner in Cass, Crow Wing, and Hubbard Counties to be used or developed for trails primarily for off-road vehicles as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797#stat.84.797.7">84.797, subdivision 7</a>, except:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) upon approval by the legislature; or</p>
        <p class="in">(2) in designated off-road vehicle use areas.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8045.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.26.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.17.0">2010 c 382 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.805"><b>84.805</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.52.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 52</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SNOWMOBILES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.81">
        <h1 class="shn">84.81 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of Laws 1967, chapter 876, the terms defined herein shall have the meanings ascribed to them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" includes an individual, partnership, corporation, the state and its agencies and subdivisions, and any body of persons, whether incorporated or not.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Snowmobile.</span></h2>
          <p>"Snowmobile" means a self-propelled vehicle originally manufactured and designed for travel on snow or ice steered by skis or runners. Snowmobile does not include the following vehicles equipped with aftermarket ski and track configurations:</p>
          <p>(1) an all-terrain vehicle defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) an off-highway motorcycle defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.787">84.787</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) an off-road vehicle defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.797">84.797</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) a mini truck defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011">169.011</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) a utility task vehicle described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.045">169.045</a>; or</p>
          <p>(6) any other vehicle being operated off road.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person, other than a lienholder, having the property in or title to a snowmobile entitled to the use or possession thereof.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Operate.</span></h2>
          <p>"Operate" means to ride in or on and control the operation of a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Operator.</span></h2>
          <p>"Operator" means every person who operates or is in actual physical control of a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Register.</span></h2>
          <p>"Register" means the act of assigning a registration number to a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources acting directly or through the commissioner's authorized agent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Roadway.</span></h2>
          <p>"Roadway" means that portion of a highway improved, designed, or ordinarily used for vehicular travel.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Dealer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Dealer" means a person, partnership, or corporation engaged in the business of selling snowmobiles at wholesale or retail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Manufacturer" means a person, partnership, or corporation engaged in the business of manufacturing snowmobiles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Collector snowmobile.</span></h2>
          <p>"Collector snowmobile" means a snowmobile that is 25 years old or older, was originally produced as a separate identifiable make by a manufacturer, and is owned and operated solely as a collector's item.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.81.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Metal traction device.</span></h2>
          <p>"Metal traction device" means any metal device or array of metal devices attached to a snowmobile track to enhance traction that is:</p>
          <p>(1) made of metal, except that metal cleats affixed perpendicular to the direction of travel of a snowmobile track which was manufactured in 1981 or earlier shall not be considered a metal traction device; or</p>
          <p>(2) affixed to a snowmobile track with metal components that extend more than one-fourth inch from the bottom of the track.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.3.0">1967 c 876 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 695 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 577 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.5.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.86.0">1999 c 231 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.8.0">2014 c 289 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.82">
        <h1 class="shn">84.82 EXEMPTIONS; COLLECTOR SNOWMOBILES; UNLIMITED USE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.155.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 155</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">General requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not operate a snowmobile unless the snowmobile has been registered under this section. A person may not sell a snowmobile without furnishing the buyer a bill of sale on a form prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application, issuance, issuing fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Application for registration or reregistration shall be made to the commissioner or an authorized deputy registrar of motor vehicles in a format prescribed by the commissioner and shall state the legal name and address of every owner of the snowmobile.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person who purchases a snowmobile from a retail dealer shall make application for registration to the dealer at the point of sale. The dealer shall issue a dealer temporary 21-day registration permit to each purchaser who applies to the dealer for registration. The temporary permit must contain the dealer's identification number and phone number. Each retail dealer shall submit completed registration and fees to the deputy registrar at least once a week. No fee may be charged by a dealer to a purchaser for providing the temporary permit.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Upon receipt of the application and the appropriate fee, the commissioner or deputy registrar shall issue to the applicant, or provide to the dealer, an assigned registration number or a commissioner or deputy registrar temporary 21-day permit. The registration number must be printed on a registration decal issued by the commissioner or a deputy registrar. Once issued, the registration decal must be affixed to the snowmobile in a clearly visible and permanent manner for enforcement purposes according to subdivision 3b. A dealer subject to paragraph (b) shall provide the registration materials or temporary permit to the purchaser within the temporary 21-day permit period. The registration is not valid unless signed by at least one owner.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Each deputy registrar of motor vehicles acting pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.33">168.33</a> shall also be a deputy registrar of snowmobiles. The commissioner of natural resources in agreement with the commissioner of public safety may prescribe the accounting and procedural requirements necessary to ensure efficient handling of registrations and registration fees. Deputy registrars shall strictly comply with these accounting and procedural requirements.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) In addition to other fees prescribed by law, an issuing fee of $4.50 is charged for each snowmobile registration renewal, duplicate or replacement registration card, and replacement decal, and an issuing fee of $7 is charged for each snowmobile registration and registration transfer issued by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a registrar or a deputy registrar and must be deposited in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.33#stat.168.33.2">168.33, subdivision 2</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the commissioner and must be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Nontrail use registration.</span></h2>
          <p>A snowmobile may be registered for nontrail use. A snowmobile registered under this subdivision may not be operated on a state or grant-in-aid snowmobile trail. The fee for a nontrail use registration of a snowmobile with an engine displacement that is greater than 125 cubic centimeters is $45 for three years. A nontrail use registration is not transferable. In addition to other penalties prescribed by law, the penalty for violation of this subdivision is immediate revocation of the nontrail use registration. The commissioner shall ensure that the registration provided for limited nontrail use is of a different color and is distinguishable from other snowmobile registration provided.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Registration fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The fee for registration of each snowmobile, other than those used for an agricultural purpose, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92#stat.84.92.1c">84.92, subdivision 1c</a>; those registered by a dealer or manufacturer pursuant to paragraph (b) or (c); or those registered under subdivision 2a shall be as follows: $105 for three years and $10 for a duplicate or transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) The total registration fee for all snowmobiles owned by a dealer and operated for demonstration or testing purposes shall be $50 per year.</p>
          <p>(c) The total registration fee for all snowmobiles owned by a manufacturer and operated for research, testing, experimentation, or demonstration purposes shall be $150 per year. Dealer and manufacturer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p>(d) The onetime fee for registration of an exempt snowmobile under subdivision 6a is $6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All snowmobile registrations, excluding temporary registration permits, required under this section expire June 30 of the year of expiration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Display of registration decal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person must not operate a snowmobile in the state or allow another to operate the person's snowmobile in the state unless the snowmobile has its unexpired registration decal affixed to each side of the snowmobile and the decals are legible.</p>
          <p>(b) The registration decal must be affixed:</p>
          <p>(1) for snowmobiles made after June 30, 1972, in the areas provided by the manufacturer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.821#stat.84.821.2">84.821, subdivision 2</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) for all other snowmobiles, on each side of the cowling on the upper half of the snowmobile.</p>
          <p>(c) When any previously affixed registration decal is destroyed or lost, a duplicate must be affixed in the same manner as provided in paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>Every owner of a snowmobile shall renew its registration in such manner as the commissioner shall prescribe, upon payment of the same registration fees provided in subdivision 3 hereof.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Snowmobiles owned by state or political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>A registration number shall be issued without the payment of a fee for snowmobiles owned by the state of Minnesota or a political subdivision thereof upon application therefor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Registration is not required under this section for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a snowmobile owned and used by the United States, an Indian tribal government, another state, or a political subdivision thereof;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a snowmobile registered in a country other than the United States temporarily used within this state;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a snowmobile that is covered by a valid license of another state and has not been within this state for more than 30 consecutive days or that is registered by an Indian tribal government to a tribal member and has not been outside the tribal reservation boundary for more than 30 consecutive days;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a snowmobile used exclusively in organized track-racing events;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a snowmobile in transit by a manufacturer, distributor, or dealer;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) a snowmobile at least 15 years old in transit by an individual for use only on land owned or leased by the individual;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) a snowmobile while being used to groom a state or grant-in-aid trail; or</p>
          <p class="in">(8) a snowmobile with an engine displacement that is 125 cubic centimeters or less and the snowmobile is not operated on a state or grant-in-aid trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Exemption; collector unlimited snowmobile use.</span></h2>
          <p>Snowmobiles may be issued an exempt registration if the machine is at least 25 years old. Exempt registration is valid from the date of issuance until ownership of the snowmobile is transferred. Exempt registrations are not transferable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Out-of-state snowmobiles.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may issue special permits to out-of-state snowmobiles from a state or country where registration is not required to operate in Minnesota for limited periods of time not to exceed 30 days in connection with organized group outings, trail rides, races, rallies and other promotional events.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Collector snowmobiles; limited use.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a special permit to a person or organization to operate a collector snowmobile without registration in parades or organized group outings, such as races, rallies, and other promotional events and for up to ten days each year for personal transportation. The commissioner may impose a reasonable restriction on a permittee and may revoke, amend, suspend, or modify a permit for cause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Registration by persons under 18 prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No person under the age of 18 years may register a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/54/#laws.0.3.0">1985 c 54 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Proof of sales tax payment; collection and refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person applying for initial registration of a snowmobile must provide a receipt, invoice, or other document to prove that:</p>
          <p>(1) the sales and use tax under chapter 297A was paid;</p>
          <p>(2) the purchase was exempt from tax under chapter 297A; or</p>
          <p>(3) the snowmobile was purchased from a retailer that is maintaining a place of business in this state as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.66#stat.297A.66.1">297A.66, subdivision 1</a>, and is a dealer.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner or authorized deputy registrars, acting as agents of the commissioner of revenue under an agreement between the commissioner and the commissioner of revenue, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.825">297A.825</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) must collect use tax from the applicant if the applicant does not provide the proof required under paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(2) are authorized to issue refunds of use tax paid to them in error.</p>
          <p>(c) Subdivision 11 does not apply to refunds under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.82.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a refund on a registration, not including any issuing fees paid under subdivision 2, paragraph (e), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), if the refund request is received within 60 days of the original registration, the registration is not used or transferred, and:</p>
          <p>(1) the snowmobile was registered incorrectly; or</p>
          <p>(2) the snowmobile was registered twice, once by the dealer and once by the customer.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.4.0">1967 c 876 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/131/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 131 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.3.0">1969 c 695 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 577 s 2</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/48/#laws.0.33.0">Ex1971 c 48 s 33</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/202/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 202 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/241/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 241 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/580/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 580 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/54/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 54 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.5.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.8.1.0">1991 c 291 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.1.1.0">1993 c 375 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.61.0">1995 c 220 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.6.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.61.0">1997 c 216 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.7.0">1997 c 226 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.44.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.7.0">2001 c 185 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.6.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 30</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.5.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.8.0">2008 c 357 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.2.7.0">2009 c 176 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.11.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.4.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.6.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.9.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.7.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.21.1.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.24.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.7.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.8.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to subdivisions 2a and 11 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, sections 8 and 9, are effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8205">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8205 SNOWMOBILE STATE TRAIL PASS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass required; fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A snowmobile that is not registered in the state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82#stat.84.82.3">84.82, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), or that is registered by a manufacturer or dealer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82#stat.84.82.3">84.82, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b) or (c), may not be operated on a state or grant-in-aid snowmobile trail unless a snowmobile state trail pass is available for inspection by a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of natural resources shall issue a pass upon application and payment of a fee. The fee is:</p>
          <p>(1) $50 for a one-year snowmobile state trail pass purchased by an individual; and</p>
          <p>(2) $15 for a one-year snowmobile state trail pass purchased by a dealer or manufacturer.</p>
          <p>(c) In addition to other penalties prescribed by law, an individual in violation of this subdivision must purchase an annual state trail pass for a fee of $70. The pass is valid from November 1 through June 30. Fees collected under this section, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund and, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, must be used for grants-in-aid, trail maintenance, grooming, and easement acquisition.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A state trail pass is not required under this section for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a snowmobile that is owned and used by the United States, an Indian tribal government, another state, or a political subdivision thereof that is exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82">84.82</a>, subdivision 6;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a collector snowmobile that is operated as provided in a special permit issued for the collector snowmobile under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.82">84.82</a>, subdivision 7a;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a person operating a snowmobile only on the portion of a trail that is owned by the person or the person's spouse, child, or parent; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a snowmobile while being used to groom a state or grant-in-aid trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.10.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may appoint agents to issue and sell state trail passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time. The commissioner may adopt additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall observe all rules adopted by the commissioner for accounting and handling of passes pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall promptly deposit and remit all money received from the sale of the passes, exclusive of the issuing fee, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Issuing passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue and sell snowmobile state trail passes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Agent's fee.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the fee for a pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee may be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes issued by the commissioner shall be deposited in the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund and retained for the operation of the electronic licensing system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8205.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Duplicate state trail passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue a duplicate pass to persons whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405#stat.97A.405.3">97A.405, subdivision 3</a>, and rules promulgated thereunder. The fee for a duplicate state trail pass is $2, with an issuing fee of 50 cents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.62.0">1997 c 216 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.21.0">1998 c 401 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.87.0">1999 c 231 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.7.0">2004 c 255 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.32.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 32</a>-35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.1.3.0">2006 c 281 art 1 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.6.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.10.0">2008 c 357 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.14.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.7.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.8.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.9.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.10.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to this section by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 10, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.821">
        <h1 class="shn">84.821 REQUIREMENTS OF SNOWMOBILE MAKERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Identification number.</span></h2>
          <p>All snowmobiles made after June 30, 1972, and sold in Minnesota, shall bear the maker's permanent identification number stamped in letters and numbers in the form and at a location prescribed by rule of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
