        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Area for registration number.</span></h2>
          <p>All snowmobiles made after June 30, 1972, and sold in Minnesota, shall be designed and made to provide an area on which to affix the registration decal. A clear area must be provided on each side of the cowling with a minimum size of 3-1/2 square inches and at least 12 inches from the ground when the machine is resting on a hard surface.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.6.0">1971 c 577 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.9.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.83">
        <h1 class="shn">84.83 SNOWMOBILE TRAILS AND ENFORCEMENT ACCOUNT; RECEIPTS AND ALLOCATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is created in the state treasury an account known as the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Money deposited in account.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees from the registration of snowmobiles and from the issuance of snowmobile state trail passes and the unrefunded gasoline tax attributable to snowmobile use pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.18">296A.18</a> shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the snowmobile trails and enforcement account.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Purposes; allocation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The money deposited in the account and interest earned on that money may be expended only as appropriated by law for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) for a grant-in-aid program to counties and municipalities for construction and maintenance of snowmobile trails that are determined by the commissioner to be part of the state's grant-in-aid system, including maintenance of trails on lands and waters of Voyageurs National Park; on Lake of the Woods; on Rainy Lake; on the following lakes in St. Louis County: Burntside, Crane, Little Long, Mud, Pelican, Shagawa, and Vermilion; and on the following lakes in Cook County: Devil Track and Hungry Jack. The commissioner may establish a performance-based funding formula for annual grants-in-aid. The procedures and criteria for grants-in-aid are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. In administering the performance-based grants-in-aid, the commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(i) determine annual grant amounts based on a funding formula that includes consideration of historical costs, snowfall, use, and tourism;</p>
          <p>(ii) make grant payments based on:</p>
          <p>(A) successful completion of performance benchmarks;</p>
          <p>(B) reimbursement of eligible expenditures; or</p>
          <p>(C) a combination of subitems (A) and (B); and</p>
          <p>(iii) assess penalties to nonperforming grant-in-aid recipients, which may include withholding grant payments or making the grantee or trail system ineligible for future grant-in-aid funding;</p>
          <p>(2) to acquire, develop, and maintain state recreational snowmobile trails;</p>
          <p>(3) for snowmobile safety programs; and</p>
          <p>(4) to administer and enforce sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9011">84.9011</a> and appropriated grants to local law enforcement agencies.</p>
          <p>(b) No less than 60 percent of revenue collected from snowmobile registration and snowmobile state trail pass fees must be expended for grants-in-aid to develop, maintain, and groom trails and acquire easements.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Provisions applicable to funding recipients.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Recipients of Minnesota trail assistance program funds must be afforded the same protection and be held to the same standard of liability as a political subdivision under chapter 466 for activities associated with the administration, design, construction, maintenance, and grooming of snowmobile trails.</p>
          <p>(b) Recipients of Minnesota trail assistance program funds who maintain ice trails on public waters listed under subdivision 3, clause (1), or on waters of Voyageurs National Park are expressly immune from liability under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.03#stat.466.03.6e">466.03, subdivision 6e</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Trail continuation on lands acquired by state.</span></h2>
          <p>When the commissioner acquires lands with easements or other agreements for snowmobile trails that have received grant-in-aid financing under this section, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) continue the easements or other agreements for the snowmobile trail; or</p>
          <p>(2) develop an alternative route for the trail, including acquiring any necessary easements or other agreements for the trail right-of-way and providing funding for all expenses associated with clearing and marking the snowmobile trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.83.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Fines and forfeited bail.</span></h2>
          <p> Fines and forfeited bail collected from prosecutions of violations of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.90">84.90</a> or rules adopted thereunder must be deposited in the state treasury. Half the receipts must be credited to the general fund, and half the receipts must be credited to the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.83.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.5.0">1967 c 876 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.5.0">1969 c 695 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/580/#laws.0.3.0">1982 c 580 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.97.0">1987 c 404 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.17.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/573/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 573 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/230/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 230 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 1</a>,67; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/299/#laws.0.30.0">1998 c 299 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.88.0">1999 c 231 s 88</a>,89; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/165/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 165 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.8.0">2001 c 185 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.79.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.8.0">2004 c 255 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.36.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 36</a>,37; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.7.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.8.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.18.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.3.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.11.0">2023 c 25 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.11.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 11</a>,12</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to subdivisions 2 and 3 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, sections 11 and 12, are effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.84">
        <h1 class="shn">84.84 TRANSFER OR TERMINATION OF SNOWMOBILE OWNERSHIP.</h1>
        <p>(a) Within 15 days after the transfer of ownership, or any part thereof, other than a security interest, or the destruction or abandonment of any snowmobile, written notice of the transfer or destruction or abandonment shall be given to the commissioner in such form as the commissioner shall prescribe.</p>
        <p>(b) An application for transfer must be executed by the current owner and the purchaser using a bill of sale that includes the vehicle serial number.</p>
        <p>(c) The purchaser is subject to the penalties imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a> if the purchaser fails to apply for transfer of ownership as provided under this subdivision. Every owner or part owner of a snowmobile shall, upon failure to give notice of destruction or abandonment, be subject to the penalties imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.84.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.6.0">1967 c 876 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.11.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.10.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.85">
        <h1 class="shn">84.85 LICENSING BY POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS.</h1>
        <p>No political subdivision of this state shall require licensing or registration of snowmobiles covered by the provisions of Laws 1967, chapter 876.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.85.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.7.0">1967 c 876 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.86">
        <h1 class="shn">84.86 RULES; ACCIDENT REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.86.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Required rules, fees, and reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) With a view of achieving maximum use of snowmobiles consistent with protection of the environment the commissioner of natural resources shall adopt rules in the manner provided by chapter 14, for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) registration of snowmobiles;</p>
          <p>(2) use of snowmobiles insofar as game and fish resources are affected;</p>
          <p>(3) use of snowmobiles on public lands and waters, or on grant-in-aid trails;</p>
          <p>(4) uniform signs to be used by the state, counties, and cities, which are necessary or desirable to control, direct, or regulate the operation and use of snowmobiles;</p>
          <p>(5) specifications relating to snowmobile mufflers; and</p>
          <p>(6) a comprehensive snowmobile information and safety education and training program that includes but is not limited to preparing and disseminating snowmobile information and safety advice to the public, training snowmobile operators, and issuing snowmobile safety certificates to snowmobile operators who successfully complete the snowmobile safety education and training course.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purpose of administering the program under paragraph (a), clause (6), and to defray expenses of training and certifying snowmobile operators, the commissioner shall collect a fee from each person who receives the youth or adult training. The commissioner shall collect a fee, to include a $1 issuing fee for licensing agents, for issuing a duplicate snowmobile safety certificate. The commissioner shall establish both fees in a manner that neither significantly overrecovers nor underrecovers costs, including overhead costs, involved in providing the services. The fees are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The fees may be established by the commissioner notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>. The fees, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents under this subdivision, shall be deposited in the snowmobile trails and enforcement account in the natural resources fund and the amount thereof, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, and issuing fees collected by the commissioner, is appropriated annually to the Enforcement Division of the Department of Natural Resources for administering the programs. In addition to the fee established by the commissioner, instructors may charge each person any fee paid by the instructor for the person's online training course and up to the established fee amount for class materials and expenses. The commissioner shall cooperate with private organizations and associations, private and public corporations, and local governmental units in furtherance of the program established under paragraph (a), clause (6). School districts may cooperate with the commissioner and volunteer instructors to provide space for the classroom portion of the training. The commissioner shall consult with the commissioner of public safety in regard to training program subject matter and performance testing that leads to the certification of snowmobile operators.</p>
          <p>(c) The operator of any snowmobile involved in an accident resulting in injury requiring medical attention or hospitalization to or death of any person or total damage to an extent of $500 or more, shall forward a written report of the accident to the commissioner on a form prescribed by the commissioner. If the operator is killed or is unable to file a report due to incapacitation, any peace officer investigating the accident shall file the accident report within ten business days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.86.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Public safety rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety may adopt rules not inconsistent herewith in the manner provided by chapter 14, regulating the use of snowmobiles on streets and highways.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.86.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.8.0">1967 c 876 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.6.0">1969 c 695 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/491/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 491 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.7.0">1971 c 577 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/594/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 594 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.194.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 194</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/184/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 184 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.63.0">1997 c 216 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.22.0">1998 c 401 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.11.0">2004 c 221 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.9.0">2004 c 255 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.38.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.19.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.11.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.862">
        <h1 class="shn">84.862 SNOWMOBILE TRAINING REQUIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.862.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Youth and adult safety training.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any resident born after December 31, 1976, who operates a snowmobile in Minnesota, must possess:</p>
          <p>(1) a valid snowmobile safety certificate;</p>
          <p>(2) a driver's license that has a valid snowmobile qualification indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.12">171.07, subdivision 12</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) an identification card that has a valid snowmobile qualification indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.12">171.07, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For youth or adults taking the youth course, the certificate or qualification indicator may only be issued upon successful completion of a course authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.86">84.86</a>. Either the youth course under this paragraph or the adult course under paragraph (c) may be completed by persons 16 years of age or older.</p>
          <p>(c) Persons 16 years of age or older may take the adult snowmobile safety training course. The certificate or qualification indicator may only be issued upon successful completion of a safety course designed for adults or persons 16 years of age or older.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.862.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.51.0">2004 c 255 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.862.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Certificates issued in other states.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person completes a safety course in another state that is recognized by the commissioner under a reciprocity agreement or certified by the commissioner as substantially similar to requirements in this state, evidence that the person has completed that course is acceptable in lieu of a certificate under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.862.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Training for offenders.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who is convicted for a second or subsequent speeding violation in a snowmobile season, or any conviction for careless or reckless operation of a snowmobile, must successfully complete a training course in subdivision 1 before continuing operation of a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.862.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/216/#laws.0.64.0">1997 c 216 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.91.0">1999 c 231 s 91</a>,92; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.10.0">2001 c 185 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.7.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.10.0">2004 c 255 s 10</a>-12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.87">
        <h1 class="shn">84.87 OPERATION; REGULATIONS BY POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Operation on streets and highways.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No person shall operate a snowmobile upon the roadway, shoulder, or inside bank or slope of any trunk, county state-aid, or county highway in this state and, in the case of a divided trunk or county highway, on the right-of-way between the opposing lanes of traffic, except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.90">84.90</a>. No person shall operate a snowmobile within the right-of-way of any trunk, county state-aid, or county highway between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise, except on the right-hand side of such right-of-way and in the same direction as the highway traffic on the nearest lane of the roadway adjacent thereto. No snowmobile shall be operated at any time within the right-of-way of any interstate highway or freeway within this state.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any provision of paragraph (a) to the contrary:</p>
          <p>(1) under conditions prescribed by the commissioner of transportation, the commissioner of transportation may allow two-way operation of snowmobiles on either side of the trunk highway right-of-way where the commissioner of transportation determines that two-way operation will not endanger users of the trunk highway or riders of the snowmobiles using the trail;</p>
          <p>(2) under conditions prescribed by a local road authority as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>, the road authority may allow two-way operation of snowmobiles on either side of the right-of-way of a street or highway under the road authority's jurisdiction, where the road authority determines that two-way operation will not endanger users of the street or highway or riders of the snowmobiles using the trail;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner of transportation under clause (1) and the local road authority under clause (2) shall notify the commissioner of natural resources and the local law enforcement agencies responsible for the streets or highways of the locations of two-way snowmobile trails authorized under this paragraph; and</p>
          <p>(4) two-way snowmobile trails authorized under this paragraph shall be posted for two-way operation at the authorized locations.</p>
          <p>(c) A snowmobile may make a direct crossing of a street or highway at any hour of the day provided:</p>
          <p>(1) the crossing is made at an angle of approximately 90 degrees to the direction of the highway and at a place where no obstruction prevents a quick and safe crossing;</p>
          <p>(2) the snowmobile is brought to a complete stop before crossing the shoulder or main traveled way of the highway;</p>
          <p>(3) the driver yields the right-of-way to all oncoming traffic which constitutes an immediate hazard;</p>
          <p>(4) in crossing a divided highway, the crossing is made only at an intersection of such highway with another public street or highway or at a safe location approved by the road authority;</p>
          <p>(5) if the crossing is made between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise or in conditions of reduced visibility, only if both front and rear lights are on; and</p>
          <p>(6) a snowmobile may be operated upon a bridge, other than a bridge that is part of the main traveled lanes of an interstate highway, when required for the purpose of avoiding obstructions to travel when no other method of avoidance is possible; provided the snowmobile is operated in the extreme right-hand lane, the entrance to the roadway is made within 100 feet of the bridge and the crossing is made without undue delay.</p>
          <p>(d) No snowmobile shall be operated upon a public street or highway unless it is equipped with at least one headlamp, one tail lamp, each of minimum candlepower as prescribed by rules of the commissioner, reflector material of a minimum area of 16 square inches mounted on each side forward of the handle bars, and with brakes each of which shall conform to standards prescribed by rule of the commissioner pursuant to the authority vested in the commissioner by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.86">84.86</a>, and each of which shall be subject to approval of the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(e) A snowmobile may be operated upon a public street or highway other than as provided by paragraph (c) in an emergency during the period of time when and at locations where snow upon the roadway renders travel by automobile impractical.</p>
          <p>(f) All provisions of chapters 169 and 169A shall apply to the operation of snowmobiles upon streets and highways, except for those relating to required equipment, and except those which by their nature have no application. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.09">169.09</a> applies to the operation of snowmobiles anywhere in the state or on the ice of any boundary water of the state.</p>
          <p>(g) Any sled, trailer, or other device being towed by a snowmobile must be equipped with reflective materials as required by rule of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Organized contests.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Nothing in this section or chapter 169 shall prohibit the use of snowmobiles within the right-of-way of any state trunk or county state-aid highway or upon public lands or waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner of natural resources, in any organized contest, subject to the consent of the official or board having jurisdiction over the highway or public lands or waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In permitting such contest, the official or board having jurisdiction may prescribe such restrictions or conditions as they may deem advisable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Operation generally.</span></h2>
          <p>It shall be unlawful for any person to drive or operate any snowmobile in the following unsafe or harassing ways:</p>
          <p>(1) at a rate of speed greater than reasonable or proper under all the surrounding circumstances;</p>
          <p>(2) in a careless, reckless or negligent manner so as to endanger the person or property of another or to cause injury or damage thereto;</p>
          <p>(3) without a lighted head and taillight when required for safety; or</p>
          <p>(4) in any tree nursery or planting in a manner which damages or destroys growing stock.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Operation prohibited on airports.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate any snowmobile on an airport defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/360.013#stat.360.013.39">360.013, subdivision 39</a>, or other applicable law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/615/#laws.0.28.0">1994 c 615 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Application of speed limits to testing activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A speed limit established by the commissioner in rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.86">84.86</a> does not apply to a snowmobile that is being operated as part of a testing program established by a snowmobile manufacturer if:</p>
          <p>(1) the snowmobile is operated for testing purposes by a driver employed by the snowmobile manufacturer;</p>
          <p>(2) the snowmobile is clearly marked as a test machine; and</p>
          <p>(3) the snowmobile is operated in compliance with all other applicable laws and rules.</p>
          <p>(b) A card containing a photograph of the driver and identifying the driver as a test driver for the manufacturer must be in the driver's possession at all times when the snowmobile is being operated at a speed in excess of the limit established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.86">84.86</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Regulation by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding anything in this section to the contrary, a county board may by resolution permit the operation of snowmobiles upon the roadway, shoulder, or inside bank or slope of any county highway or county state-aid highway if safe operation in the ditch or outside bank or slope thereof is impossible, in which case the county board shall cause appropriate notice thereof to be given.</p>
          <p>(b) Any county, city, or any town acting by its town board, may regulate the operation of snowmobiles on public lands, waters, and property under their jurisdiction and on streets and highways within their boundaries by resolution or ordinance of the governing body and by giving appropriate notice, provided such regulations are not inconsistent with the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a> inclusive and rules promulgated thereunder. However, no such governmental unit may adopt an ordinance which (1) imposes a fee for the use of public land or water under the jurisdiction of either the commissioner of natural resources or any other agency of the state, or for the use of any access thereto owned by the state, or a county or city; or (2) requires a snowmobile operator to possess a motor vehicle driver's license while operating a snowmobile.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Snowmobile speed limit; lakes greater than 10,000 acres.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law or rule to the contrary, a county may enact an ordinance to raise the speed limit up to 65 miles per hour for snowmobiles traveling on marked trails during daylight hours on lakes greater than 10,000 acres, subject to the approval of the commissioner of natural resources. Within any posted speed limit, it remains unlawful for any person to drive or operate any snowmobile at a rate of speed greater than is reasonable or proper under all of the surrounding conditions or circumstances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.87.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Snowmobile operation during firearms deer season.</span></h2>
          <p>Snowmobile operation during the firearms deer-hunting season is restricted as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777#stat.84.777.2">84.777, subdivision 2</a>, and rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.9.0">1967 c 876 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.7.0">1969 c 695 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/491/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 491 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.8.0">1971 c 577 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/701/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 701 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/51/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 51 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/239/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 239 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.48.0">1976 c 2 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/31/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 31 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/363/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 363 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/368/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 368 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 331 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/573/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 573 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.8.0">1997 c 226 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/355/#laws.0.5.0">2002 c 355 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/244/#laws.0.1.0">2004 c 244 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/72/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 72 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 290 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.18.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.12.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.871">
        <h1 class="shn">84.871 MUFFLER REQUIREMENTS; PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.871.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Mufflers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided under paragraph (c), a person may not operate a snowmobile unless:</p>
          <p>(1) the snowmobile is equipped with a muffler meeting the requirements of rules adopted by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) the snowmobile is equipped at all times with a muffler in good working order that blends the exhaust noise into the overall snowmobile noise and is in constant operation to prevent excessive or unusual noise.</p>
          <p>(b) A snowmobile operated, offered for sale, or sold in this state must have an exhaust system that does not emit or produce a sharp popping or crackling sound.</p>
          <p>(c) This section does not apply to organized races or similar competitive events held on:</p>
          <p>(1) private lands, with the permission of the owner, lessee, or custodian of the land;</p>
          <p>(2) public lands and water under the jurisdiction of the commissioner of natural resources, with the commissioner's permission; or</p>
          <p>(3) other public lands, with the consent of the public agency owning the land.</p>
          <p>(d) No person shall have for sale, sell, or offer for sale on any new snowmobile any muffler that fails to comply with the specifications required by the rules of the commissioner after the effective date of the rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.871.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/4/#laws.0.5.0">1999 c 4 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.871.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certification.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning July 1, 2026, all after-market mufflers installed on a snowmobile must have a permanent stamp, clearly visible on the muffler, certified by the muffler manufacturer and stating that the muffler conforms to the snowmobile muffler noise limits specified by the rules of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.871.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who operates a snowmobile in violation of subdivision 1, paragraph (a) or (b), is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.101#stat.609.101.4">609.101, subdivision 4</a>, clause (2), the minimum fine for a person who operates a snowmobile in violation of subdivision 1, paragraph (a) or (b), must not be less than:</p>
          <p>(1) $250 for the first offense;</p>
          <p>(2) $500 for the second offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) $1,000 for the third and subsequent offenses.</p>
          <p>(c) A conservation officer or other licensed peace officer may issue a civil citation to a person who operates a snowmobile in violation of subdivision 1, paragraph (a) or (b). A civil citation under this subdivision must impose a penalty of:</p>
          <p>(1) $250 for the first offense;</p>
          <p>(2) $500 for the second offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) $1,000 for the third and subsequent offenses.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.871.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.9.0">1969 c 695 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/401/#laws.0.23.0">1998 c 401 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.12.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.8712">
        <h1 class="shn">84.8712 METAL TRACTION DEVICES; PROHIBITION ON PAVED TRAILS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not use a snowmobile with metal traction devices on a paved public trail that is designated closed to such use by a local government with jurisdiction over the trail or on a paved state trail or any portion of a paved state trail that is designated closed to such use by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Civil citation; authority to issue.</span></h2>
          <p>Conservation officers and other licensed peace officers may issue civil citations to a person who operates a snowmobile in violation of this section. The citation must impose a penalty of $50 for the first offense, $200 for the second offense, and $500 for third and subsequent offenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appeals.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil citations for offenses under this section may be appealed under the procedures in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072#stat.116.072.6">116.072, subdivision 6</a>, if the recipient of the citation requests a hearing by notifying the commissioner in writing within 15 days after receipt of the citation. For the purposes of the enforcement of this section, the terms "commissioner" and "agency" as used in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072">116.072</a> mean the commissioner of natural resources. If a hearing is not requested within the 15-day period, the citation becomes a final order not subject to further review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Civil citations for offenses under this section may be enforced under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116.072#stat.116.072.9">116.072, subdivision 9</a>. If a person fails to pay a penalty owed under this section, the person may not operate a snowmobile until the penalty is paid. Penalty amounts must be remitted within 30 days of issuance of the penalty citation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Allocation of penalty amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Penalty amounts collected from civil citations issued under this section are deposited to the unit of government employing the officer that issues the civil citation. The commissioner must deposit penalty amounts received by the state in the snowmobile trails and enforcement account established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.83#stat.84.83.1">84.83, subdivision 1</a>. The penalty amounts in the account must be dedicated for the repair of paved public trails.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.8712.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Selecting remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>A person operating a snowmobile in violation of this section is guilty of a petty misdemeanor punishable by a fine of no more than $50 for the first offense, no more than $300 for the second offense, and no more than $600 for the third and subsequent offenses. A peace officer may not seek both civil and petty misdemeanor penalties for a violation of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.8712.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/4/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.93.0">1999 c 231 s 93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/324/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 324 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.12.0">2004 c 221 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.17.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 17</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/277/#laws.1.10.0">2012 c 277 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.8713"><b>84.8713</b> MS 2002 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.94.0">1999 c 231 s 94</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.8715"><b>84.8715</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/4/#laws.0.5.0">1999 c 4 s 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.872">
        <h1 class="shn">84.872 YOUTHFUL SNOWMOBILE OPERATORS; PROHIBITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.872.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Restrictions on operation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding anything in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.87">84.87</a> to the contrary, no person under 14 years of age shall make a direct crossing of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway as the operator of a snowmobile, or operate a snowmobile upon a street or highway within a municipality. A person 14 years of age or older, but less than 18 years of age, may make a direct crossing of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway only if the person has in immediate possession:</p>
          <p>(1) a valid snowmobile safety certificate issued by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) a driver's license that has a valid snowmobile qualification indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.12">171.07, subdivision 12</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) an identification card that has a valid snowmobile qualification indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.12">171.07, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.862">84.862</a>, no person under the age of 14 years shall operate a snowmobile unless supervised by or accompanied by one of the following listed persons on the same or an accompanying snowmobile, or on a device towed by the same or an accompanying snowmobile: the person's parent, legal guardian, or other person 18 years of age or older designated by the parent or guardian. However, a person 12 years of age or older but under the age of 14 years may operate a snowmobile if the person has in immediate possession a valid snowmobile safety certificate issued by the commissioner or an identification card with a valid snowmobile qualification indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.12">171.07, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The snowmobile safety certificate exceptions under paragraph (b) do not allow a person under the age of 14 years to make a direct crossing of a highway as the operator of a snowmobile or to operate a snowmobile upon a street or highway, as prohibited under paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.872.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Helmet required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No person under the age of 18 shall operate or ride a snowmobile in this state without wearing protective headgear that complies with standards established by the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(b) The provisions of this subdivision shall not apply to persons during their participation in a parade that has been granted a permit or other official authorization by a local unit of government or to a person operating a snowmobile on land that is owned by the person or the person's parents, grandparents, siblings, uncles, or aunts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.872.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Owner's duties.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for any person who is the owner or in lawful control of a snowmobile to permit the snowmobile to be operated contrary to the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.872.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting convictions; suspensions.</span></h2>
          <p>When the judge of a juvenile court, or any of its duly authorized agents, shall determine that any person, while less than 18 years of age, has violated the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a>, or any other state or local law or ordinance regulating the operation of snowmobiles, the judge, or duly authorized agent, shall immediately report this determination to the commissioner and may recommend the suspension of the person's snowmobile safety certificate. The commissioner is hereby authorized to suspend the certificate, without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.872.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/695/#laws.0.10.0">1969 c 695 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.9.0">1971 c 577 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/89/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 89 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/184/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 184 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/623/#laws.1.9.0">1994 c 623 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.2.20.0">1994 c 632 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/226/#laws.0.9.0">1997 c 226 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/231/#laws.0.95.0">1999 c 231 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.12.0">2001 c 185 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.13.0">2004 c 255 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.873"><b>84.873</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.88">
        <h1 class="shn">84.88 PENALTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.88.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who shall violate any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.89">84.89</a> or any rule of the commissioner of natural resources or of the commissioner of public safety promulgated pursuant to law shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.88.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Owner's penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person registered as owner of a snowmobile may be fined not to exceed $300 if a snowmobile bearing the person's registration number is operated contrary to the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.091">97B.091</a>. The registered owner may not be so fined if (a) the snowmobile was reported as stolen to the commissioner or a law enforcement agency at the time of the alleged unlawful act, or if (b) the registered owner demonstrates that the snowmobile either was stolen or was not in use at the time of the alleged unlawful act, or if (c) the registered owner furnishes to law enforcement officers upon request the identity of the person in actual physical control of the snowmobile at the time of such violation. The provisions of this subdivision do not apply to any person who rents or leases a snowmobile if such person keeps a record of the name and address of the person or persons renting or leasing such snowmobile, the registration number thereof, the departure date and time, and expected time of return thereof. Such record shall be preserved for at least six months and shall be prima facie evidence that the person named therein was the operator thereof at the time it was operated contrary to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.88">84.88</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97B.091">97B.091</a>. The provisions of this subdivision do not prohibit or limit the prosecution of a snowmobile operator for violating any of the sections referred to in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.88.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/876/#laws.0.10.0">1967 c 876 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.10.2.0">1969 c 1129 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/23/#laws.0.10.0">1971 c 23 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/491/#laws.0.3.0">1971 c 491 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/577/#laws.0.10.0">1971 c 577 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/701/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 701 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.14.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 14</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.89">
        <h1 class="shn">84.89 CONFISCATING SNOWMOBILE USED IN BURGLARY.</h1>
        <p>A law enforcement officer shall seize any snowmobile, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81">84.81</a>, used for the purpose of gaining access to property for the purpose of committing the crime of burglary, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.582">609.582</a>. Any snowmobile seized pursuant to this section shall be held, subject to the order of the district court of the county in which the burglary was committed, and shall be confiscated after conviction of the person from whom the snowmobile was seized and disposed of in accordance with the procedure provided for equipment used in committing game and fish violations by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.225">97A.225</a>, except that the balance of the proceeds from the sale of a confiscated snowmobile which are paid into the state treasury shall be credited to the general fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.89.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/176/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 176 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/386/#laws.4.15.0">1986 c 386 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.1.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.90">
        <h1 class="shn">84.90 OPERATING LIMITATIONS; RECREATIONAL MOTOR VEHICLES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p>(1) "Recreational motor vehicle" means any self-propelled vehicle and any vehicle propelled or drawn by a self-propelled vehicle used for recreational purposes, including but not limited to snowmobile, trail bike or other all-terrain vehicle, hovercraft, or motor vehicle licensed for highway operation which is being used for off-road recreational purposes.</p>
          <p>(2) "Snowmobile" has the same meaning given by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.81#stat.84.81.3">84.81, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Within metropolitan area.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the seven-county metropolitan area, no person shall enter and operate a recreational motor vehicle on lands not owned by the person, except where otherwise allowed by law, without the written or oral permission of the owner, occupant, or lessee of such lands. Written permission may be given by a posted notice of any kind or description that the owner, occupant, or lessee prefers, so long as it specifies the kind of vehicles allowed, such as by saying "Recreational Vehicles Allowed," "Snowmobiles Allowed," "Trail Bikes Allowed," "All-Terrain Vehicles Allowed," or words substantially similar.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Outside metropolitan area.</span></h2>
          <p>Outside the seven-county metropolitan area, no person shall enter on any land not owned by the person for the purpose of operating a recreational motor vehicle after being notified, either orally or by written or posted notice, by the owner, occupant, or lessee not to do so. Where posted notice is used, signs shall bear letters not less than two inches high and shall state one of the following: "Recreational Vehicles Prohibited," "Snowmobiles Prohibited," "Trail Bikes Prohibited," "All-Terrain Vehicles Prohibited," or words substantially similar. In lieu of the above notice an owner, occupant or lessee may post any sign prohibiting recreational motor vehicles which has been adopted by rule of the commissioner of natural resources. The notice or sign shall be posted at corners and ordinary ingress and egress to the property and when so posted shall serve so as to raise a conclusive presumption that a person operating a recreational motor vehicle thereon had knowledge of entering upon such posted lands. Failure to post notice as provided in this subdivision shall not deprive a person of the right to bring a civil action for damage to one's person or property as otherwise provided by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Posting; trail facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for a person to post, mutilate, or remove any notice or sign provided in this section upon any lands or waters over which the person has no right, title, interest, or license. It is unlawful for a person other than a duly constituted legal authority to so post any public lands, including but not limited to tax-forfeited lands, as above described. It is unlawful for a person to mutilate, destroy, damage, or remove any shelter, comfort station or other trail facility on any trail established on state-owned land or on any recreational trail which is funded in whole or in part by state grant-in-aid funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Gates; fencing.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall enter or leave the lands of another with a recreational motor vehicle, or pass from one portion of such lands to another portion, through a closed gate, without returning the gate to its original position. No person shall enter or leave the lands of another with a recreational motor vehicle by cutting any wire or tearing down or destroying any fence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Additional prohibitions.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall limit or otherwise qualify the power of municipalities, counties, school districts, or other political subdivisions of the state or any agency of the state to impose additional restrictions or prohibitions on the operation of recreational motor vehicles on property not owned by the operator in accordance with law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.90.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person violating the provisions of this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.101#stat.609.101.4">609.101, subdivision 4</a>, clause (2), the minimum fine for a person who operates an off-highway motorcycle, off-road vehicle, all-terrain vehicle, or snowmobile in violation of this section must not be less than the amount set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.775">84.775</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/468/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 468 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/215/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 215 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.13.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.901"><b>84.901</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.162.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 162</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9011">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9011 OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE SAFETY AND CONSERVATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9011.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of natural resources shall establish a program to promote the safe and responsible operation of off-highway vehicles in a manner that does not harm the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9011.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agreements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall enter into agreements with organizations for volunteer services that promote the safe and responsible operation of off-highway vehicles in a manner that does not harm the environment to maintain, make improvements to, and monitor trails on state forest land and other public lands. The organizations shall promote the operation of off-highway vehicles in a safe and responsible manner that complies with the laws and rules that relate to the operation of off-highway vehicles.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The organizations may provide assistance to the department in locating, recruiting, and training instructors for off-highway vehicle training programs.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner may provide assistance to enhance the comfort and safety of volunteers and to facilitate the implementation and administration of the safety and conservation program.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall establish standards, train, and certify organizations and individuals participating as volunteers under this section. The training shall include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the identification of invasive species;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) correctly reporting the location of invasive species; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) basic global positioning system operation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9011.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Worker displacement prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may not enter into any agreement that has the purpose of or results in the displacement of public employees by volunteers participating in the off-highway safety and conservation program under this section. The commissioner must certify to the appropriate bargaining agent that the work performed by a volunteer will not result in the displacement of currently employed workers or workers on seasonal layoff or layoff from a substantially equivalent position, including partial displacement such as reduction in hours of nonovertime work, wages, or other employment benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9011.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Off-Highway Vehicle Safety Advisory Council.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of natural resources shall appoint an Off-Highway Vehicle Safety Advisory Council to advise the commissioner on:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) off-highway vehicle safety; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) standards and certification for organizations and individuals participating as volunteers under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.27.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 27</a>
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.84.911">
        <h1 class="shn">84.911 REPORTS OF DEATH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.911.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Coroner to report death.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Every coroner or medical examiner shall report in writing to the Department of Natural Resources the death of any person within the jurisdiction of the coroner or medical examiner as the result of an accident involving an off-road recreational vehicle, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.03#stat.169A.03.16">169A.03, subdivision 16</a>, and the circumstances of the accident. The report shall be made within 15 days after the death.</p>
          <p>(b) In the case of drivers killed in off-road recreational vehicle accidents and of the death of passengers 14 years of age or older, who die within four hours after accident, the coroner or medical examiner shall examine the body and shall make tests as are necessary to determine the presence and percentage concentration of alcohol, and drugs if feasible, in the blood of the victim. This information shall be included in each report submitted pursuant to the provisions of this subdivision and shall be tabulated by the Department of Natural Resources. Periodically, the commissioner of natural resources must transmit a summary of the reports to the commissioner of public safety.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.911.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/368/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 368 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.13.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/570/#laws.2.2.0">1992 c 570 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/615/#laws.0.3.0">1994 c 615 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/185/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 185 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.3.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.912"><b>84.912</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.915">
        <h1 class="shn">84.915 LAND USE FOR CERTAIN VEHICLES RESTRICTED.</h1>
        <p>After June 1, 1993, the commissioner may not allow the use of state lands or acquire private lands for development or operation of a motor sports area for use by all-terrain vehicles, motorcycles, or four-wheel drive trucks without legislative approval. This restriction does not apply to recreational trails.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.915.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/203/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 203 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.92">
        <h1 class="shn">84.92 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Agricultural zone.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural zone" means the areas in Minnesota lying south and west of a line starting at the Minnesota-North Dakota border and formed by rights-of-way of Trunk Highway No. 10, thence easterly along Trunk Highway No. 10 to Trunk Highway No. 23, thence easterly along Trunk Highway No. 23 to Trunk Highway No. 95, thence easterly along Trunk Highway No. 95 to its termination at the Minnesota-Wisconsin border.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Accompanied.</span></h2>
          <p>"Accompanied" means being subject to continuous direction or control.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Agricultural purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural purpose" means used exclusively for an agricultural use as defined in subdivision 1d.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1d.<span class="headnote">Agricultural use.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agricultural use" means use in agriculturally related activities or harvesting of wood for commercial or firewood purposes by any person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.1e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1e.<span class="headnote">City.</span></h2>
          <p>"City" means a home rule charter or statutory city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Dealer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Dealer" means a person engaged in the business of selling all-terrain vehicles at wholesale or retail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Manufacturer.</span></h2>
          <p>"Manufacturer" means a person engaged in the business of manufacturing all-terrain vehicles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Owner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Owner" means a person, other than a person with a security interest, having a property interest in or title to an all-terrain vehicle and entitled to the use and possession of the vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" means an individual or an organization as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/336.1-201">336.1-201</a> (b)(27).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Public road right-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public road right-of-way" means the entire right-of-way of a public road, including the traveled portions, banks, ditches, shoulders, and medians of a roadway, that is not privately owned.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Register.</span></h2>
          <p>"Register" means the act of assigning a registration number to an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">All-terrain vehicle or vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) "All-terrain vehicle" or "vehicle" means a motorized vehicle with: (1) not less than three, but not more than six low pressure or non-pneumatic tires; (2) a total dry weight of 2,000 pounds or less; and (3) a total width from outside of tire rim to outside of tire rim that is 65 inches or less. All-terrain vehicle includes a class 1 all-terrain vehicle and class 2 all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) All-terrain vehicle does not include an electric-assisted bicycle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011#stat.169.011.27">169.011, subdivision 27</a>, golf cart, mini-truck, dune buggy, or go-cart or a vehicle designed and used specifically for lawn maintenance, agriculture, logging, or mining purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Class 1 all-terrain vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Class 1 all-terrain vehicle" means an all-terrain vehicle that has a total width from outside of tire rim to outside of tire rim that is 50 inches or less.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.92.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Class 2 all-terrain vehicle.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Class 2 all-terrain vehicle" means an all-terrain vehicle that has a total width from outside of tire rim to outside of tire rim that is greater than 50 inches but not more than 65 inches.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 647 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 452 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.3.0">1989 c 331 s 3</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.2.1.0">1990 c 426 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/1/#laws.0.8.0">1Sp1995 c 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.28.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/162/#laws.3.4.0">2004 c 162 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.1.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.8.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 8</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.15.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.9.0">2011 c 107 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.9.0">2014 c 289 s 9</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.6.0">2014 c 290 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.12.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/5/#laws.4.5.0">1Sp2021 c 5 art 4 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.922">
        <h1 class="shn">84.922 REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless exempted in subdivision 1a, a person may not operate and an owner may not give permission for another to operate an all-terrain vehicle within the state unless the vehicle has been registered with the commissioner of natural resources, or is exempt from registration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All-terrain vehicles exempt from registration are:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) vehicles owned and used by the United States, an Indian tribal government, the state, another state, or a political subdivision;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) vehicles that are registered in another state or country and have not been in this state for more than 30 consecutive days or that are registered by an Indian tribal government to a tribal member and have not been outside the tribal reservation boundary for more than 30 consecutive days;</p>
          <p>(3) vehicles that:</p>
          <p>(i) are owned by a resident of another state or country that does not require registration of all-terrain vehicles;</p>
          <p>(ii) have not been in this state for more than 30 consecutive days; and</p>
          <p>(iii) are operated on state and grant-in-aid trails by a nonresident possessing a nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail pass;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) vehicles used exclusively in organized track-racing events; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) vehicles that are 25 years old or older and were originally produced as a separate identifiable make by a manufacturer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application, issuance, reports.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Application for registration or continued registration shall be made to the commissioner or an authorized deputy registrar of motor vehicles in a form prescribed by the commissioner. The form must state the name and address of every owner of the vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person who purchases an all-terrain vehicle from a retail dealer shall make application for registration to the dealer at the point of sale. The dealer shall issue a dealer temporary 21-day registration permit to each purchaser who applies to the dealer for registration. The dealer shall submit the completed registration application and fees to the deputy registrar at least once each week. No fee may be charged by a dealer to a purchaser for providing the temporary permit.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Upon receipt of the application and the appropriate fee, the commissioner or deputy registrar shall issue to the applicant, or provide to the dealer, an assigned registration number or a commissioner or deputy registrar temporary 21-day permit.
