Once issued, the registration number must be affixed to the vehicle in a manner prescribed by the commissioner. A dealer subject to paragraph (b) shall provide the registration materials or temporary permit to the purchaser within the 21-day temporary permit period. The commissioner shall use the snowmobile registration system to register vehicles under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Each deputy registrar of motor vehicles acting under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.33">168.33</a>, is also a deputy registrar of all-terrain vehicles. The commissioner of natural resources in agreement with the commissioner of public safety may prescribe the accounting and procedural requirements necessary to assure efficient handling of registrations and registration fees. Deputy registrars shall strictly comply with the accounting and procedural requirements.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) In addition to other fees prescribed by law, a filing fee of $4.50 is charged for each all-terrain vehicle registration renewal, duplicate or replacement registration card, and replacement decal and a filing fee of $7 is charged for each all-terrain vehicle registration and registration transfer issued by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a deputy registrar and shall be deposited in the treasury of the jurisdiction where the deputy is appointed, or retained if the deputy is not a public official; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the commissioner and shall be deposited to the state treasury and credited to the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Private use registration.</span></h2>
          <p>All-terrain vehicles may be registered for private use that are used exclusively for private or agricultural use or used exclusively on private property. Private use registration is valid from the date of issuance until ownership of the all-terrain vehicle is transferred. Private or agricultural use registrations are not transferable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Collector vehicles; exempt registration.</span></h2>
          <p>All-terrain vehicles may be issued an exempt registration if requested and the machine is at least 25 years old. Exempt registration is valid from the date of issuance until ownership of the all-terrain vehicle is transferred. Exempt registrations are not transferable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Registration card; signature.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide to the registrant a registration card that includes the registration number, the date of registration, the make and serial number of the vehicle, the owner's name and address, and additional information the commissioner may require. Information concerning each registration shall be retained by the commissioner. The registration is not valid unless signed by at least one owner. Upon a satisfactory showing that the registration card has been lost or destroyed the commissioner shall issue a replacement registration card upon payment of a fee of $4. The fees collected from replacement registration cards shall be deposited in the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report of transfers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Application for transfer of ownership must be made to the commissioner within 15 days of the date of transfer.</p>
          <p>(b) An application for transfer must be executed by the current owner and the purchaser using a bill of sale that includes the vehicle serial number.</p>
          <p>(c) The purchaser is subject to the penalties imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.774">84.774</a> if the purchaser fails to apply for transfer of ownership as provided under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The fee for a three-year registration of an all-terrain vehicle under this section, other than those registered by a dealer or manufacturer under paragraph (b) or (c), is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) for public use, $60;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) for private use, $6; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) for a duplicate or transfer, $4.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The total registration fee for all-terrain vehicles owned by a dealer and operated for demonstration or testing purposes is $50 per year. Dealer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The total registration fee for all-terrain vehicles owned by a manufacturer and operated for research, testing, experimentation, or demonstration purposes is $150 per year. Manufacturer registrations are not transferable.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The onetime fee for registration of an all-terrain vehicle under subdivision 2b is $6.</p>
          <p>(e) The fees collected under this subdivision must be credited to the all-terrain vehicle account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>Every owner of an all-terrain vehicle must renew registration in a manner prescribed by the commissioner upon payment of the registration fees in subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Vehicles owned by state or political subdivision.</span></h2>
          <p>A registration number must be issued without the payment of a fee for all-terrain vehicles owned by the state or a political subdivision upon application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 331 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Licensing by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>No political subdivision of this state shall require licensing or registration of all-terrain vehicles covered by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Registration by minors prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No person under the age of 18 may register an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Proof of sales tax payment; collection and refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person applying for initial registration in Minnesota of an all-terrain vehicle must provide a receipt, invoice, or other document to prove that:</p>
          <p>(1) the sales and use tax under chapter 297A was paid;</p>
          <p>(2) the purchase was exempt from tax under chapter 297A; or</p>
          <p>(3) the all-terrain vehicle was purchased from a retailer that is maintaining a place of business in this state as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.66#stat.297A.66.1">297A.66, subdivision 1</a>, and is a dealer.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner or authorized deputy registrars, acting as agents of the commissioner of revenue under an agreement between the commissioner and the commissioner of revenue, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.825">297A.825</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) must collect use tax from the applicant if the applicant does not provide the proof required under paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(2) are authorized to issue refunds of use tax paid to them in error.</p>
          <p>(c) Subdivision 12 does not apply to refunds under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may issue a refund on a registration, not including any issuing fees paid under subdivision 2, paragraph (e), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), if the refund request is received within 60 days of the original registration, the registration is not used or transferred, and:</p>
          <p>(1) the vehicle was registered incorrectly; or</p>
          <p>(2) the vehicle was registered twice, once by the dealer and once by the customer.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.922.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">No-registration weekend.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall designate, by written order published in the State Register, one weekend each year when, notwithstanding subdivision 1, an all-terrain vehicle may be operated on state and grant-in-aid all-terrain vehicle trails without a registration issued under this section. Nonresidents may participate during the designated weekend without a state trail pass required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9275">84.9275</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.922.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 647 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.2.0">1986 c 452 s 2</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 331 s 9</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.18.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.62.0">1995 c 220 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/410/#laws.0.21.0">1996 c 410 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.13.0">2001 c 185 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.2.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.29.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 29</a>,30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.39.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 39</a>,40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.28.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.11.0">2008 c 357 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.17.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; art 2 s 8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.17.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 17</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.9.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.4.0">2013 c 121 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.15.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.10.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.21.2.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 21 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.14.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.1.13.0">2024 c 90 art 1 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 12 by Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 13, is effective upon full implementation of the replacement electronic license system. The commissioner of natural resources must notify the revisor of statutes when the replacement electronic license system is fully implemented. Laws 2024, chapter 90, article 1, section 52.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.923">
        <h1 class="shn">84.923 REQUIREMENTS OF MAKERS OF ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.923.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Identification number.</span></h2>
          <p>All vehicles made after January 1, 1985, and sold in the state, must have manufacturer's permanent identification number stamped in letters and numbers on the vehicle in the form and at a location prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.923.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Registration number.</span></h2>
          <p>All vehicles made after January 1, 1985, and sold in the state, must be designed and made to provide an area to affix the registration number. This area shall be at a location and of dimensions prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.923.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.3.0">1984 c 647 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.924">
        <h1 class="shn">84.924 RULEMAKING; ACCIDENT REPORT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.924.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of natural resources.</span></h2>
          <p>With a view of achieving proper use of all-terrain vehicles consistent with protection of the environment, the commissioner of natural resources shall adopt rules under chapter 14 relating to:</p>
          <p>(1) registration of all-terrain vehicles and display of registration numbers;</p>
          <p>(2) use of all-terrain vehicles insofar as game and fish resources are affected;</p>
          <p>(3) use of all-terrain vehicles on public lands and waters;</p>
          <p>(4) uniform signs to be used by the state, counties, and cities necessary or desirable to control, direct, or regulate the operation and use of all-terrain vehicles; and</p>
          <p>(5) specifications relating to all-terrain vehicle mufflers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.924.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of public safety.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety may adopt rules under chapter 14 regulating the use of all-terrain vehicles on streets and highways.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.924.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Accident report; requirement and form.</span></h2>
          <p>The operator and an officer investigating an accident of an all-terrain vehicle involved in an accident resulting in injury requiring medical attention or hospitalization to or death of a person or total damage to an extent of $500 or more shall within ten business days forward a written report of the accident to the commissioner of natural resources on a form prescribed by either the commissioner of natural resources or by the commissioner of public safety. If the operator is killed or is unable to file a report due to incapacitation, any peace officer investigating the accident shall file the accident report within ten business days. Periodically, the commissioner of natural resources must transmit a summary of the accident reports to the commissioner of public safety.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.924.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.10.0">1986 c 452 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.13.0">1989 c 331 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/184/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 184 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/615/#laws.0.4.0">1994 c 615 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.925">
        <h1 class="shn">84.925 EDUCATION AND TRAINING PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.925.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Training and certification programs established.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall establish:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a comprehensive all-terrain vehicle environmental and safety education and training certification program, including the preparation and dissemination of vehicle information and safety advice to the public, the training of all-terrain vehicle operators, and the issuance of all-terrain vehicle safety certificates to vehicle operators over the age of 12 years who successfully complete the all-terrain vehicle environmental and safety education and training course; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a voluntary all-terrain vehicle online training program for youth and a parent or guardian, offered at no charge for operators at least six years of age but younger than ten years of age.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A parent or guardian must be present at a training program when the youth is under ten years of age.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For the purpose of administering the program and to defray the expenses of training and certifying vehicle operators, the commissioner shall collect a fee from each person who receives the training for certification under paragraph (a), clause (1). The commissioner shall collect a fee, to include a $1 issuing fee for licensing agents, for issuing a duplicate all-terrain vehicle safety certificate. The commissioner shall establish both fees in a manner that neither significantly overrecovers nor underrecovers costs, including overhead costs, involved in providing the services. The fees are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The fees may be established by the commissioner notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>. Fee proceeds, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents under this subdivision, shall be deposited in the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund and the amount thereof, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027#stat.84.027.15">84.027, subdivision 15</a>, and issuing fees collected by the commissioner, is appropriated annually to the Enforcement Division of the Department of Natural Resources for the administration of the programs. In addition to the fee established by the commissioner, instructors may charge each person up to the established fee amount for class materials and expenses.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall cooperate with private organizations and associations, private and public corporations, and local governmental units in furtherance of the programs established under this section. School districts may cooperate with the commissioner and volunteer instructors to provide space for the classroom portion of the training. The commissioner shall consult with the commissioner of public safety in regard to the subject matter of the training programs and performance testing that leads to the certification of vehicle operators. The commissioner shall incorporate a riding component in the training certification programs established under this section and may incorporate a riding component in the training program established in paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.925.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 331 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.925.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">All-terrain vehicle safety courses; reciprocity with other states; accepted equivalencies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner may enter into reciprocity agreements or otherwise certify all-terrain vehicle environmental and safety education and training courses from other states that are substantially similar to in-state courses. Proof of completion of a course subject to a reciprocity agreement or certified as substantially similar is adequate to meet the safety certificate requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Proof of completion of the ATV RiderCourse offered by the All-Terrain Vehicle Safety Institute is adequate to meet the safety certificate requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.925.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exemption from rulemaking and legislative approval.</span></h2>
          <p>The fee to issue a duplicate all-terrain vehicle safety certificate under subdivision 1 is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. The commissioner may establish the duplicate all-terrain safety certificate fee notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.925.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Training requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An individual who was born after July 1, 1987, and who is 16 years of age or older, must successfully complete the independent study course component of all-terrain vehicle safety training before operating an all-terrain vehicle on public lands or waters, public road rights-of-way, or state or grant-in-aid trails.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) An individual who is convicted of violating a law related to the operation of an all-terrain vehicle must successfully complete the independent study course component of all-terrain vehicle safety training before continuing operation of an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) An individual who is convicted for a second or subsequent excess speed, trespass, or wetland violation in an all-terrain vehicle season, or any conviction for careless or reckless operation of an all-terrain vehicle, must successfully complete the independent study and the testing and operating course components of all-terrain vehicle safety training before continuing operation of an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) An individual who receives three or more citations and convictions for violating a law related to the operation of an all-terrain vehicle in a two-year period must successfully complete the independent study and the testing and operating course components of all-terrain vehicle safety training before continuing operation of an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) An individual must present evidence of compliance with this subdivision before an all-terrain vehicle registration is issued or renewed. A person may use the following as evidence of meeting all-terrain vehicle safety certificate requirements:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a valid all-terrain vehicle safety certificate issued by the commissioner;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a driver's license that has a valid all-terrain vehicle safety certificate indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.18">171.07, subdivision 18</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) an identification card that has a valid all-terrain vehicle safety certificate indicator issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.07#stat.171.07.18">171.07, subdivision 18</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.925.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 647 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.11.0">1986 c 452 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/495/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 495 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.80.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.8.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/221/#laws.0.13.0">2004 c 221 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 41</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.7.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.20.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.10.0">2011 c 107 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.12.0">2014 c 289 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.11.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.9254"><b>84.9254</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.69.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 69</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9256">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9256 YOUTHFUL OPERATORS; PROHIBITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9256.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions on youthful operators.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Except for operation on public road rights-of-way that is permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a> and as provided under paragraph (j), a driver's license issued by the state or another state is required to operate an all-terrain vehicle along or on a public road right-of-way.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person under 12 years of age shall not:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) operate an all-terrain vehicle on a public road right-of-way in the state; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) operate an all-terrain vehicle on public lands or waters, except as provided in paragraph (f).</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Except for public road rights-of-way of interstate highways, a person 12 years of age but less than 16 years may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway or operate on public lands and waters or state or grant-in-aid trails, only if that person possesses a valid all-terrain vehicle safety certificate issued by the commissioner and is accompanied by a person 18 years of age or older who holds a valid driver's license.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) To be issued an all-terrain vehicle safety certificate, a person at least 12 years old, but less than 16 years old, must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) successfully complete the safety education and training program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.925">84.925</a>, subdivision 1, including a riding component; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) be able to properly reach and control the handle bars and reach the foot pegs while sitting upright on the seat of the all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) A person at least ten years of age may take the safety education and training program and may receive an all-terrain vehicle safety certificate under paragraph (d), but the certificate is not valid until the person reaches age 12.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A person at least ten years of age but under 12 years of age may operate an all-terrain vehicle with an engine capacity up to 110cc if the vehicle is a class 1 all-terrain vehicle with straddle-style seating or up to 170cc if the vehicle is a class 1 all-terrain vehicle with side-by-side-style seating on public lands or waters if accompanied by a parent or legal guardian.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) A person under 15 years of age shall not operate a class 2 all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) A person under the age of 16 may not operate an all-terrain vehicle on public lands or waters or on state or grant-in-aid trails if the person cannot properly reach and control:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the handle bars and reach the foot pegs while sitting upright on the seat of the all-terrain vehicle with straddle-style seating; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the steering wheel and foot controls of a class 1 all-terrain vehicle with side-by-side-style seating while sitting upright in the seat with the seat belt fully engaged.</p>
          <p>(i) Notwithstanding paragraph (c), a nonresident at least 12 years old, but less than 16 years old, may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway or operate an all-terrain vehicle on public lands and waters or state or grant-in-aid trails if:</p>
          <p>(1) the nonresident youth has in possession evidence of completing an all-terrain safety course offered by the ATV Safety Institute or another state as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.925">84.925</a>, subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(2) the nonresident youth is accompanied by a person 18 years of age or older who holds a valid driver's license.</p>
          <p>(j) A person 12 years of age but less than 16 years of age may operate an all-terrain vehicle on the roadway, bank, slope, or ditch of a public road right-of-way as permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a> if the person:</p>
          <p>(1) possesses a valid all-terrain vehicle safety certificate issued by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) is accompanied by a parent or legal guardian on a separate all-terrain vehicle.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9256.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Helmet and seat belts required.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A person less than 18 years of age shall not ride as a passenger or as an operator of an all-terrain vehicle on public land, public waters, or on a public road right-of-way unless wearing a safety helmet approved by the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person less than 18 years of age shall not ride as a passenger or as an operator of an all-terrain vehicle without wearing a seat belt when provided by the manufacturer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9256.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Parent or guardian authorization.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A person under age 16 shall not operate and a person shall not allow a person under age 16 to operate an all-terrain vehicle, unless the parent or guardian of the person under age 16 authorizes the operation. For purposes of this subdivision, "guardian" means the legal guardian of the person under age 16 or a person age 18 or older who has been authorized by the parent or legal guardian to supervise the person under age 16.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9256.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prohibitions on person in lawful control.</span></h2>
          <p>It is unlawful for any person who is in lawful control of an all-terrain vehicle to permit it to be operated contrary to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9256.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Suspension.</span></h2>
          <p>When the judge of a juvenile court, or its duly authorized agent, determines that a person, while less than 18 years of age, has violated sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>, or other state or local law or ordinance regulating the operation of an all-terrain vehicle, the judge or duly authorized agent shall immediately report the determination to the commissioner and (1) may recommend the suspension of the person's all-terrain vehicle safety certificate, or (2) may recommend to the commissioner of public safety, the suspension of the person's driver's license. The commissioner may suspend the certificate without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9256.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.13.0">1986 c 452 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.14.0">1989 c 331 s 14</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/184/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 184 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.81.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/146/#laws.0.5.0">2005 c 146 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.43.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.4.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.8.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.12.0">2008 c 357 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.21.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.10.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.6.0">2013 c 121 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.13.0">2014 c 289 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.17.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.12.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.21.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9257">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9257 PASSENGERS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) A person 18 years of age or older may operate a class 1 all-terrain vehicle carrying only one passenger.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A person 18 years of age or older may operate a class 2 all-terrain vehicle while carrying only one passenger, or up to the number of passengers for which the vehicle was designed, whichever is greater.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) A person 12 to 17 years of age may operate a class 1 all-terrain vehicle carrying only one passenger and the passenger must be the person's parent or legal guardian.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9257.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.82.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.44.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.5.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.10.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/107/#laws.0.11.0">2011 c 107 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.926">
        <h1 class="shn">84.926 VEHICLE USE ON PUBLIC LANDS; EXCEPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Exception by permit.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773#stat.84.773.1">84.773, subdivision 1</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>, on a case-by-case basis, the commissioner may issue a permit authorizing a person to operate an off-highway vehicle on individual public trails under the commissioner's jurisdiction during specified times and for specified purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">All-terrain vehicles; managed or limited forests; off trail.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>, but subject to the commissioner's authority under subdivision 5, on state forest lands classified as managed or limited, other than the Richard J. Dorer Memorial Hardwood Forest, a person may use an all-terrain vehicle off forest trails or forest roads when:</p>
          <p>(1) hunting big game or transporting or installing hunting stands during October, November, and December, when in possession of a valid big-game-hunting license;</p>
          <p>(2) retrieving big game in September, when in possession of a valid big-game-hunting license;</p>
          <p>(3) tending traps during an open trapping season for protected furbearers, when in possession of a valid trapping license; or</p>
          <p>(4) trapping minnows, when in possession of a valid minnow dealer, private fish hatchery, or aquatic farm license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">All-terrain vehicles; closed forests; hunting.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>, the commissioner may determine whether all-terrain vehicles are allowed on specific forest roads, on state forest lands classified as closed, for the purpose of hunting big game during an open big-game season. The determination shall be by written order as published in the State Register and is exempt from chapter 14. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Off-road and all-terrain vehicles; limited or managed forests; trails.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.777">84.777</a>, but subject to the commissioner's authority under subdivision 5, on state forest lands classified as limited or managed, other than the Richard J. Dorer Memorial Hardwood Forest, a person may use vehicles registered under chapter 168 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.798">84.798</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922">84.922</a>, including class 2 all-terrain vehicles, on forest trails designated for off-road vehicle use and on forest trails that are not designated for a specific use when:</p>
          <p>(1) hunting big game or transporting or installing hunting stands during October, November, and December, when in possession of a valid big-game-hunting license;</p>
          <p>(2) retrieving big game in September, when in possession of a valid big-game-hunting license;</p>
          <p>(3) tending traps during an open trapping season for protected furbearers, when in possession of a valid trapping license; or</p>
          <p>(4) trapping minnows, when in possession of a valid minnow dealer, private fish hatchery, or aquatic farm license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Limitations on off-trail and undesignated trail use.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may designate areas on state forest lands that are not subject to the exceptions provided in subdivisions 2 and 4. Such designations are not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply. Before designating such areas, the commissioner shall hold a public meeting in the county where the largest portion of the forest lands are located to provide information to and receive comment from the public regarding the proposed designation. Sixty days before the public meeting, notice of the proposed designation shall be published in the legal newspapers that serve the counties in which the lands are located, in a statewide Department of Natural Resources news release, and in the State Register.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Operation; class 2 vehicles.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as provided in subdivision 4, operation of class 2 all-terrain vehicles on lands administered by the commissioner is limited to forest roads, minimum maintenance roads, and trails designated or signed for class 2 all-terrain vehicles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.926.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Snowmobiles; closed forests; off trail.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Forests classified as closed forests are open to off-trail snowmobile use unless prohibited, as determined by the commissioner by written order published in the State Register. The determination is not subject to the rulemaking provisions of chapter 14, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> does not apply.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.926.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.5.0">1984 c 647 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.31.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.45.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.6.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.11.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/289/#laws.0.14.0">2014 c 289 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.927">
        <h1 class="shn">84.927 ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLE ACCOUNT; RECEIPTS AND ALLOCATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.927.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registration revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees from the registration of all-terrain vehicles and the unrefunded gasoline tax attributable to all-terrain vehicle use under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/296A.18">296A.18</a>, as well as the net proceeds from the sale of all-terrain vehicles forfeited pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.63">169A.63</a>, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.927.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Subject to appropriation by the legislature, money in the all-terrain vehicle account may only be spent for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the education and training program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.925">84.925</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) administration, enforcement, and implementation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.773">84.773</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) acquisition, maintenance, and development of vehicle trails and use areas;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) grant-in-aid programs to counties and municipalities to construct and maintain all-terrain vehicle trails and use areas;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) grants-in-aid to local safety programs;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) enforcement and public education grants to local law enforcement agencies; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) maintenance of minimum-maintenance forest roads designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.71#stat.89.71.5">89.71, subdivision 5</a>, and county forest roads that are part of a designated trail system within state forest boundaries as established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.021">89.021</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The distribution of funds made available through grant-in-aid programs must be guided by the statewide comprehensive outdoor recreation plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.927.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.6.0">1984 c 647 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.14.0">1986 c 452 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.4.19.0">1989 c 335 art 4 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/230/#laws.0.5.0">1995 c 230 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/2/#laws.0.4.0">1Sp1997 c 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/299/#laws.0.30.0">1998 c 299 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.32.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.30.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9275">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9275 NONRESIDENTS; STATE TRAIL PASS FOR ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9275.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pass required; fee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A tribal member exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922#stat.84.922.1a">84.922, subdivision 1a</a>, clause (2), or a nonresident may not operate an all-terrain vehicle on a state or grant-in-aid all-terrain vehicle trail unless the operator carries a valid nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail pass in immediate possession. The pass must be available for inspection by a peace officer, a conservation officer, or an employee designated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0835">84.0835</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of natural resources shall issue a pass upon application and payment of a $30 fee. The pass is valid from January 1 through December 31. Fees collected under this section, except for the issuing fee for licensing agents, shall be deposited in the state treasury and credited to the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund and, except for the electronic licensing system commission established by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15, must be used for grants-in-aid to counties and municipalities for all-terrain vehicle organizations to construct and maintain all-terrain vehicle trails and use areas.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail pass is not required for:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) an all-terrain vehicle that is owned and used by the United States, another state, or a political subdivision thereof that is exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922">84.922</a>, subdivision 1a;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a person operating an all-terrain vehicle only on the portion of a trail that is owned by the person or the person's spouse, child, or parent; or</p>
          <p>(3) a nonresident operating an all-terrain vehicle that is registered according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.922">84.922</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9275.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">License agents.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may appoint agents to issue and sell nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail passes. The commissioner may revoke the appointment of an agent at any time. The commissioner may adopt additional rules as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall observe all rules adopted by the commissioner for accounting and handling of passes pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.485#stat.97A.485.11">97A.485, subdivision 11</a>. An agent shall promptly deposit and remit all money received from the sale of the passes, exclusive of the issuing fee, to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9275.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Issuing passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue and sell nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail passes. The commissioner shall also make the passes available through the electronic licensing system established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.027">84.027</a>, subdivision 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9275.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agent's fee.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the fee for a pass, an issuing fee of $1 per pass shall be charged. The issuing fee may be retained by the seller of the pass. Issuing fees for passes issued by the commissioner shall be deposited in the all-terrain vehicle account in the natural resources fund and retained for the operation of the electronic licensing system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9275.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duplicate passes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and agents shall issue a duplicate pass to persons whose pass is lost or destroyed using the process established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.405#stat.97A.405.3">97A.405, subdivision 3</a>, and rules adopted thereunder. The fee for a duplicate nonresident all-terrain vehicle state trail pass is $2, with an issuing fee of 50 cents.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/37/#laws.1.18.0">2009 c 37 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.22.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.7.0">2013 c 121 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.14.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.928">
        <h1 class="shn">84.928 OPERATION REQUIREMENTS; LOCAL REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Operation on roads and rights-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Unless otherwise allowed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a> or by local ordinance under paragraph (k), a person shall not operate an all-terrain vehicle in this state along or on the roadway, shoulder, or inside bank or slope of a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A person may operate a class 1 all-terrain vehicle in the ditch or the outside bank or slope of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway unless prohibited under paragraph (d) or (f).</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A person may operate a class 1 all-terrain vehicle designed by the manufacturer for off-road use to be driven by a steering wheel and equipped with operator and passenger seat belts and a roll-over protective structure or a class 2 all-terrain vehicle:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) within the public road right-of-way of a county state-aid or county highway on the right shoulder or the extreme right-hand side of the road and left turns may be made from any part of the road if it is safe to do so under the prevailing conditions, unless prohibited under paragraph (d) or (f);</p>
          <p class="in">(2) on the bank, slope, or ditch of a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway but only to access businesses or make trail connections, and left turns may be made from any part of the road if it is safe to do so under the prevailing conditions, unless prohibited under paragraph (d) or (f); and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) on the bank or ditch of a public road right-of-way on a designated class 2 all-terrain vehicle trail.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A road authority as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02">160.02</a>, subdivision 25, may after a public hearing restrict the use of all-terrain vehicles in the public road right-of-way under its jurisdiction.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The restrictions in paragraphs (a), (d), (h), (i), and (j) do not apply to the operation of an all-terrain vehicle on the shoulder, inside bank or slope, ditch, or outside bank or slope of a trunk, interstate, county state-aid, or county highway:</p>
          <p>(1) that is part of a funded grant-in-aid trail; or</p>
          <p>(2) when the all-terrain vehicle is owned by or operated under contract with:</p>
          <p>(i) a road authority as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>; or</p>
          <p>(ii) a publicly or privately owned utility or pipeline company and used for work on utilities or pipelines.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The commissioner may limit the use of a right-of-way for a period of time if the commissioner determines that use of the right-of-way causes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) degradation of vegetation on adjacent public property;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) siltation of waters of the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) impairment or enhancement to the act of taking game; or</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a threat to safety of the right-of-way users or to individuals on adjacent public property.</p>
          <p class="bl">The commissioner must notify the road authority as soon as it is known that a closure will be ordered. The notice must state the reasons and duration of the closure.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) A person may operate an all-terrain vehicle registered for private use and used for agricultural purposes on a public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway in this state if the all-terrain vehicle is operated on the extreme right-hand side of the road, and left turns may be made from any part of the road if it is safe to do so under the prevailing conditions.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) A person shall not operate an all-terrain vehicle within the public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway from April 1 to August 1 in the agricultural zone unless the vehicle is being used exclusively as transportation to and from work on agricultural lands. This paragraph does not apply to an agent or employee of a road authority, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02">160.02</a>, subdivision 25, or the Department of Natural Resources when performing or exercising official duties or powers.</p>
          <p class="in">(i) A person shall not operate an all-terrain vehicle within the public road right-of-way of a trunk, county state-aid, or county highway between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise, except on the right-hand side of the right-of-way and in the same direction as the highway traffic on the nearest lane of the adjacent roadway.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) A person shall not operate an all-terrain vehicle at any time within the right-of-way of an interstate highway or freeway within this state.</p>
          <p>(k) A county, city, or town, acting through its governing body, may by ordinance allow a person to operate an all-terrain vehicle on a public road or street under its jurisdiction to access businesses and residences and to make trail connections.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Crossing public road right-of-way.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An all-terrain vehicle may make a direct crossing of a public road right-of-way provided:</p>
          <p>(1) the crossing is made at an angle of approximately 90 degrees to the direction of the road and at a place where no obstruction prevents a quick and safe crossing;</p>
          <p>(2) the vehicle is brought to a complete stop before crossing the shoulder or main-traveled way of the road;</p>
          <p>(3) the driver yields the right-of-way to all oncoming traffic that constitutes an immediate hazard;</p>
          <p>(4) in crossing a divided road, the crossing is made only at an intersection of the road with another public road; and</p>
          <p>(5) if the crossing is made between the hours of one-half hour after sunset to one-half hour before sunrise or in conditions of reduced visibility, only if both front and rear lights are on.</p>
          <p>(b) An all-terrain vehicle may be operated upon a bridge, other than a bridge that is part of the main-traveled lanes of an interstate highway, or roadway shoulder or inside bank of a public road right-of-way when required for the purpose of avoiding obstructions to travel or environmentally sensitive areas when no other method of avoidance is possible; provided the all-terrain vehicle is operated in the extreme right-hand lane, the entrance to the roadway is made within 100 feet of the bridge, obstacle, or sensitive area, and the crossing is made without undue delay.</p>
          <p>(c) A person shall not operate an all-terrain vehicle upon a public street or highway unless the vehicle is equipped with at least one headlight and one taillight, each of minimum candlepower as prescribed by rules of the commissioner, and with brakes conforming to standards prescribed by rule of the commissioner, and all of which are subject to the approval of the commissioner of public safety.</p>
          <p>(d) An all-terrain vehicle may be operated upon a public road right-of-way other than as provided by paragraph (b) in an emergency during the period of time when and at locations where the condition of the roadway renders travel by automobile impractical.</p>
          <p>(e) Chapters 169 and 169A apply to the operation of all-terrain vehicles upon streets and highways, except for those provisions relating to required equipment and except those provisions which by their nature have no application.</p>
          <p>(f) A sled, trailer, or other device being towed by an all-terrain vehicle must be equipped with reflective materials as required by rule of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(g) A driver's license is not required to operate an all-terrain vehicle along or on a public road right-of-way if the right-of-way encompasses a trail administered by the commissioner and designated for all-terrain vehicle use or multiple use.</p>
          <p>(h) A road authority as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02#stat.160.02.25">160.02, subdivision 25</a>, may by permit designate corridor access trails on public road rights-of-way for purposes of accessing established all-terrain vehicle trails. A driver's license is not required to operate an all-terrain vehicle on a designated corridor access trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Operation generally.</span></h2>
          <p>A person may not drive or operate an all-terrain vehicle:</p>
          <p>(1) at a rate of speed greater than reasonable or proper under the surrounding circumstances;</p>
          <p>(2) in a careless, reckless, or negligent manner so as to endanger or to cause injury or damage to the person or property of another;</p>
          <p>(3) without headlight and taillight lighted at all times if the vehicle is equipped with headlight and taillight;</p>
          <p>(4) without a functioning stoplight if so equipped;</p>
          <p>(5) in a tree nursery or planting in a manner that damages or destroys growing stock;</p>
          <p>(6) without a brake operational by either hand or foot;</p>
          <p>(7) with more than one person on the vehicle, except as allowed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9257">84.9257</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) at a speed exceeding ten miles per hour on the frozen surface of public waters within 100 feet of a person not on an all-terrain vehicle or within 100 feet of a fishing shelter; or</p>
          <p>(9) in a manner that violates operation rules adopted by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/615/#laws.0.28.0">1994 c 615 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Operation prohibited on airports.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for employees and agents while acting incident to the operation of the airport, it is unlawful for a person to drive or operate an all-terrain vehicle on an airport defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/360.013#stat.360.013.39">360.013, subdivision 39</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Organized contests; using highways and public lands and waters.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Nothing in this section or chapter 169 prohibits the use of all-terrain vehicles within the right-of-way of a state trunk or county state-aid highway or upon public lands or waters under the jurisdiction of the commissioner of natural resources, in an organized contest or event, subject to the consent of the official or board having jurisdiction over the highway or public lands or waters.</p>
          <p>(b) In permitting the contest or event, the official or board having jurisdiction may prescribe restrictions or conditions as they may deem advisable.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9256">84.9256</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), a person under 12 years of age may operate an all-terrain vehicle in an organized contest on public lands or waters, if the all-terrain vehicle has an engine capacity of 90cc or less, the person complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.9256">84.9256</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (h), and the person is supervised by a person 18 years of age or older.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Regulation by political subdivisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a city or town, acting through its governing body, may by resolution or ordinance prohibit the operation of all-terrain vehicles on city streets or town roads in its jurisdiction provided the regulations are otherwise consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A county or city, or a town acting by its town board, may regulate the operation of all-terrain vehicles on public lands, waters, and property under its jurisdiction other than public road rights-of-way within its boundaries, by resolution or ordinance of the governing body and by giving appropriate notice, provided:</p>
          <p>(1) the regulations must be consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.92">84.92</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.928">84.928</a> and rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.924">84.924</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) an ordinance may not impose a fee for the use of public land or water under the jurisdiction of either the Department of Natural Resources or other agency of the state, or for the use of an access to it owned by the state or a county or a city; and</p>
          <p>(3) an ordinance may not require an all-terrain vehicle operator to possess a motor vehicle driver's license while operating an all-terrain vehicle.</p>
          <p>(c) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a county board by ordinance may allow the operation of all-terrain vehicles on the road right-of-way shoulder, or inside bank or slope of a county highway or county state-aid highway, if:</p>
          <p>(1) the highway is in the agricultural zone; or</p>
          <p>(2) safe operation in the ditch or outside slope is impossible, and the county posts the appropriate notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 331 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.928.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.96.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 96</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.928.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/647/#laws.0.7.0">1984 c 647 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/452/#laws.0.15.0">1986 c 452 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.2.2.0">1987 c 149 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/368/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 368 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/331/#laws.0.17.0">1989 c 331 s 17</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/635/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 635 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.83.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 83</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.33.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.2.46.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 2 s 46</a>,47; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.7.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/131/#laws.1.13.0">2007 c 131 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.9.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 9</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/361/#laws.4.23.0">2010 c 361 art 4 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.11.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/121/#laws.0.8.0">2013 c 121 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/4/#laws.4.18.0">1Sp2015 c 4 art 4 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.22.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.929"><b>84.929</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.52.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 52</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MOTORIZED TRAIL GRANTS-IN-AID</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.930">
        <h1 class="shn">84.930 MOTORIZED TRAIL GRANTS-IN-AID.</h1>
        <p>(a) This section applies to grants-in-aid for motorized trail construction and maintenance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.794">84.794</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.803">84.803</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.83">84.83</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.927">84.927</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If the commissioner of natural resources determines that a grant-in-aid recipient has violated any federal or state law or any of the terms of the grant agreement with the commissioner, the commissioner may withhold all grant payments for any work occurring after the date the recipient was notified of the violation and seek restitution for any property damage caused by the violation.</p>
        <p>(c) A grant-in-aid recipient may appeal the commissioner's decision under paragraph (b) in a contested case hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.58">14.58</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.930.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.34.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PLANNING AND PROTECTING RESOURCES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.94">
        <h1 class="shn">84.94 AGGREGATE PLANNING AND PROTECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.94.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>It is the purpose of this section to protect aggregate resources; to promote orderly and environmentally sound development; to spread the burden of development; and to introduce aggregate resource protection into local comprehensive planning and land use controls.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.94.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of this section, "municipality" means a home rule charter or statutory city, or a town.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.94.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Identification and classification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Department of Natural Resources, with the cooperation of the state Geological Survey, the Department of Transportation, and the Department of Employment and Economic Development, outside of the metropolitan area as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121">473.121</a>, shall conduct a program of identification and classification of potentially valuable publicly or privately owned aggregate lands located outside of urban or developed areas where aggregate mining is restricted, without consideration of their present land use. The program shall give priority to identification and classification in areas of the state where urbanization or other factors are or may be resulting in a loss of aggregate resources to development. Lands shall be classified as:</p>
          <p>(1) identified resources, being those containing significant aggregate deposits;</p>
          <p>(2) potential resources, being those containing potentially significant deposits and meriting further evaluation; or</p>
          <p>(3) subeconomic resources, being those containing no significant deposits.</p>
          <p>(b) As lands are classified, the information on the classification shall be transmitted to each of the departments and agencies named in this subdivision, to the planning authority of the appropriate county and municipality, and to the appropriate county engineer. The county planning authority shall notify owners of land classified under this subdivision by publication in a newspaper of general circulation in the county or by mail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.94.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Local action.</span></h2>
          <p>Each planning authority of a county or municipality receiving information pursuant to subdivision 3 shall consider the protection of identified and important aggregate resources in their land use decisions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/605/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 605 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.3.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FISH AND WILDLIFE <br>MANAGEMENT PLANS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.941">
        <h1 class="shn">84.941 POLICY.</h1>
        <p>It is the policy of the state that fish and wildlife are renewable natural resources to be conserved and enhanced through planned scientific management, protection, and utilization.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.941.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/383/#laws.0.7.0">1986 c 383 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.942">
        <h1 class="shn">84.942 FISH AND WILDLIFE MANAGEMENT PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Preparation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall prepare fish and wildlife management plans designed to accomplish the policy of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.941">84.941</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.70.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 70</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Public agency coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must coordinate fish and wildlife planning efforts with appropriate public agencies to achieve optimum public benefit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.942.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Public involvement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must make fish and wildlife management plans available for public input, review, and comment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.942.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/383/#laws.0.8.0">1986 c 383 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.11.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CRITICAL NATURAL HABITAT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.943">
        <h1 class="shn">84.943 MINNESOTA CRITICAL HABITAT PRIVATE SECTOR MATCHING ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.943.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota critical habitat private sector matching account is established as a separate account in the reinvest in Minnesota resources fund established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.95">84.95</a>. The account shall be administered by the commissioner of natural resources as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.943.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Funding sources.</span></h2>
          <p>The critical habitat private sector matching account shall consist of contributions from private sources and appropriations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.943.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriations matched by private funds or state bond fund appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Appropriations transferred to the critical habitat private sector matching account and money credited to the account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.1296#stat.168.1296.5">168.1296, subdivision 5</a>, may be expended only to the extent that they are matched with contributions from private sources; by funds contributed to the nongame wildlife management account; or by appropriations from the bond proceeds fund for projects that benefit critical natural habitat. The private contributions may be made in cash, property, land, or interests in land. Appropriations transferred to the account that are not matched within three years from the date of the appropriation shall cancel to the source of the appropriation. For the purposes of this section, the private contributions of property, land, or interests in land that are retained by the commissioner shall be valued in accordance with their appraised value.</p>
          <p>(b) For every dollar used as a match under paragraph (a), the commissioner may expend up to two dollars from the account for the purposes described in subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.943.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Management.</span></h2>
          <p>The critical habitat private sector matching account shall be managed to earn the highest interest compatible with prudent investment, preservation of principal, and reasonable liquidity. Unless an appropriation to the account reverts to its original source under subdivision 3, the principal and interest in the account remain in the account until expended as provided in this section.</p>
