        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.943.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Pledges and contributions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of natural resources may accept contributions and pledges to the critical habitat private sector matching account. A pledge that is made contingent on an appropriation is acceptable and shall be reported with other pledges as required in this section. The commissioner may agree to match a contribution contingent on a future appropriation. In the budget request for each biennium, the commissioner shall report the balance of contributions in the account and the amount that has been pledged for payment in the succeeding two calendar years.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources only for the direct acquisition, restoration, or enhancement of land or interests in land as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.944">84.944</a>. Acquisition includes:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) purchase of land or an interest in land by the commissioner; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) acceptance by the commissioner of gifts of land or interests in land as program projects.</p>
          <p>(c) To the extent of available appropriations other than bond proceeds, the money matched to the nongame wildlife management account may be used for:</p>
          <p>(1) the management of nongame wildlife projects as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.431">290.431</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) restoration and enhancement activities for critical natural habitat; or</p>
          <p>(3) monitoring and evaluation activities for rare resources and native plant communities that inform the management of critical natural habitat.</p>
          <p class="bl">No more than 30 percent of the nongame wildlife management account appropriations each fiscal year may be used to match money from the critical habitat private sector matching account for monitoring and evaluation activities.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.943.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/383/#laws.0.9.0">1986 c 383 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/357/#laws.0.13.0">1987 c 357 s 13</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/220/#laws.0.63.0">1995 c 220 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/281/#laws.2.9.0">2006 c 281 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/368/#laws.1.8.0">2008 c 368 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.26.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 26</a>,27</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.944">
        <h1 class="shn">84.944 ACQUIRING CRITICAL NATURAL HABITAT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.944.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Acquisition, restoration, and enhancement considerations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In determining what critical natural habitat shall be acquired, restored, or enhanced, the commissioner shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the significance of the land or water as existing or potential habitat for fish and wildlife and providing fish and wildlife oriented recreation;</p>
          <p>(2) the significance of the land, water, or habitat improvement to maintain or enhance native plant, fish, or wildlife species designated as endangered or threatened under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.0895">84.0895</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) the presence of native ecological communities that are now uncommon or diminishing; and</p>
          <p>(4) the significance of the land, water or habitat improvement to protect or enhance natural features within or contiguous to natural areas including fish spawning areas, wildlife management areas, scientific and natural areas, riparian habitat and fish and wildlife management projects.</p>
          <p>(b) Based on the above clauses, the commissioner by rule must establish a process to prioritize what critical habitat shall be acquired or improved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.944.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Designating acquired sites.</span></h2>
          <p>The critical natural habitat acquired in fee title by the commissioner under this section shall be designated by the commissioner as: (1) an outdoor recreation unit pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.07#stat.86A.07.3">86A.07, subdivision 3</a>; or (2) as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/89.018#stat.89.018.2">89.018, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.101">97A.101</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.125">97A.125</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97C.001">97C.001</a>. The commissioner may so designate any critical natural habitat acquired in less than fee title.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.944.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">County approval.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must follow the procedures under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/97A.145#stat.97A.145.2">97A.145, subdivision 2</a>, for critical natural habitat acquired under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.944.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/383/#laws.0.10.0">1986 c 383 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/149/#laws.2.3.0">1987 c 149 art 2 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/357/#laws.0.16.0">1987 c 357 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.7.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/259/#laws.0.7.0">1991 c 259 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/375/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 375 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/290/#laws.0.9.0">2014 c 290 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.28.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.946">
        <h1 class="shn">84.946 NATURAL RESOURCES ASSET PRESERVATION AND REPLACEMENT (NRAPR).</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.946.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature recognizes that the Department of Natural Resources owns and operates capital assets that in number, size, and programmatic use differ significantly from the capital assets owned and operated by other state departments and agencies. However, the legislature recognizes the need for standards to aid in categorizing and funding capital projects. The purpose of this section is to provide standards for those natural resource projects that are intended to preserve and replace existing facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.946.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An appropriation for asset preservation may be used only for a capital expenditure on a capital asset previously owned by the state, within the meaning of generally accepted accounting principles as applied to public expenditures. The commissioner of natural resources will consult with the commissioner of management and budget to the extent necessary to ensure this and will furnish the commissioner of management and budget a list of projects to be financed from the account in order of their priority. The legislature assumes that many projects for preservation and replacement of portions of existing capital assets will constitute betterments and capital improvements within the meaning of the constitution and capital expenditures under generally accepted accounting principles, and will be financed more efficiently and economically under this section than by direct appropriations for specific projects.</p>
          <p>(b) An appropriation for asset preservation must not be used to acquire land or to acquire or construct buildings or other facilities.</p>
          <p>(c) Capital budget expenditures for natural resource asset preservation and replacement projects must be for one or more of the following types of capital projects that support the existing programmatic mission of the department: code compliance including health and safety, Americans with Disabilities Act requirements, hazardous material abatement, access improvement, or air quality improvement; building energy efficiency improvements using current best practices; building or infrastructure repairs necessary to preserve the interior and exterior of existing buildings; projects to remove life safety hazards such as building code violations or structural defects; or renovation of other existing improvements to land, including but not limited to trails and bridges.</p>
          <p>(d) Up to ten percent of an appropriation awarded under this section may be used for design costs for projects eligible to be funded from this account in anticipation of future funding from the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.946.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.98.0">2012 c 272 s 98</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.946.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Priorities; report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must establish priorities for natural resource asset preservation and replacement projects. By March 1 each year, the commissioner must submit to the commissioner of management and budget a list of the projects that have been paid for with money from a natural resource asset preservation and replacement appropriation during the preceding calendar year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.946.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/93/#laws.1.25.0">2009 c 93 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.15.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/8/#laws.2.4.0">1Sp2017 c 8 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.29.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">REINVESTMENT IN MINNESOTA<br>RESOURCES FUND</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.95">
        <h1 class="shn">84.95 REINVEST IN MINNESOTA RESOURCES FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.95.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program fund; establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A reinvest in Minnesota resources fund is created as a separate fund in the state treasury. The fund shall be managed to earn the highest interest compatible with prudent investment, preservation of principal, and reasonable liquidity. The principal and interest attributable to the principal shall remain in the fund until spent. Proceeds of state bonds issued for purposes of the fund shall be segregated in a special account and disbursed only for capital costs of the acquisition and betterment of public land and easements in land and improvements in land for which the proceeds are appropriated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.95.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purposes and expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Money from the reinvest in Minnesota resources fund may only be spent for the following fish and wildlife conservation enhancement purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) development and implementation of the fish and wildlife management plans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.942">84.942</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) implementation of the reinvest in Minnesota reserve program established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103F.515">103F.515</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) soil and water conservation practices to improve water quality, reduce soil erosion and crop surpluses;</p>
          <p>(4) enhancement or restoration of fish and wildlife habitat on lakes, streams, wetlands, and public and private forest lands;</p>
          <p>(5) acquisition and development of public access sites and recreation easements to lakes, streams, and rivers for fish and wildlife oriented recreation;</p>
          <p>(6) matching funds with government agencies, federally recognized Indian tribes and bands, and the private sector for acquisition and improvement of fish and wildlife habitat;</p>
          <p>(7) research and surveys of fish and wildlife species and habitat;</p>
          <p>(8) enforcement of natural resource laws and rules;</p>
          <p>(9) information and education;</p>
          <p>(10) implementing the aspen recycling program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.80">88.80</a> and for other forest wildlife management projects; and</p>
          <p>(11) necessary support services to carry out these purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.95.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/255/#laws.0.51.0">2004 c 255 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/383/#laws.0.12.0">1986 c 383 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/357/#laws.0.17.0">1987 c 357 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/353/#laws.0.8.0">1989 c 353 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/391/#laws.8.15.0">1990 c 391 art 8 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/227/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 227 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/176/#laws.1.50.0">2009 c 176 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/2/#laws.5.12.0">1Sp2011 c 2 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PRAIRIE LAND MANAGEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.96">
        <h1 class="shn">84.96 NATIVE PRAIRIE BANK.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish a native prairie bank, determine where native prairie land is located in the state, and prescribe eligibility requirements for inclusion of land in the native prairie bank.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "native prairie" means a grassland dominated by original native prairie vegetation, usually occurring where the sod has never been broken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Easement acquisition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may acquire native prairie for conservation purposes by entering into easements with landowners or with the land administrator of state school trust lands. Before acquiring easements under this subdivision on school trust lands, the commissioner must receive advice from the school trust lands director according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.353#stat.127A.353.4">127A.353, subdivision 4</a>. The easements must be conservation easements as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84C.01">84C.01</a>, clause (1), except the easements may be made possessory as well as nonpossessory if agreed upon by the landowner or land administrator and the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The easements may be permanent or of limited duration. Highest priority must be given to permanent easements consistent with the purposes of this section. Easements of limited duration must be for at least 20 years, with provision for renewal for at least another 20-year period. For easements of limited duration, the commissioner may reexamine and adjust the payment rates at the beginning of any renewal period after considering current land and crop values.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Easement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the easement between the commissioner and an owner, the owner must agree:</p>
          <p>(1) to place in the program for the period of the easement eligible native prairie areas designated by the owner, including prairie covered by a federal or state easement that allows agricultural use and desirable land adjacent to the prairie as determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) not to alter the native prairie by plowing, heavy grazing, seeding to nonnative grasses or legumes, spraying with large amounts of herbicides, or otherwise destroying the native prairie character of the easement area, except mowing the native prairie tract for wild hay may qualify for easement as determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) to implement the native prairie conservation and development plan as provided in the easement agreement, unless a requirement in the easement agreement is waived or modified by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(4) to forfeit all rights to further payments under the terms of the easement and to refund to the state all payments received under the easement if the easement is violated at any time when the owner has control of the land subject to the easement, if the commissioner determines that the violation warrants termination of the easement, or if the commissioner determines that the violation does not warrant termination of the easement, the commissioner may determine refunds or payment adjustments to be paid by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(5) not to adopt a practice specified by the commissioner in the easement as a practice that would tend to defeat the purposes of the easement; and</p>
          <p>(6) to additional provisions included in the easement that the commissioner determines are desirable.</p>
          <p>(b) In return for the easement of the owner, the commissioner shall make payments as provided in subdivision 5 and may provide advice on conservation and development practices on the native prairie in the easement and adjacent areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Payments.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For interests in lands acquired under this section, the commissioner must make payments to the landowner or land administrator according to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) For a permanent easement, the commissioner may pay up to ten percent more than the Board of Water and Soil Resources pays for noncrop easements. If the Board of Water and Soil Resources does not establish a noncrop easement payment rate, the commissioner must establish land value rates for payments considering market factors, such as county-assessed land value and sales ratio studies, along with ecological, biological, and cultural factors that take into consideration the global rarity of native prairie. The rates must be based on the need to protect the extremely small amount of the globally vulnerable and imperiled remaining native prairie in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(c) For an easement of limited duration, the commissioner may pay up to 65 percent of the permanent prairie bank easement rate for the time period when the application is made.</p>
          <p>(d) To maintain and protect native prairies, the commissioner may enter into easements that allow selected agricultural practices. Payment must be based on paragraph (b) or (c) but may be reduced due to the agricultural practices allowed after negotiation with the landowner or land administrator.</p>
          <p>(e) If a native prairie qualifies for the native prairie bank but the landowner requests that the commissioner acquire the native prairie in fee rather than acquire an easement, the commissioner may acquire it as any outdoor recreation system classification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86A.05">86A.05</a> with protections equivalent to a native prairie bank easement. For acquisition under this paragraph, the commissioner may pay up to 25 percent more than what the Board of Water and Soil Resources pays for noncrop easements. If the Board of Water and Soil Resources does not establish a noncrop easement payment rate, the commissioner must establish land value rates for payments considering market factors, such as county-assessed land value and sales ratio studies, along with ecological, biological, and cultural factors that take into consideration the global rarity of native prairie.</p>
          <p>(f) For a permanent easement acquired on school trust lands under this section, the commissioner must pay no less than 100 percent of the easement's appraised value at the time of closing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>A limited-term easement may be converted to a permanent easement or renewed at the end of the easement period by mutual agreement of the commissioner and the owner, subject to any rate redetermination by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Easement runs with land.</span></h2>
          <p>If during the easement period the owner sells or otherwise disposes of the ownership or right of occupancy of the land, the new owner must continue the easement under the same terms or conditions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Modification and termination by agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may terminate an easement by mutual agreement with the owner if the commissioner determines that the termination would be in the public interest. The commissioner may agree to modifications of agreements if the commissioner determines the modification is desirable to implement the native prairie program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.96.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may adopt rules that include the procedures and payment rates to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/357/#laws.0.19.0">1987 c 357 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/254/#laws.2.8.0">1991 c 254 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/90/#laws.4.1.0">2024 c 90 art 4 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.961">
        <h1 class="shn">84.961 PRAIRIE LAND MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.961.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Native prairie values.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must recognize the value of native prairie land by taking into consideration the wildlife, scientific, erosion control, educational, and recreational benefits of native prairie.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.961.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Planning.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must plan for management, development, and restoration of:</p>
          <p>(1) prairie land under the commissioner's jurisdiction; and</p>
          <p>(2) prairie landscape reserves, comprised of an integrated network of protected prairie lands, prairie restoration sites, and private prairie lands.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.961.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prairie landscape reserves.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must develop and manage permanent prairie landscape reserves to maintain the native plant and animal populations, landscape features, and habitat types that are characteristic of intact native prairie ecosystems. Management practices may include haying and grazing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.961.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.40.0">2008 c 357 s 40</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.961.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.98.0">1987 c 404 s 98</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.963">
        <h1 class="shn">84.963 PRAIRIE PLANT SEED PRODUCTION AREAS; PRAIRIE ESTABLISHMENT GUIDANCE.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of natural resources shall study the feasibility of establishing private or public prairie plant seed production areas within prairie land locations. If prairie plant seed production is feasible, the commissioner may aid the establishment of production areas. The commissioner may enter cost-share or sharecrop agreements with landowners having easements for conservation purposes of ten or more years on their land to commercially produce prairie plant seed of Minnesota origin. The commissioner may only aid prairie plant seed production areas on agricultural land used to produce crops before December 23, 1985, and cropped three out of five years between 1981 and 1985.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall compile, prepare, and electronically disseminate to the public prairie establishment guidance materials and resources. The resources must provide information and guidance on project planning, seed selection including ecotype and species mix, site preparation, seeding, maintenance, and technical service providers. The commissioner shall use actual prairie restoration projects under development on state-owned land to illustrate and demonstrate the practices described.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.963.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.99.0">1987 c 404 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/57/#laws.1.31.0">2007 c 57 art 1 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.964"><b>84.964</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/286/#laws.2.4.0">2014 c 286 art 2 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.965"><b>84.965</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/248/#laws.0.19.0">2014 c 248 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.966"><b>84.966</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.967"><b>84.967</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.968"><b>84.968</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.969"><b>84.969</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.9691"><b>84.9691</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.9692"><b>84.9692</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/385/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 385 art 2 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.9695"><b>84.9695</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/17.457">17.457</a>]
</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STATE LANDS CONSERVATION MANAGEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.97">
        <h1 class="shn">84.97 CONTROLLED BURNING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.97.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may establish a controlled burning program on public and private land to propagate wildlife requiring new vegetative growth and brush habitats, to manage the prairie, and to reduce the wildfire hazard.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.97.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Burning permits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person may not conduct a controlled burn without a permit.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may provide a manual that describes financial and technical assistance available and provides detailed information on conducting a controlled burn.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.97.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Assistance for private burns.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may provide financial and technical assistance to persons who desire to conduct controlled burns approved by the commissioner. Technical assistance includes controlled burn plan development, demonstration controlled burns, and personnel assistance for a controlled burn.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.97.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/714/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 714 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.972">
        <h1 class="shn">84.972 PRAIRIE AND GRASSLANDS PUBLIC GRAZING PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall establish a prairie and grasslands public grazing program. The commissioner shall enter into cooperative farming agreements or lease agreements with livestock owners to annually graze prairie and grasslands administered by the commissioner where grazing will enhance wildlife habitat. The commissioner shall maintain a list of lands grazed under the program describing the location, acreage, and years grazed. The program shall have a goal of being financially self-sufficient. Unless otherwise provided by law, revenues received under this section shall be deposited in the game and fish fund and are appropriated to the commissioner for purposes of the program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.972.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/272/#laws.0.6.0">2012 c 272 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.973">
        <h1 class="shn">84.973 POLLINATOR HABITAT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall develop best management practices and habitat restoration guidelines for pollinator habitat enhancement. Best management practices and guidelines developed under this section must be used for all habitat enhancement or restoration of lands under the commissioner's control.</p>
        <p>(b) Prairie restorations conducted on state lands or with state funds must include an appropriate diversity of native species selected to provide habitat for pollinators throughout the growing season.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.973.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/114/#laws.4.12.0">2013 c 114 art 4 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9735">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9735 INSECTICIDES ON STATE LANDS.</h1>
        <p>A person may not use a pesticide containing an insecticide in a wildlife management area, state park, state forest, aquatic management area, or scientific and natural area if the insecticide is from the neonicotinoid class of insecticides or contains chlorpyrifos.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9735.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.15.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9736">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9736 STATE COOPERATIVE FARMING AGREEMENT AND AGRICULTURAL LEASE REQUIREMENTS; FOOD PLOTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of natural resources must require state cooperative farming agreements and agricultural leases of lands administered by the commissioner located east of Interstate Highway 35 in the karst region of the state to:</p>
        <p>(1) prohibit application of fertilizer in the fall;</p>
        <p>(2) require that no more than 50 percent of the nitrogen budget may be applied before crop emergence;</p>
        <p>(3) prohibit nitrogen application rates from exceeding the University of Minnesota recommendations on rates; and</p>
        <p>(4) require the use of fall cover crops.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must evaluate existing food plots and establish a process to retire food plots on lands administered by the commissioner that do not have a significant value to resident and migrating wildlife.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9736.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.3.13.0">2024 c 116 art 3 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.974">
        <h1 class="shn">84.974 MILKWEED.</h1>
        <p>When feasible, the commissioner of natural resources is encouraged to plant milkweed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.974.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/2/#laws.1.3.0">1Sp2015 c 2 art 1 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SHORELAND MANAGEMENT GRANTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.975">
        <h1 class="shn">84.975 SHORELAND MANAGEMENT GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.975.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources may make grants to local governments:</p>
          <p>(1) to administer, monitor, and enforce state approved shoreland management ordinances;</p>
          <p>(2) to adopt shoreland management ordinances consistent with statewide standards;</p>
          <p>(3) to develop comprehensive lake by lake or river shoreland management strategies that provide a unique plan to guide activities on and adjacent to a lake or river; and</p>
          <p>(4) to implement elements of a comprehensive lake or river management strategy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.975.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action on grant applications.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of a request for a shoreland management grant, the commissioner of natural resources must confer with the local government requesting the grant and may make a grant based on the following considerations:</p>
          <p>(1) the number and classification of lakes and rivers in the jurisdiction of the local government;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent of current shoreland development;</p>
          <p>(3) the development trends for the lakes and rivers;</p>
          <p>(4) the miles of lake and river shoreline;</p>
          <p>(5) whether the shoreland management ordinance or regulation adopted by the local government meets the minimum standards established by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(6) the degree and effectiveness of administration, enforcement, and monitoring of the existing shoreland ordinances;</p>
          <p>(7) the ability of the local government to finance the program or project; and</p>
          <p>(8) the degree to which the program considers a comprehensive approach to lake or river management including land use, recreation, water levels, surface water use, fish, wildlife, and water quality that may be secondary to the other elements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.975.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>The maximum annual shoreland management grant to local government for purposes of subdivision 1, clauses (1) and (2), may not exceed the local contribution to the shoreland management activity. Any federal program aid for shoreland management shall serve to reduce the state and local contribution to the activity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.975.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Aquatic plant grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make grants for aquatic plant restoration projects, research, and propagation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.975.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.266.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 266</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/376/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 376 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">NO CHILD LEFT INSIDE PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.976">
        <h1 class="shn">84.976 NO CHILD LEFT INSIDE; GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.976.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources must establish and administer a program to provide grants for outdoor environmental, ecological, and other natural-resource-based education and recreation programs serving youth.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.976.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may award grants under this section to public entities or private nonprofit organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.976.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Priorities.</span></h2>
          <p>In awarding grants under this section, the commissioner must give priority to programs that:</p>
          <p>(1) provide students with opportunities to directly experience and understand nature and the natural world;</p>
          <p>(2) use a research-based, effective environmental, ecological, agricultural, or other natural-resource-based educational curriculum;</p>
          <p>(3) maximize the number of participants that can be served;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) serve children with limited opportunities to participate in natural-resource-based outdoor activities;</p>
          <p>(5) use public park and other natural resource venues and personnel as a resource; and</p>
          <p>(6) commit matching funds or in-kind resources.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.976.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/4/#laws.3.23.0">1Sp2019 c 4 art 3 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.9765">
        <h1 class="shn">84.9765 OUTDOOR ENGAGEMENT GRANT ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9765.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The outdoor engagement grant account is established as an account in the natural resources fund. The purpose of the account is to provide funding from private sources to support the no child left inside grant program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.976">84.976</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9765.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Funding sources.</span></h2>
          <p>Appropriations, gifts, grants, and other contributions to the outdoor engagement grant account must be credited to the account. All interest and other earnings on money in the account must be credited to the account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.9765.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation; expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of natural resources and may be used only for grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.976">84.976</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.9765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 2 s 30</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CONSERVATION CORPS MINNESOTA</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.98"><b>84.98</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.176.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 176</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.84.99"><b>84.99</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.176.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 176</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.991">
        <h1 class="shn">84.991 CONSERVATION CORPS MINNESOTA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Minnesota Conservation Corps is moved to the Friends of the Minnesota Conservation Corps, an existing nonprofit corporation under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended, doing business as Conservation Corps Minnesota under the supervision of a board of directors.</p>
          <p>(b) The expenditure of state funds by Conservation Corps Minnesota is subject to audit by the legislative auditor and regular annual report to the legislature in general and specifically to the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over environment and natural resources policy and finance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Staff; corps members.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Staff employed by Conservation Corps Minnesota are not state employees, but, at the option of the board of directors of the nonprofit corporation and at the expense of the corporation or its staff, employees who are in the employ of the Minnesota Conservation Corps on or before June 30, 2003, may continue to participate in state retirement and deferred compensation that apply to state employees.</p>
          <p>(b) Employment as a Conservation Corps Minnesota member is noncovered employment for purposes of eligibility for unemployment benefits under chapter 268.</p>
          <p>(c) Conservation Corps Minnesota is authorized to continue to have staff and corps members participate in the state of Minnesota workers' compensation program through the Department of Natural Resources. Staff and corps members' claim and administrative costs must be allocated and set annually by the Department of Natural Resources in a manner that is consistent with how these costs are allocated across that agency's operations. The Friends of the Minnesota Conservation Corps shall establish and follow loss-control strategies that are consistent with loss-control activities of the Department of Natural Resources. In the event that the Friends of the Minnesota Conservation Corps becomes insolvent or cannot otherwise fund its claim and administrative costs, liability for these costs shall be assumed by the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
          <p>(d) Conservation Corps Minnesota is a training and service program and exempt from Minnesota prevailing wage guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">State and other agency collaboration.</span></h2>
          <p>The Departments of Natural Resources, Agriculture, Public Safety, Transportation, and other appropriate state agencies must constructively collaborate with Conservation Corps Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Equipment and service purchases; state contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>Conservation Corps Minnesota may purchase or lease equipment and services, including fleet, through state contracts administered by the commissioner of administration or the Department of Natural Resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Employee displacement.</span></h2>
          <p>Each employing state or local agency must certify that the assignment of Conservation Corps Minnesota members will not result in the displacement of currently employed workers or workers on seasonal layoff, including partial displacement such as reduction in hours of nonovertime work, wages, or other employment benefits. Supervising agencies that participate in the program may not terminate, lay off, reduce the seasonal hours, or reduce the working hours of any employee for the purpose of using a corps member with available funds. The positions and job duties of corps members employed in projects shall be submitted to affected exclusive representatives prior to actual assignment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.991.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Joint powers.</span></h2>
          <p>Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> relating to joint exercise of powers applies to Conservation Corps Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.35.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 35; 2010 c 361 art 4 s 82</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.84.992">
        <h1 class="shn">84.992 MINNESOTA NATURALIST CORPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Naturalist Corps is established under the direct control and supervision of the commissioner of natural resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of natural resources shall develop and implement a program for the Minnesota Naturalist Corps that supports state parks and trails in providing interpretation of the natural and cultural features of state parks and trails in order to enhance visitors' awareness, understanding, and appreciation of those features and encourages the wise and sustainable use of the environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Training and mentoring.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must develop and implement a training program that adequately prepares Minnesota Naturalist Corps members for the tasks assigned. Each corps member is assigned an interpretive naturalist as a mentor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Uniform pin.</span></h2>
          <p>Uniforms worn by members of the Minnesota Naturalist Corps must have a pin that includes the name of the Minnesota Naturalist Corps and information that the program is funded by the clean water, land, and legacy amendment to the Minnesota Constitution adopted by the voters in November 2008.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A person is eligible to enroll in the Minnesota Naturalist Corps if the person is a participant in an approved college internship program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Corps member status.</span></h2>
          <p>Minnesota Naturalist Corps members are not eligible for unemployment benefits and are not eligible for other benefits except workers' compensation. The corps members are not employees of the state within the meaning of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.02">43A.02</a>, subdivision 21.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.84.992.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Employee displacement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must certify that the assignment of Minnesota Naturalist Corps members will not result in the displacement of currently employed workers or workers on seasonal layoff or layoff from a substantially equivalent position, including partial displacement such as reduction in hours of nonovertime work, wages, or other employment benefits. The department may not terminate, lay off, reduce the seasonal hours of, or reduce the working hours of any employee for the purpose of using a corps member with available funds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.84.992.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/172/#laws.3.8.0">2009 c 172 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/93/#laws.2.17.0">2017 c 93 art 2 s 17</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.16.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 16</a>,17</p>
      </div>
    </div>
